
### Paper [1]
Title: Optical Signature of Bias-Induced Carrier Injection in a WSe2/MoS2 Heterostructure Diode
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00147
Authors: Yasuyuki Hirata, Hibiki Kinpara, Shinya Ohno, Yoshihiro Miyauchi
Abstract: We investigate the bias-dependent photoluminescence (PL) response of a WSe$_2$/MoS$_2$ heterostructure diode. Under forward bias, trion-related emission in WSe$_2$ is markedly enhanced, whereas reverse bias produces minimal spectral changes. This asymmetric response cannot be explained by electric-field effects alone and instead reflects carrier injection at the functional interface. These results demonstrate that PL provides a direct optical probe of carrier injection in an operating van der Waals heterostructure. More broadly, this approach establishes spatially resolved PL spectroscopy as a versatile tool for pinpointing the interfaces governing device operation in van der Waals heterostructures.

### Paper [2]
Title: Weak-coupling selection and finite-torus entropy in the triangular Ising antiferromagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00154
Authors: Weiqi Jiang, Pengcheng Hou
Abstract: An antiferromagnetic second-neighbor interaction favors stripe order in the triangular-lattice Ising antiferromagnet, but uniform stripes cannot satisfy odd periodic boundaries. At each fixed odd size $L\geq5$, we minimize this interaction over the ground states of the zero-field nearest-neighbor model on an $L\times L$ torus. We prove a minimum total interface length of $4L$ and classify all $L^2(L^2-1)/2$ selected states. Under the uniform measure on these states, the area fractions of the three stripe orientations approach a uniform distribution over all nonnegative triples summing to one. Thus selection reduces an exponential degeneracy to polynomial growth, while the whole-sample orientation fractions remain broadly distributed. We also derive explicit two-sided entropy-density bounds for periodic spin ground states and honeycomb dimer coverings on $M\times N$ tori, where $M,N$ are the periods along the two primitive lattice directions. For all $M,N\geq3$, the errors relative to the bulk residual entropy are bounded by explicit constants times $\min(M,N)^{-2}$, without an aspect-ratio restriction. These bounds give finite-size accuracy guarantees and control the exponentially small fraction of unperturbed ground states that survive the selection.

### Paper [3]
Title: Y2CdX4 (X = S, Se, Te) Semiconductors: A First-Principles Study of Structural, Electronic, Optical, and Energy-Related Properties
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00167
Authors: Faiq Umar, Sikander Azam
Abstract: We present a comprehensive density functional theory (DFT) investigation of the structural, mechanical, electronic, optical, thermoelectric, and bonding properties of Y2CdX4 (X = S, Se, Te) compounds for potential optoelectronic and energy-conversion applications. Structural optimization reveals a systematic expansion of the equilibrium lattice parameters from S to Te. The calculated bulk moduli decrease from 83 GPa for Y2CdS4 to 68 GPa for Y2CdSe4 and 54 GPa for Y2CdTe4, indicating progressive lattice softening. Electronic band-structure calculations predict indirect band gaps of 2.42, 2.08, and 1.71 eV for Y2CdS4, Y2CdSe4, and Y2CdTe4, respectively, with further narrowing upon inclusion of spin-orbit coupling. The calculated dielectric functions, refractive indices, absorption coefficients, reflectivity, optical conductivities, and electron energy-loss spectra reveal systematic changes in optical response across the chalcogen series. The absorption onset shifts toward lower photon energies, from approximately 2.4 eV in Y2CdS4 to 1.7 eV in Y2CdTe4. Thermoelectric calculations indicate promising Seebeck coefficients and power factors, with Y2CdSe4 exhibiting a favorable balance of transport properties. Charge-density distributions, electron localization functions, and Bader charge analyses suggest increasing covalent bonding contributions from sulfide to telluride. These findings highlight the potential of Y2CdX4 compounds for tunable optoelectronic and energy-conversion applications.

### Paper [4]
Title: On-the-fly $\Delta$-machine learned force fields for warm dense matter using a Yukawa reference model: Application to magnesium
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00170
Authors: Sayan Bhowmik, John E. Pask, Phanish Suryanarayana
Abstract: We develop a framework for on-the-fly $\Delta$-machine learned force field ($\Delta$-MLFF) molecular dynamics (MD) simulations of warm dense matter (WDM). In particular, we employ a Yukawa reference model that captures the dominant screened-Coulomb interactions between the ions, with its parameters fit on the fly to Kohn--Sham density functional theory (DFT) data, and an MLFF scheme based on the kernel method and Bayesian linear regression that learns the differences in the energies, atomic forces, and stresses between the Yukawa model and Kohn--Sham DFT. We verify the accuracy and performance of the formalism by comparing the predicted properties of warm dense carbon with Kohn--Sham DFT results in the literature. In so doing, we demonstrate that $\Delta$-MLFF has significantly lower errors in the energies, atomic forces, and stresses than the standard MLFF scheme at a comparable cost, while accelerating ab initio MD simulations by up to three orders of magnitude. We apply this framework to calculate the equation of state, structure, and transport properties of magnesium at densities of 5.22 to 10.44 g/cm$^3$ and temperatures of 600 kK to 1.5 MK. We find that the pressure, self-diffusion coefficient, and shear viscosity obey power laws in temperature and density, that the dynamic structure factor exhibits a well-defined ion-acoustic mode whose negative dispersion is characteristic of strongly coupled liquids, and that the Stokes--Einstein relation holds to within 10% over the conditions considered.

### Paper [5]
Title: Fabry-P\'erot Cavity formed by Magnonic waveguide using holes and notches
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00178
Authors: Rajesh Kumar, Yat-Yin Au, Snorri Ingvarsson
Abstract: In this paper, we have investigated a spin-wave-based Fabry-P\'erot (FP) cavity using two air holes and notches embedded in a magnonic waveguide. Through micromagnetic simulations, we have demonstrated the spin-wave profile, as well as the transmission and reflection spectra of the FP type magnonic waveguide, by varying the distance between the center of holes and notches. We have introduced an analysis of the reflection spectra in an FP type magnonic waveguide, which is essential and considerable for the performance of magnonic integrated circuits. We have also presented a theoretical model to describe the transmission and reflection characteristics of the FP-type magnonic waveguide. Furthermore, active tuning of the resonance peak of the FP type magnonic wavguide by varying the external magnetic field has been demonstrated. Our proposed structure and its study may contribute to the development of magnonic integrated circuits for low-power computing and data processing in next-generation spintronic technologies.

### Paper [6]
Title: The Halperin (111) state as a paired composite-fermion wave function: Two exact finite-size identities on the sphere
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00204
Authors: Glenn Wagner
Abstract: Two wavefunctions have previously been proposed to describe the $\nu=1/2+1/2$ quantum Hall bilayer: $p$-wave pairing of composite fermions and $s$-wave pairing of composite fermions with anti-composite fermions. We show analytically in the spherical geometry and within the Jain-Kamilla lowest Landau level projection scheme that both of these paired composite fermion wavefunctions exactly reproduce the Halperin (111) state for a specific form of the pairing that we derive. We also find a second wavefunction that is common between both classes of wavefunctions. However, we provide evidence the two variational families generate overlapping but not identical sets of electronic states, ruling out a universal reparametrization that identifies their wavefunctions.

### Paper [7]
Title: Intermolecular and Interfacial Origins of Layering in a Near-Isotropic Organic Glass Former
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00223
Authors: Charles H. Carroll, Juliana I. Bonilla-Lugo, Arthur Y. Lin, Richard Stephens, Peng Luo, Sarah E. Wolf, Cindy Yueli Chen, Dong Hyup Kim, Yu Wu, Patrick J. Walsh, Patryk W\k{a}sik, Whitney S. Loo, Zahra Fahkraai, Rose K. Cersonsky
Abstract: Physical vapor deposition can produce organic glasses with enhanced stability and pronounced structural anisotropy (layering) even when the constituent molecules are nearly spherical. However, the microscopic origin of the layering in spherical molecular glass formers remains unclear. We investigated vapor-deposited films of 9-(3,5-di(naphthalen-1-yl)phenyl)anthracene ({\alpha},{\alpha}-A) by grazing-incidence wide-angle X-ray scattering, atomistic molecular dynamics, and coarse-grained simulations. Experimentally, the layering signal varies nonmonotonically with deposition temperature and is strongest near 0.8T_g. Simulated scattering calculations reproduce the principal experimental features and show that the layering peak does not originate from shifts in single molecule degrees of freedom. In fact, the simulated deposited and bulk glasses show largely the same single-molecule conformations. Interestingly, a one-site coarse-grained liquid with isotropic interactions develops a comparable interfacial layering signature. These results indicate that layering in {\alpha},{\alpha}-A arises primarily from collective density correlations generated at the free surface during deposition rather than from conformational bias or orientational ordering. The findings connect structural anisotropy in vapor-deposited molecular glasses to the generic interfacial organization of simple liquids.

### Paper [8]
Title: In the age of big data and AI: do we still need chemical bonds?
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00228
Authors: Mohammed Al-Fahdi, Aakash Naik, Katharina Ueltzen, Luc Walterbos, Tom Demeyere, Philipp Benner, Janine George
Abstract: Chemical bonding is the language through which chemistry rationalizes materials structure, stability, properties, and reactivity. Meanwhile, modern deep-learning models achieve remarkable predictive performance using representations based largely on compositional and structural information, raising the question of whether explicit bonding concepts remain necessary. In this work, we argue that artificial intelligence does not render chemical bonding obsolete but provides a new framework for evaluating and refining bonding concepts. We show that, through linear probing, Allegro and MACE embeddings recover bonding descriptors with (R^2 > 0.95), indicating that bonding emerges naturally from Allegro's and MACE's latent spaces. We also show that bonding descriptors provide information beyond bond distance in our electronic-structure-featurized MEGNet (ESF-MEGNet) model, reducing prediction error for properties including bulk and shear moduli by 15%. We discuss implications for interpretable machine learning, synthesizability prediction, and generative materials design, and suggest joint-embedding approaches for learning more general bonding representations. More broadly, we propose that chemical bonding can be understood as a learnable representation of electronic structure. From this perspective, different bonding models represent alternative compressions of the underlying electronic information and can be evaluated through their predictive power, dimensionality, and complexity. The age of machine learning shifts the focus from whether a bonding model is physically motivated to whether it captures the information required to explain and predict materials behavior. If a materials genome exists, it should be encodable through a representation of chemical bonding. If no bonding representation can capture this information, our understanding of the chemical bond may require revision.

### Paper [9]
Title: Ab initio exploration of hydrogen-intercalated transition metal dichalcogenides: From identifying stable structures to emergent superconductivity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00271
Authors: William Wenborn, Jonas Bekaert
Abstract: We present a first-principles study of hydrogen-intercalated transition metal dichalcogenides (TMDs), examining the structural, thermodynamic, electronic and superconducting properties of distinct intercalation configurations. In most configurations, the two intercalated hydrogen atoms contribute two electrons per unit cell, creating a band gap and thus rendering the system semiconducting. Among the dynamically stable configurations there is one exception to this rule, with inherently metallic behavior. The pristine phases of these materials are known to exhibit a charge density wave instability, which is fully suppressed by specific hydrogen configurations. Moreover, the semiconducting structures can be metallized by hole doping, albeit that the superconducting critical temperatures ($T_c$) are vanishingly low. On the other hand, electron doping drives the $T_c$ up to 25 K, by populating dense states above the gap. This finding can motivate further experimental exploration of superconductivity in hydrogen-intercalated TMDs through ionic-liquid gating or alkali co-intercalation.

### Paper [10]
Title: Impact of Surface Preparation on the Propulsion of Catalytic Janus Colloids and Implications for Reproducibility
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00274
Authors: Muhammad Haroon (Case Western Reserve University), Mitchell Lau (Case Western Reserve University), Christopher L. Wirth (Case Western Reserve University)
Abstract: Platinum-coated polystyrene (PS) Janus particles (JPs) propelled by the catalytic decomposition of hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) are a widely used model system for active matter, yet reported speeds for similar particles and fuel concentrations differ by more than an order of magnitude. Broad adoption of these materials requires increasing consistency in the preparation and achieving improved repeatability in their behavior. Herein, we focused on two routine, but rarely reported, preparation steps that we found to alter the particle speed. First, we found that pre-exposing the platinum cap to H2O2 before cell assembly increased the mean speed by a factor of 2.4. The size of this effect depended on batch history, where an aged batch responded strongly, while a freshly prepared batch sped up by only 16%. Second, we found that plasma cleaning of the glass substrate decreased the speed 2.7 times. In a 120 $\mu$m cell the suppression was the same at both walls even when only one wall was treated. The suppression grew with the treated glass area per unit fluid volume, which pointed to species released into the bulk solution rather than to a change at the wall. Plasma-cleaned glass released more ions into water than untreated glass, and XPS detected an ionic metal fluoride on the plasma-cleaned surface. A model in which the released ions lower the speed through the solution conductivity reproduced the measured speeds in all four cell geometries. Of the 17 published studies compared here, pre-activation is reported in only four studies and substrate cleaning in only six studies. We propose a minimum reporting standard for the preparation and associated speed measurements of catalytic Janus particles.

### Paper [11]
Title: The Agentic AI Kerr Lab: LLM Agents for Kerr Microscopy Experiments with Magnetic Skyrmions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00299
Authors: Kilian Leutner, Yuean Zhou, Aditya Kumar, Thomas B. Winkler, Robert Fr\"omter, Hans Fangohr, Mathias Kl\"aui
Abstract: Magnetic skyrmions are topological spin textures that can be observed directly by magnetic microscopy and driven by fields and currents, which makes them a model system for fundamental physics and candidates for spintronic devices. Magneto-optical Kerr microscopy is a method to image them in real time; however, most experiments use an inefficient cycle of consecutive imaging, analysis and excitation in which every step requires the judgement of the experimentalist operating the setup. Here we present the Agentic AI Kerr Lab (AgentKerr), in which an agent based on a large language model operates this cycle automatically and efficiently on a Kerr microscope. Rather than relying on pattern detection by a language model, a convolutional neural network trained on Kerr images turns live images into skyrmion observables such as position, size and shape, on which the agent bases its decisions. The architecture lets agents operate the instruments safely, as every hardware action is checked deterministically against an action policy, independently of the agent that issues it. With no procedure given, the agent determines by itself how to create, tune and move skyrmions, and writes its own analyses. The approach can be transferred to other spin textures and points towards magnetic imaging experiments in which the researcher poses the question and the agent finds the way to realize the experiments that yield the answer.

### Paper [12]
Title: Quantum dynamics of ultrasmall magnetic skyrmions in atomic-defect landscapes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00304
Authors: Samir Lounis
Abstract: Magnetic skyrmions are prime candidates for future information technology, and their fate in devices is settled by the defects they encounter. In ultrathin films, skyrmions can be as small as a couple of nanometers, carrying only a few tens of magnetic moments, while atomic defects reshape their energy landscape on the atomic scale. Here, we demonstrate that such ultrasmall skyrmions can behave quantum mechanically and that their quantum dynamics is unusual. Since the gyrotropic force dominates, the two coordinates of a ferromagnetic skyrmion are canonically conjugate and the skyrmion behaves as a particle in the lowest Landau level of an effective magnetic field, with a magnetic length of 0.6~\AA. Utilizing skyrmion-defect interaction profiles of Cr and Fe adatoms and dimers in PdFe/Ir(111), obtained from atomistic spin simulations parametrized from first principles, we unveil quantized gyrotropic orbits with level spacings of 20--85~$\mu$eV, bound not only by pinning defects but also, counter-intuitively, by repulsive ones. Saddle points of the energy landscape act as quantum beam splitters for skyrmions, and quantum depinning reaches rates of 1--100~Hz below the critical current. Embedded impurities, in contrast, bind the skyrmion so strongly that it ceases to behave as a rigid particle, defining a strong-coupling regime. For the Gilbert damping typical of ultrathin films, the quantized levels remain well resolved. The thermal-to-quantum crossover lies between 40 and 160~mK, which is within reach of millikelvin scanning tunneling microscopy, where tip-induced escape statistics and electron spin resonance STM can reveal the quantized gyrotropic motion of skyrmions pinned at defects crafted atom by atom.

### Paper [13]
Title: Evidence for topological flat band magnetism in Co$_3$Sn$_2$S$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00310
Authors: Ola Kenji Forslund, Andrzej Ptok, Chun Lin, Aksel Kobialka, Wan Yu Chen, Ugne Miniotaite, Martin M{\aa}nsson, Yasmine Sassa, Timur Kim, Cephise Cacho, Jian Rui Soh, Chin Shen Ong, Vladislav Borisov, Olle Eriksson, Johan Chang
Abstract: Correlations and topology are central pillars in the study of emergent electronic phases. Topological flat bands near the Fermi level unite these concepts, providing a route towards correlated topological phenomena and unconventional magnetism. Despite theoretical predictions, momentum-resolved observation of a topological flat band at the Fermi level has remained elusive. Using angle resolved photoemission spectroscopy, we observe a flat band at the Fermi level within the topologically non-trivial electronic structure of the magnetic kagome Weyl semimetal Co$_3$Sn$_2$S$_2$. We further uncover a strong temperature dependence in which the flat band shifts across the Fermi level, accompanied by a reconstruction of the Fermi surface. Strikingly, this evolution occurs on the same temperature scale as previously reported anomalies in the magnetic response. Together, these results provide evidence for topological flat band magnetism in Co$_3$Sn$_2$S$_2$, establishing a microscopic setting in which topology, electronic correlations, and magnetism become intertwined.

### Paper [14]
Title: Multistable elastic pixels: nematic elasticity for non-volatile positioning of colloidal optical scatterers
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00318
Authors: Jed-Joan S. Edziah, Brian Edwards, Nader Engheta, Kathleen J. Stebe
Abstract: Non-volatile reconfiguration of optical devices, in which a configuration persists without continuous energy input, remains a challenge. We introduce multistable elastic pixels (MEPs), lithographically defined pores filled with a nematic liquid crystal, whose curved ends capture a scattering inclusion in one of two mechanically stable sites. Simulations and experiments show that pore geometry and homeotropic surface anchoring define the energy minima and the height of the barrier between them. This barrier far exceeds thermal energies, stabilizing a scatterer location absent an external field. Colloidal inclusions switch reversibly between sites under transient electric fields. Using arrays of static scatterers, we show that the two positional states produce distinct responses at visible wavelengths, demonstrating the potential of MEPs for reconfigurable devices and metasurfaces. Unlike phase-change and liquid-crystal-tuned approaches, which switch a material surrounding fixed scatterers, MEPs use the nematoelastic energy landscape to move the optical element itself.

### Paper [15]
Title: Quantum Dipolar Magnon Drag in Conducting Magnetic Bilayers
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00320
Authors: Ling Gan, Shufeng Zhang
Abstract: We predict electrical drag between two electrically isolated metallic ferromagnets mediated by quantum dipolar magnon coupling. A charge current in one layer generates a nonequilibrium magnon current whose momentum is transferred across an insulating spacer through interlayer dipolar interactions. The resulting magnon current in the passive layer produces a measurable voltage through electron-magnon scattering. We show the combination of resonant quantum momentum transfer and magnetic tunability through the control of magnetization configurations distinguish quantum dipolar drag from conventional drag mechanism.

### Paper [16]
Title: A Julia implementation of Floquet-Bloch theory for high-harmonic generation in periodic systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00354
Authors: Ashwin Pillai, Aurelien Manchon, Claudio Attaccalite
Abstract: We present a flexible Julia codebase implementing Floquet-Bloch theory for high-harmonic generation (HHG) in periodic tight-binding systems. Using the frequency-domain Fourier-Sambe formalism, the package maps the time-dependent Schroedinger equation onto a static eigenvalue problem. This allows direct computation of Floquet states, quasienergy bands, and harmonic currents for 1D, 2D, or 3D lattices under arbitrary light polarizations. The implementation supports both analytical Fourier-Bessel expansions (via Peierls substitution) and numerical Fast Fourier Transforms (FFT). We validate the code against a canonical 1D two-band model and showcase its capabilities on 2D honeycomb systems: gapped graphene mapping HHG cutoff scaling with hopping parameters and the Kane-Mele model, exploring field-induced quasienergy gaps and topological transitions under linear and circular driving fields. In addition to the theoretical presentation, we provide a flexible and efficient open-source Julia implementation of the Floquet formalism: https://github.com/Ashwin-A-Pillai/Floquet-Fourier-Numericals/tree/main.

### Paper [17]
Title: Kinetics of pollutant adsorption onto polymers: the Langevin dynamics study
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00358
Authors: K. Haydukivska, V. Blavatska, Ja. Ilnytskyi
Abstract: We study adsorption kinetics of low-molecular-weight adsorbate particles on a polymer via the Langevin dynamics. The impact of the polymer molecular weight and of the strength of the polymer-particle attraction are examined. We consider mixtures of $n_p$ chains of the length $N$ with adsorbate, where $n_pN=600$ holds. Analysis includes examination of visualizations and evaluation of adsorption capacity, binding energy, average gyration radius, and asphericity of the polymer-adsorbate complexes. Both adsorption capacity and total binding energy show monotonic, logistic-like behavior; the former reaches a plateau sooner than the latter does, indicating further optimization of the maximum-capacity complex. Binding energy per particle and asphericity of complexes both demonstrate non-trivial behaviors, with distinct extrema at the intermediate stages of adsorption. The analysis reveals three stages of adsorption: (i) formation of small polymer-particle complexes, (ii) gradual merge of these into a single complex, (iii) stationary state. The stage (i) completes faster for short chains, as in this case fewer conformational rearrangements are required. The stage (ii) completes faster for long chains because of the necklace-like arrangement of small complexes along a long chain, which promotes their merge vs. diffusion-driven merge of the complexes of short chains. Our results indicate that for the rapid removal of pollutants in the high interaction regime, it is more effective to use the set of short polymer chains. On the other hand, long linear chains are optimal for adsorbing pollutants in a weak interaction regime.

### Paper [18]
Title: Non-Equilibrium Statistical Mechanics of Glass-Forming Liquids under Continuous Cooling: Microscopic Inner Clocks, Fictive Temperatures, and Kauzmann Diagrams
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00382
Authors: R. Peredo-Ortiz, M. Medina-Noyola, L. F. Elizondo-Aguilera
Abstract: The description of macroscopic properties in out-of-equilibrium amorphous solids such as gels and glasses, typically processed under finite cooling rates, remains a fundamental challenge for modern statistical physics. In this work, we employ the Non-Equilibrium Self-Consistent Generalized Langevin Equation (NESCGLE) theory to construct a microscopic description of the structural and calorimetric glass transition under continuous cooling. Building upon the microscopic Onsager-Machlup interpretation of Narayanaswamy's material time (the inner clock $u(t)$) [J.Chem.Phys. 156, 244506 (2022)], we recover Tool's fictive temperature $T_f$ directly from the evolving spatial pair correlations and show that wavenumber dispersion naturally generalizes the classical scalar $T_f$ into a continuous mode spectrum governed by de Gennes narrowing at the primary cage peak. Across wide variations in cooling rate down to the quasi-static limit, the theory yields first-principles non-equilibrium Kauzmann diagrams for the two-body excess entropy that dynamically avert the Kauzmann crisis above the ideal dynamic arrest temperature $T_c$, while predicting sigmoidal relaxational heat-capacity steps consistent with computer simulations and calorimetry. Finally, we extract the cooling-rate-dependent glass transition temperature $T_g(q)$ from both equal-area integrals and differential heat-capacity landmarks, demonstrating that its asymptotic approach toward $T_c$ obeys a critical power-law scaling governed directly by the equilibrium dynamic arrest exponent.

### Paper [19]
Title: Helical Magnetic Nature of $\beta$-Mn Structured CoZn Thin-Films Determined by a Bayesian Approach to Modeling Polarized Neutron Reflectivity Data
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00390
Authors: M. Dearg, A. J. Caruana, C. J. Kinane, N. Satchell, G. Burnell, S. Langridge, C. H. Marrows
Abstract: Chiral magnetic materials can host skyrmions and hence are of interest for numerous spintronic applications including low-power storage. To reach this goal, room temperature operation and a thin film form are needed. We have studied the chiral magnetism in a $\beta$-Mn structured CoZn sputtered film, with a Curie temperature of $\approx 130$ {\deg}C, by means of polarized neutron reflectometry (PNR) in order to reveal its magnetic field-temperature phase diagram. In bulk this material has a helimagnetic ground state. Here we use PNR data to show that the helimagnetism survives in a CoZn film only 100 nm thick, roughly half a helical period. Given the subtlety of differentiating the helical state from a uniform state with reduced interfacial magnetism, we have developed and employed a Bayesian approach that uses model selection to analyze our PNR data and to map out the field-temperature phase diagram of our thin film.

### Paper [20]
Title: Colloidal gel morphology and phase behavior differs based on the electrostatic interaction model in concentrated electrolytes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00419
Authors: Hwigwang Lim, Caleb Youngwerth, Lisa Je, Matthew Gebbie, Rose K. Cersonsky
Abstract: Predicting how nanoparticles interact in concentrated electrolytes remains a central challenge in nanoscience, with implications spanning energy storage, catalysis, and soft materials design. While Derjaguin-Landau-Verwey-Overbeek (DLVO) theory provides a widely used framework for modeling colloidal interactions, its electrostatic component relies on electric double-layer (EDL) theories whose validity in ionic liquids and concentrated electrolytes is consistently debated. Experiments have revealed significant deviations from classical screening behavior, including underscreening and oscillatory charge ordering, yet the consequences of these nonclassical effects for collective nanoparticle behavior remain unresolved.
  Here, we demonstrate that the extended range and oscillatory nature of electrostatic screening in concentrated electrolytes predicts different phase behavior and morphology of colloidal ionogels, yielding distinct thermodynamic signatures, percolation thresholds, and amorphous network structures. Using molecular dynamics simulations with DLVO-type pair interactions parameterized by competing EDL models, we systematically compare the consequences of different screening theories on gel formation and configurations.

### Paper [21]
Title: Effect of Rapid Thermal Annealing on the Superconductivity of ALD-Grown TiN thin films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00424
Authors: Z. Sadirkhanov, A. Tuigynbek, M. Surendran, A. Jakyp, L. Akisheva, A. T. Hammack, A. B. Tikhonov, A. Kemelbay
Abstract: Titanium nitride (TiN) is a promising superconducting material for scalable quantum technologies owing to its tunable kinetic inductance and compatibility with CMOS thin-film processing. However, the superconducting performance of atomic-layer-deposited TiN can be degraded by oxygen incorporation, off-stoichiometry, and crystallographic disorder. In this work, we investigate rapid thermal annealing (RTA) in high-purity nitrogen as a post-deposition strategy to improve polycrystalline TiN films grown by RF-biased plasma-enhanced atomic layer deposition. Grazing-incidence X-ray diffraction reveals reduced peak broadening, a smaller lattice parameter, and a stronger (111) preferred orientation with increasing annealing temperature. X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy depth profiling shows reduced oxygen-related bonding and a shift toward a Ti--N-dominated chemical environment after annealing. These structural and chemical changes result in a 25% increase in the superconducting transition temperature and a significant narrowing of the transition width. Annealing also reduced the sheet resistance and the estimated sheet kinetic inductance, increased the residual resistance ratio, and improved the magnetic-field tolerance. Together, the structural, chemical, and transport results are consistent with reduced material disorder and improved superconducting uniformity. Because RTA is a relatively low thermal budget and is a semiconductor fabrication compatible process, it offers a practical route to improve the quality of ALD-grown TiN for superconducting applications.

### Paper [22]
Title: Validation of the five-electron pO qubit in Si using full configuration interaction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00443
Authors: David W. Kanaar, John H. Caporaletti, J. P. Kestner, Christopher R. Anderson, Mark F. Gyure
Abstract: The $pO$ qubit is a recently proposed charge qubit consisting of five electrons confined in a gate-defined Si quantum dot. Because its two qubit states have no dipole moment, it couples to electric fields only through the quadrupole moment, suppressing the dominant decoherence channel of conventional charge qubits while retaining all-electrical control. The original proposal was formulated in a shell-filling picture without Coulomb interactions. In this work we perform full configuration interaction (FCI) calculations to assess the impact of the Coulomb interaction on the viability of the $pO$ qubit. We find that the interacting eigenstates occupy orbital shells beyond the shell-filling picture, with the degree of excitation governed by the competition between valley splitting, orbital energy, and Coulomb energy scales. Additionally, we find that the ground states form degenerate doublets, but of two distinct kinds. Above a crossover near a valley splitting of $E_{\rm{VS}}\approx50~\mu$eV the doublet is a pair of orbital angular momentum excitations with $|\langle L_z\rangle|=1$ in the lower valley, while below the crossover the doublet is a pair of single valley excitations with $\langle L_z\rangle=0$. Despite this substantial departure from the shell-filling picture, for $E_{\rm{VS}} > 50~\mu$eV the essential features, vanishing dipole moments and nonzero quadrupole tensor between qubit states are preserved. We show that the vanishing dipole is in fact guaranteed by the symmetry properties of the five-electron Hamiltonian. The same energy scales that govern the orbital occupation also set the quadrupole tensor elements and the first excited state energy gap, both of which are in principle tunable through device engineering.

### Paper [23]
Title: Fast Neural Noise Stabilizes Associative Memory against Slow Synaptic Noise
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00516
Authors: Tomoki Kurikawa, Kunihiko Kaneko
Abstract: Neural systems operate under multi-timescale stochastic dynamics, yet reliably maintain stable functions. We investigate how fast neural fluctuations and slow synaptic variability asymmetrically influence the structural robustness of associative memories. While synaptic noise degrades memory formation, neural noise stabilizes the network against structural perturbations. For sequential learning of multiple memories, the average recall performance is maximized at an intermediate neural-noise amplitude, whereas increasing synaptic noise rapidly degrades memory performance. Using an adiabatic mean-field framework, we derive an analytical stationary distribution of stability factors. Our theory reveals that fast neural fluctuations push the system away from marginal stability, serving as an indispensable prerequisite for systemic stability.

### Paper [24]
Title: Physics-informed X-ray tomography of magnetization and material parameters
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00528
Authors: Alexander Setescak, Polly Mitchell, Luke J. R. Higgins, Burkhard Kaulich, Luke Turnbull, Rikako Yamamoto, Marina Raboni Ferreira, Daniel A. Mayoh, Geetha Balakrishnan, Anuj Chavan, Dieter Suess, Amalio Fern\'andez-Pacheco, Manuel Guizar-Sicairos, Claire Donnelly, Claas Abert
Abstract: Three-dimensional spin textures are stabilized by microscopic interactions within their host materials. X-ray vector tomography can image these nanoscale structures, but weak magnetic contrast and a limited range of viewing angles often leave parts of the magnetization poorly determined. Here we show that a reconstruction constrained by the micromagnetic energy recovers the three-dimensional magnetization from such data and gains sensitivity to the underlying material interactions. Applied to the chiral magnet Co$_8$Zn$_9$Mn$_3$, the method resolves the three-dimensional helical texture even from a small fraction of the dual-axis projections typically required for a successful tomographic reconstruction, or from a single tilt axis alone. By testing which ratio of exchange to Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction in the reconstruction best reproduces the measured projections, we obtain a value for the sample that differs by about 10 % from the literature value derived from the helical winding period. Taken together, our findings suggest a way to reduce the time needed for vector tomography and to extend it to determining fundamental magnetic interactions in a material.

### Paper [25]
Title: Machine Learning Aided Computational Discovery of Boride Substrates for Borophene Synthesis
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00533
Authors: Luqing Wang, Qunfei Zhou, Qiucheng Li, Venkata Surya Chaitanya Kolluru, Hui Li, Albert Tsui, Joshua Paul, Pierre Darancet, Mark C. Hersam, Maria K. Y. Chan
Abstract: Borophene, a monolayer of boron, is distinctive among two-dimensional materials owing to its unique properties, including polymorphism and potential applications in quantum technologies and energy conversion and storage. Nevertheless, its applications are hindered by the strong binding of borophene to metal substrates, which impedes the realization of freestanding borophene. Alternative substrates with tunable binding to borophene are therefore highly desirable. Since boride layers arise during borophene synthesis on metals, borides provide potential alternative substrates for borophene synthesis. In this work, we investigate the binding energies and interactions between borophene and 1356 boride surfaces for two borophene phases (v1/6 and v1/9), using density functional theory (DFT) calculations and machine learning (ML). Importantly, incorporating ML-predicted charge transfer as an input feature to a subsequent ML model enables physics-informed predictions of binding energies. Additional DFT calculations on four representative cases were performed to validate the ML predictions. SHAP analysis reveals that the majority of key descriptors are associated with delocalized electrons, highlighting the role of electronic properties in governing binding strength. Combined DFT and ML screening identifies 65 and 73 promising boride surfaces for the v1/6 and v1/9 phases, respectively. Al- and Ag-based borides, including AlB2 and AgB2, emerge as predominant candidates for both phases. The computationally predicted candidate substrates and the deduced design guidelines provide a charge-transfer-informed ML strategy for substrate selection and represent promising steps toward the experimental realization of freestanding borophene.

### Paper [26]
Title: Nodeless $d$-wave superconductivity driven by charge fluctuations in monolayer FeSe on SrTiO$_3$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00534
Authors: Jin-Wei Dong, Sen Zhou, Jian Wang, Gang Su, Ziqiang Wang
Abstract: Monolayer FeSe and Fe(Te,Se) grown on SrTiO$_3$ exhibit some of the highest superconducting transition temperatures among iron-based superconductors, yet their pairing mechanism and symmetry remain elusive. Motivated by the recent surprising discovery of nodeless $d$-wave superconductivity using scanning Josephson tunneling microscopy (SJTM) [T. Wei et al., arXiv:2610.38421 (2026)], we investigate superconductivity in monolayer FeSe/SrTiO$3$ (1L-FeSe/STO) within an extended Hubbard model incorporating both onsite and intersite Coulomb interactions among Fe $3d$ and Se $4p$ orbitals. We show that Fe-Se intersite Coulomb repulsion strongly enhances small-momentum $p$-$d$ charge-transfer fluctuations, which mediate attractive intraorbital spin-singlet pairing interactions. These electronic fluctuations may be further enhanced by forward-scattering optical phonons of the SrTiO$3$ substrate. The resulting intrapocket attraction, together with residual repulsive interpocket interactions, stabilizes a mirror-odd, nodeless $B_{1g}$ ($d$-wave) superconducting state with opposite gap signs on the two electron pockets. Both Fe $t_{2g}$ and Se $4p$ orbitals contribute substantially to this multiorbital superconductivity. The calculated superconducting gap and density of states exhibit a two-peak structure, while the Josephson tunneling conductance displays a half-lattice-period modulation, in excellent agreement with experimental observations. Our results establish electronic charge-transfer fluctuations as a viable pairing mechanism for unconventional nodeless $d$-wave superconductivity in monolayer FeSe/STO.

### Paper [27]
Title: RIXS study of strain-tuned coupled valence and spin-state transition in epitaxial $(\mathrm{Pr}_{0.85}\mathrm{Y}_{0.15})_{0.7}\mathrm{Ca}_{0.3}\mathrm{CoO}_{3}$ films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00540
Authors: Yiu-Fung Chiu, Jaewon Choi, Nileena Nandakumaran, Mirian Garcia-Fernandez, Jonathan Pelliciari, Shiyu Fan, Valentina Bisogni, Chris Leighton, Ke-Jin Zhou, Stefano Agrestini
Abstract: Strain-tuned $(\mathrm{Pr}_{0.85}\mathrm{Y}_{0.15})_{0.7}\mathrm{Ca}_{0.3}\mathrm{CoO}_{3}$ thin films provide a model system for controlling coupled valence, spin-state, structural and metal-insulator transitions (VSSTs) in perovskite cobaltite materials. Here, we use Co $L_{2,3}$ and Pr $M_{4,5}$ X-ray Absorption Spectroscopy (XAS), along with Co $L_{3}$ edge Resonant Inelastic X-ray Scattering (RIXS), to probe the local electronic structure across the strain-controlled VSST in epitaxial (Pr,Y,Ca)CoO$_{3}$ thin films. In compressively strained films on YAlO$_{3}$ (YAO) substrates, cooling through the transition produces Pr$^{4+}$-like spectral weight, suppresses Co$^{4+}$-like character, and favors predominantly low-spin Co$^{3+}$. The changes at the Pr and Co edges are quantitatively consistent with a reduction of approximately 0.15 holes per Co in the Co--O network. By contrast, tensile-strained thin films on La$_{0.18}$Sr$_{0.82}$Al$_{0.59}$Ta$_{0.41}$O$_{3}$ (LSAT) substrates have nearly temperature-independent spectra. Multiplet calculations reproduce the principal XAS lineshapes and local $dd$ excitations at high energy loss. Additionally, low-energy RIXS intensity recovered above the transition is consistent with a combination of Co$^{4+}$-like local excitations and a broader particle--hole continuum associated with the metallic phase. These results provide element-resolved evidence for charge compensation between the Pr and Co--O sublattices and support cooperative coupling among charge-transfer, spin-state, and lattice structure at the strain-controlled VSST.

### Paper [28]
Title: Elucidating the topological nature of molecular beam epitaxy grown high Curie temperature (Mn1+ySb2-yTe4)x(Sb2-yMnyTe3)1-x using angle resolved photoelectron spectroscopy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00548
Authors: Candice R. Forrester, Jisoo Moon, Sina Mohammadi, Asish Kundu, Amy Lopez Navarro, Kaushini Wickramasinghe, Abhay Pasupathy, Anil K. Rajapitamahuni, Maya N. Nair, Elio Vescovo, Lia Krusin-Elbaum, Maria C. Tamargo
Abstract: Angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) is used to elucidate the band structure of (Mn1+ySb2-yTe4)x(Sb2-yMnyTe3)1-x, a new magnetic quantum material grown using molecular beam epitaxy that has a Curie temperature (TC) above 100K, much higher than those reported for other materials of the MnxSb2-xTe4 class. Conflicting and sparse theory and experiments, as well as the complex disordered structure of the materials make it difficult to establish their topological nature. Here we explore this question by performing ARPES studies for two samples with x=0.65 and x=0.92, respectively. Images of the band structures of these two samples were acquired using photon energy dependent studies. The bulk and surface states of this complex material system, and the effects of composition on the band structure were investigated. Evidence of surface state features as well as bulk related ones were obtained, supporting their non-trivial nature. No signature of Fermi-arcs consistent with Weyl semimetals were seen. A shift in the Fermi level of the two samples due to different carrier densities allowed observation of the onset of a Dirac point in the surface states. Temperature dependent ARPES suggests possible band splitting as the materials undergo magnetic transitions, helping to understand the magnetic interactions. Our results point to their likely magnetic topological insulating nature.

### Paper [29]
Title: Reversibility of Nonequilibrium Dynamics in a Unitary Fermi Gas
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00563
Authors: Xi-Zhi Wu, Xiangchuan Yan, Xin Xie, Dali Sun, Seong-Ho Shinn, Adolfo del Campo, Kaijun Jiang
Abstract: We theoretically describe and experimentally observe the reversibility of a nonequilibrium state in a unitary Fermi gas confined in a time-dependent trap. Unlike the equilibrium case, reversibility out of equilibrium imposes a stringent condition: the accumulated dynamical phase must be an integer multiple of $2\pi$, i.e., $\Delta\phi = 2\omega_{0} s(T) = 2n\pi$, where $\omega_{0}$ is the initial trapping frequency and $s(T)$ is the rescaled time after a total evolution time $T$. We demonstrate the reversibility of an initial nonequilibrium state in an isotropic unitary Fermi gas by driving the optical trap through a round trip. We find two types of reversible pathways, the space-time scaling-invariant and polynomial shortcut-to-adiabaticity (STA) protocols. The phase condition restricts the round-trip time $T$ to discrete values, a distinguishing feature of nonequilibrium dynamics. Our work establishes a clear criterion for the quantum control of nonequilibrium many-body systems beyond linear response and provides practical pathways to reversibility.

### Paper [30]
Title: Heterostructure morphology stability maps in multiphase nanoparticles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00575
Authors: Elodie Sandraz, Chris Wolverton
Abstract: In the rapidly growing field of nanocombinatorics, the advent of megalibraries of multiphase nanoparticles (NPs) has created a need for predictive computational approaches. A notable characteristic of multiphase NPs is the internal interface architecture that arises from the joining of constituent phases at boundaries. We propose a strategy to predict the ground-state interface architecture from the DFT-calculated surface and interfacial energies of all constituent phases as input and use this strategy to create stability maps to predict multiphase NP morphologies. A Potts model, a $q$-state generalization of the Ising model, in which $q-1$ spins represent discretized regions of a constituent phase, provides a flexible, discretized representation of a $(q-1)$-phase nanoparticle with arbitrary interfacial join morphology. Interactions between spins represent interactions between phases, or interfacial energies. To model the surface of a nanoparticle, an additional spin represents the surrounding environment and interaction energies with this spin are surface energies. We apply a Monte Carlo algorithm to search for the lowest-energy spin configuration. The trade-off between surface and interfacial energy minimization drives the formation of an interface morphology. We calculate a biphase nanoparticle interface morphology stability map to study the relation between the three biphase morphologies we found. Next, we use the stability map to predict NP morphologies of sample biphase systems and compare these to experimentally available data. We repeat the grid search approach for triphase nanoparticle interface morphologies and discover 11 triphase morphologies, nine more than previously enumerated. We then predict sample triphase systems and finally revisit experimentally reported triphase morphologies.

### Paper [31]
Title: Measuring structural restraint in an amorphous material using the variance of speckle intensity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00583
Authors: Charlotte F. Petersen, Peter Harrowell
Abstract: An experimentally accessible measure of structural restraint in supercooled liquids is defined based on the variance of the intensity of speckle pattern arising from elastic scattering of coherent radiation. Using simulation data, the resulting measure of structural restraint is shown to correlate with the temperature dependence of the shear modulus and the Debye-Waller factor. The paper includes consideration of the influence of the choice of the scattering wavevector and the degree of coarse graining used in intensity measurements on the measured restraint.

### Paper [32]
Title: SeriesXRD: automated reflection tracking and phase identification in high-pressure X-ray diffraction series
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00611
Authors: Rush Maples, Zackary Rehfuss, Hongyi Liu, Sheng Ran
Abstract: High-pressure synchrotron X-ray diffraction increasingly produces hundreds to thousands of detector frames in a single experiment, while separating, assigning, and tracking weak reflections across an entire series often remain manual. This bottleneck is particularly severe for micron- and nanoscale samples in diamond-anvil cells, where scattering from the anvils, gasket, pressure medium, and pressure markers can dominate the sample signal. We present SeriesXRD, an open-source workflow that analyzes diffraction as a series rather than as independent patterns. The program suppresses azimuthally localized diamond scattering, subtracts background, fits and tracks reflections between frames, matches known phases using pressure-dependent lattice parameters, subtracts confidently assigned contributions, and groups persistent unexplained reflections for follow-up as candidate sample-associated or unknown-phase features. We demonstrate the method on 1288 frames collected from an approximately 30-nm-thick layered UOTe flake in a diamond-anvil cell over 3.5-50.7 GPa. The dataset is strongly background dominated, with detectable UOTe diffraction in only approximately 4% of the frames. SeriesXRD recovered the sample-associated reflections identified in the original manual analysis while reducing the corresponding phase-assignment and residual-analysis workflow from more than two months of researcher time to an overnight automated run. Across the full series, 17,143 peaks were fitted, 14,395 passed quality criteria, and 90% of accepted peaks were assigned to known background or reference phases. SeriesXRD therefore converts large, background-dominated diffraction series into a tractable set of phase assignments and coherent unexplained reflections while retaining a traceable record of each decision.

### Paper [33]
Title: Kinetics of Self-Organization in One Component Active Matter Systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00618
Authors: Avinash Chauhan, Prasenjit Das
Abstract: We study an inherently nonequilibrium system consisting of self-propelled particles using molecular dynamics simulation in $d=2$. The system self-organizes through quorum-sensing interactions characterized by an interaction range $\lambda$ and a parameter $c_{\mathrm{th}}$. When the local concentration sensed by a particle, represented by an auxiliary variable $c_i(t)$ exceeds $c_{\mathrm{th}}$, its self-propulsion velocity abruptly drops to zero, leading to the emergence of particle rich domains. For small $\lambda$, we observe a droplet-like morphology of clusters, which crosses over to a bicontinuous structure as $\lambda$ increases. We confirm this transition by computing the eccentricity of the domains. Next, we characterize the evolving domain morphologies using equal-time correlation function $C(r,t)$ and structure factor $S(k,t)$ of the density field. For a fixed $\lambda$, the scaled $C(r,t)$'s at different times are indistinguishable from each other, indicating dynamical scaling. However, at a fixed time $t$, the scaling functions differ for different values of $\lambda$, confirming a $\lambda$-dependent morphological transition. In the large-wavevector limit ($k\rightarrow\infty$), the tail of $S(k,t)$ consistently follows the Porod's law: $S(k,t)\sim k^{-3}$. Furthermore, the average domain size $L(t)$ exhibits a power-law growth, $L(t)\sim t^{1/3}$, independent of $\lambda$.

### Paper [34]
Title: X-ray magnetic circular dichroism in altermagnets: insights from symmetry
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00622
Authors: Hana Schiff, Ishmam Nihal, John Vinson, Robin Msiska, Judit Romh\'anyi, David Lederman, Christoph Klewe, Sujoy Roy, Eric E. Fullerton
Abstract: Altermagnets are a unique subset of antiferromagnets possessing ferromagnet-like responses that are odd in the N\'eel vector, making X-ray magnetic circular dichroism (XMCD) a natural probe of ground state altermagnetic order. Because the relativistic ingredient of XMCD at the $L$-edge is supplied predominantly by core-level spin--orbit coupling (SOC), the dichroic signal can reflect the non-relativistic order of the valence electrons. Here we develop a complete symmetry framework for XMCD in altermagnets (AMs) preserving the crystalline unit cell. We tabulate the symmetry-allowed optical Hall vector, anisotropic magnetic dipole (AMD), and underlying octupolar components for all magnetic point groups, thereby identifying the experimental geometries that maximize the XMCD signal. We show that only twelve magnetic point groups, none with a rotation axis of order three or higher, are compatible with both altermagnetism and finite XMCD. By extending the Landau theory of collinear AMs, we connect these relativistic constraints to the spin--orbit-free order. We show that the AMD is forced to vanish at zero valence SOC in conventional antiferromagnets, and that every AM sublattice structure allows the Hall vector and AMD at linear or cubic order in the N\'eel vector; whether the resulting XMCD is nonzero depends on the N\'eel vector orientation at finite SOC. The Landau theory thus fixes the N\'eel-vector dependence of the XMCD for all AM candidates, and separates $d$-wave AMs, whose signal is inherited from a non-relativistic AMD, from $g$- and $i$-wave AMs, whose signal requires valence SOC or core--valence multiplet effects. We illustrate these predictions with first-principles Bethe--Salpeter calculations of $L$-edge XMCD for five representative altermagnetic materials and one conventional antiferromagnet.

### Paper [35]
Title: Zeeman Spin Photocurrents without Spin Textures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00655
Authors: Xihua Xiao, Ruizhi Dong, Ruixiang Fei
Abstract: Inversion symmetry forbids bulk second-order spin photocurrents driven solely by electric-dipole coupling. We show that interference between the electric-dipole and spin Zeeman couplings of light enables Zeeman spin-shift and spin-injection currents in centrosymmetric magnets. Our microscopic theory and systematic symmetry analysis establish the allowed responses and polarization selection rules for both spin-degenerate and spin-split antiferromagnets, with and without spin--orbit coupling (SOC). In collinear magnets without SOC, spin injection vanishes, whereas a pure spin-shift current survives independently of the carrier scattering time. This response arises from transverse electronic spin coherences generated by the optical magnetic field, while the ordered moments remain collinear and fixed. Model analyses validate the mechanism, and first-principles calculations for $\mathrm{MnF_2}$ predict experimentally accessible spin photocurrents. These findings establish a general framework for optical spin-current generation in centrosymmetric magnets, including a SOC-free pure-spin mechanism particularly relevant to altermagnets.

### Paper [36]
Title: Confinement Geometry Regulates Fluctuations, Densities, and Collective Escape in Interacting Active Matter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00659
Authors: Renu Parashar, Sandipan Dutta
Abstract: Active matter exhibits non-equilibrium signatures such as the violation of the fluctuation-dissipation theorem, giant number fluctuations, and non-Gaussian velocity distributions. We investigate how confinement geometry and particle number influence the steady-state behavior of sterically interacting Active Ornstein-Uhlenbeck Particles (AOUPs) in a double-well potential. Unlike bulk active systems, increasing particle number suppresses non-equilibrium fluctuations through crowding, making the steady-state density profiles increasingly resemble those of passive systems. Nevertheless, instantaneous active density profiles exhibit both unimodal and bimodal distributions, even at large particle numbers, whereas passive profiles remain bimodal. The local velocity and position distributions are unimodal in the concave regions of the potential but become bimodal in non-concave regions. Confinement suppresses long-range correlations while producing spatially localized fluctuations governed by potential geometry. Velocity fluctuations are suppressed in the densely populated wells but enhanced near the barrier, where non-equilibrium effects are strongest. At large persistence times, correlated barrier crossings and strong velocity-alignment correlations emerge, which are absent in passive systems. These results demonstrate how confinement geometry, crowding, and persistence jointly control density fluctuations, velocity correlations, and collective escape in interacting active matter.

### Paper [37]
Title: Curvature-Driven Density and Fluctuations in Interacting AOUPs under Confinement
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00664
Authors: Renu Parashar, Sandipan Dutta
Abstract: We investigate interacting active Ornstein-Uhlenbeck particles (AOUPs) confined in harmonic and double-well potentials, combining mean-field theory, analytical approximations, and numerical simulations. We derive the stationary density, position and velocity correlations, correlation length, dynamical structure factor, and leading-order entropy production. Unlike established harmonic normal-mode descriptions, our framework incorporates steric interactions, persistent forcing, and spatially varying confinement curvature through the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of the many-body Hessian. Within a locally homogeneous approximation, the correlation length is controlled by persistence and local curvature, increasing as the effective restoring stiffness decreases and formally diverging at the instability of the linearized description. Entropy production, at leading nontrivial order in persistence, is governed by gradients of the effective curvature. Harmonic confinement provides a constant-curvature reference, whereas double-well confinement creates equilibrium-like regions near the minima and strongly nonequilibrium regions near the barrier. The barrier exhibits enhanced velocity and density fluctuations, soft collective modes, long-lived correlations, and increased entropy production. Persistence amplifies these effects, while steric crowding suppresses collective fluctuations. The dynamical structure factor reveals enhanced low-frequency contributions from soft modes. Our results establish how confinement geometry, active persistence, and interactions jointly regulate collective fluctuations and irreversibility in active matter.

### Paper [38]
Title: Quantized valley source in bilayer graphene via adiabatic quantum pumping
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00733
Authors: Kai-Tong Wang, Lei Zhang, Bin Wang, Yunjin Yu, Fuming Xu
Abstract: We propose a quantized valley source based on adiabatic quantum pumping in bilayer graphene in the presence of both quantum spin Hall and quantum valley Hall effects. By periodically modulating the gate voltages of two quantum dots coupled to a bilayer graphene nanoribbon, the helical edge states enable the generation of a pure valley current. Depending on the geometry of quantum dots, the number of emitted valley pairs either forms quantized plateaus or varies continuously with the pumping amplitude. The quantized plateau structure can be tuned by the static gate voltage and by the coupling between the quantum dots and the ribbon. Quantized valley pairs are robust against Anderson disorder applied in the quantum dots and persist for both zigzag and armchair quantum-dot boundaries. These results demonstrate that adiabatic quantum pumping provides a route toward electrically controllable quantized valley sources.

### Paper [39]
Title: Beyond Synthesis Conditions: Linking In-situ Growth Dynamics to Carbon Nanotube Forest Properties through Multimodal Machine Learning
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00739
Authors: Shun Muroga, Takashi Tsuji, Shunsuke Sakurai, Don N Futaba
Abstract: Machine learning for chemical vapor deposition (CVD) of carbon nanotubes (CNTs) has largely mapped static synthesis conditions to final properties, implicitly treating these conditions as a complete description of growth. Here, we test this assumption by integrating gas-flow conditions with in-situ time-resolve growth profiles (growth curves) of vertically aligned CNT forests. We introduce an observation-window analysis, in which prediction is evaluated as a function of observation time by providing models with the growth curve only up to progressively later times, to determine not only whether a property can be predicted, but when the necessary information becomes available during synthesis. Clustering of normalized growth curves revealed distinct growth patterns independent of final forest height. The gas conditions alone could predict the initial growth rate but not the growth lifetime and failed to capture the variation in yield (R^2<0). Adding the growth curve improved yield prediction (R^2~0.4-0.5), with the relevant information emerging during the intermediate and later stages of growth rather than at its onset. In contrast, density and G/D ratio, which forest height does not record, showed no consistent improvement. The maximum height and lifetime could be estimated with moderate accuracy midway through the 10-min growth. Permutation feature importance identified water vapor and H_2, both linked to catalyst activity, as the most informative gas variables. These results indicate that variation unexplained by synthesis conditions is not merely noise but reflects an evolving catalyst state, and they establish mid-growth decision points for autonomous synthesis, enabling early termination of unpromising batches and, ultimately, in-batch adjustment of growth conditions.

### Paper [40]
Title: Informative Fisher Modes in Neural Networks as a Result of Explicit Symmetry Breaking
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00745
Authors: Ibrahim Talha Ersoy, Bj\"orn Ladewig, Karoline Wiesner
Abstract: Learning in a neural network takes place on a high dimensional, non-convex loss landscape, the Hessian curvature of which dictates the learning dynamics. Despite its high dimensionality, the large number of architectural symmetries explains some of the geometric of this landscape, in particular flat directions, i.e. near-zero modes of the Hessian. These architectural symmetries have therefore been suggested as an organizing principle of deep learning. It remains an open question, though, wether there are additional organizing principles in the remaining degrees of freedom in the learning dynamic. Here we identify task-dependent contingent symmetries as such an additional organizing principle. We show that non-flat learning directions emerge due to explicit breaking of these contingent symmetries. We isolate this symmetry in an analytically solvable minimal model. Furthermore, we show that, by tuning an L2 penalty, signatures of the symmetry breaking become measurable. As a consequence of the same mechanism, adding noise to the data drives the model through a sequence of broken symmetry recoveries in the form of Baik-Ben-Arous-P\'ech\'e (BBP) transitions. We demonstrate analytically for linear and numerically for deep linear networks that the contingent symmetry group remains explicit, and that the symmetry-breaking mechanism identifies which Hessian modes carry task-specific information. These signatures are measurable without a symmetry-group description and, hence, offer a route to study symmetry breaking as a more general principle in nonlinear networks and other learning systems.

### Paper [41]
Title: IRIS: an agentic eye on symmetry-derived paths and energy landscapes of structural phase transitions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00761
Authors: Sukriti Manna, Sraddha Agrawal, Ramamoorthy Ramesh, Panchapakesan Ganesh, Pierre Darancet, Subramanian K. R. S. Sankaranarayanan
Abstract: Structural phase transitions underpin ferroelectric and multiferroic functionality, but constructing interpretable energy landscapes remains dependent on specialist expertise. Predicting this behaviour rests on Landau free energies expanded in symmetry-adapted order parameters: their coefficients classify a transition, expose the couplings between distortions, set switching paths and barriers, and parametrize phase-field and effective-Hamiltonian models at larger scales. Obtaining these coefficients from first principles means sampling the Born--Oppenheimer energy surface along symmetry-derived structural paths, which requires crystallographic analysis, reliable structure generation and extensive computation, especially for coupled multidimensional order parameters. Here we introduce IRIS, an agentic workflow that converts plain-language requests and crystal structures into symmetry-adapted energy landscapes and fitted Landau energy models. Deterministic gates govern acceptance of structures, calculations and fits, while language models interpret requests and select constrained recovery actions. Applications across six compounds include a six-dimensional BiFeO$_3$ landscape that separates competition between polar and tilt amplitudes from directional locking and ranks homogeneous switching pathways. Controlled replays detected all 53 injected faults; disabling gates allowed 14 silent errors. Four prospective BaTiO$_3$ runs produced fitted landscapes from 96 density-functional calculations in 34 minutes total without intervening human decisions. IRIS makes physical model construction a reusable computational capability and provides a framework for scientific automation grounded in explicit verification.

### Paper [42]
Title: Diffusion-Based Generative Screening of ABX$_3$ Perovskite Compositions for Bandgap-Targeted Materials Discovery
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00766
Authors: Sunraj Patel, Krishanu Biswas
Abstract: Crystal-generation models can explore compositions and atomic arrangements beyond a fixed set of crystal prototypes, but their usefulness for a specific materials family depends on whether the generated structures retain the defining topology and whether their predicted properties remain reliable across the sampled chemistry. Here, a pretrained diffusion model is used to generate candidate ABX$_3$ perovskites and to examine their structural, chemical and electronic-property distributions. A connectivity-based filter distinguishes three-dimensional corner-sharing perovskites from structures that share the same nominal ABX$_3$ stoichiometry. The accepted set contains substantial B-site multiplicity, including ordered double-perovskite configurations that are inaccessible to the two-prototype substitution reference used here. The generated set is enriched in chloride-, bromide- and iodide-containing compositions relative to the Materials Project, while all generated B-site elements are already represented in that database. On the downstream MP-trained ALIGNN scale, the predicted bandgap distribution is shifted towards 1.4 eV, but the target-window fraction (8.2\%) is close to the MP reference fraction (7.3\%); the separate MatterGen and ALIGNN label sources prevent this comparison from establishing target-condition enrichment. An ALIGNN model performs well on a formula-grouped random test split but does not outperform a constant baseline on a deliberately severe B-site-disjoint test. First-principles calculations on six generated structures show that relaxation can alter structural classification and that predicted and calculated gaps can differ appreciably. These results identify both the chemical-space coverage offered by crystal diffusion and the structural and predictive checks needed before its outputs can be treated as materials candidates.

### Paper [43]
Title: Charge-Order pattern in $\kappa$-BEDT-TTF salts: A model independent, selection rule approach
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00798
Authors: Alberto Girlando
Abstract: A well known vibrational selection rule is invoked to determine the charge order (CO) pattern displayed by the most interesting members of the widely studied class of $\kappa$-BEDT-TTF salts and their molecular analogues. The selection rule indicates that an easily identifiable Raman band should be present if intra-dimer charge disproportionation occurs, constituting a sort of model independent ``litmus test''. The absence of such a band in the available Raman spectra of $\kappa$-BEDT-TTF salts exhibiting static of fluctuating CO, strongly suggests that the CO pattern involves inter-dimer CO, in contrast with the universally accepted intra-dimer disprortionation. This paper therefore poses a challenge for the theoretical modeling of the concerned salts: either the lack of the Raman band sensitive to intra-dimer CO pattern is explained, or the modeling of the multivariate, unique physical properties of these $\kappa$-phase salts (spin frustration, electronic ferroelectricity etc.) has to start from the perspective of a different ground state.

### Paper [44]
Title: Three-Dimensional FDTD Simulation of Superconducting Terahertz Emitters Using an Effective Medium Approximation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00840
Authors: Ryota Kobayashi, Itsuhiro Kakeya
Abstract: Compact and tunable terahertz (THz) sources with controllable polarization are sought for high-speed links, spectroscopy, and imaging. In superconducting THz emitters based on a cuprate Bi$_2$Sr$_2$CaCu$_2$O$_{8+\delta}$, which have the potential to satisfy such properties, modeling often relies on heavy nonlinear solvers or empirical isotropic dielectrics, obscuring anisotropy, plasma response, and bias dependence. We derive an anisotropic, dispersive effective permittivity from the Lawrence-Doniach model for a layered superconductor and implement it as a user-defined material in a commercial Finite-difference Time-domain (FDTD) solver. Simulations of two device geometries reproduce linearly polarized emission from a rectangular mesa and low-axial-ratio (near-circular) emission from a truncated square mesa. This CAD-ready workflow links superconducting theory to device design, enabling rapid searches and parameter optimization for next-generation THz sources.

### Paper [45]
Title: High-order concentric hopfions in a helimagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00858
Authors: Konstantin L. Metlov, Maksim M. Gordei
Abstract: Magnetic hopfions are three-dimensional topological solitons: ``knots'' in the vector field of magnetization of magnetically ordered materials, which are a key element of the three-dimensional spintronics emerging today. In this work, a simple analytical model for a new type of magnetic hopfions --- ``concentric'' ones --- is constructed and solved. Their size and equilibrium energy are estimated, their stability region on the magnetic phase diagram of a helimagnet is mapped out, and the requirements for materials admitting equilibrium concentric hopfions with high Hopf index on a uniformly magnetized background are determined.

### Paper [46]
Title: Radiofrequency plasma spheroidization of lightweight complex concentrated superalloy Al10Nb20Ta15Ti30V5Zr20
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00895
Authors: Franti\v{s}ek Luk\'a\v{c}, Jakub Kle\v{c}ka, Ji\v{r}\'i Kozl\'ik, Krist\'ina Bartha, Jozef Vesel\'y, Kate\v{r}ina Fickov\'a, Jakub Mina\v{r}\'ik, Jan \v{C}\'i\v{z}ek, Josef Str\'ask\'y
Abstract: Recent advances in additive manufacturing (AM) have significantly increased the demand for high-quality metal powders with tailored composition. For refractory complex concentrated alloys (RCCA) preparing such powders by conventional metallurgical method remains challenging due to their high melting point and chemical reactivity. In this paper, we present a method to produce spherical RCCA powders (here Al10Nb20Ta15Ti30V5Zr20) via mechanical alloying followed by radio-frequency inductively coupled plasma spheroidization. The resulting powder is suitable for AM processes because of its spherical morphology and a microstructure consisting of major BCC phases with local B2 phase. Complex carbide formation was studied by 3D atom probe tomography.

### Paper [47]
Title: Efficient Interpolation of the Bethe-Salpeter Hamiltonian at Arbitrary Momenta
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00928
Authors: Stefano Mocatti, Xiangzhou Zhu, Giulio Volpato, Pierluigi Cudazzo, Matteo Calandra, Giovanni Marini
Abstract: We develop an efficient reciprocal-space method to reconstruct the static Bethe-Salpeter (BSE) Hamiltonian at arbitrary crystal momenta from coarse first-principles grids. Bloch states are interpolated within an optimally smooth Wannier subspace, while the screened Coulomb interaction is reconstructed from the dielectric response. This decouples the electron-hole transition momenta and transferred momentum from the underlying ab initio sampling. We benchmark the method on monolayer hBN, monolayer MoS$_2$, and bulk GaAs against explicit ab initio calculations, finding excellent agreement for dense-grid Bloch states, dielectric screening, and BSE optical spectra. Furthermore, our cost analysis estimates speedups of up to $22\times$ on the grids considered here. Arbitrary transferred-momentum sampling gives direct access to the long-wavelength exciton dispersion: in hBN and MoS$_2$, we resolve the nonanalytic behaviour induced by long-range electron-hole exchange at momenta well below the spacing of practical uniform grids. For bulk GaAs, locally refined transition-momentum sampling yields a converged binding energy for the threefold-degenerate lowest exciton, in close agreement with experiment and reference calculations, using far fewer BSE points than conventional uniform meshes. As the present framework provides exciton energies and amplitudes at arbitrary crystal momenta, it opens the way to incorporating ab initio excitonic effects into nonlocal electron-phonon vertices and to extensions towards exciton-phonon interactions and nonequilibrium exciton dynamics.

### Paper [48]
Title: Spontaneous Chirality and Collective Chern Bands in a Dipole-Conserving Bose System
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00954
Authors: Yan-Guang Yue, Jie Lou, Yan Chen
Abstract: We demonstrate spontaneous chiral dipole order and an isolated collective Chern band in a time-reversal-symmetric microscopic bosonic lattice model with exact dipole-moment conservation. In a controlled large-occupation limit at zero temperature, frustrated dipole exchange and three-particle conversion stabilize two time-reversed chiral condensates. The two dipole Goldstone modes remain gapless, while a separate finite-frequency band carries Chern numbers $-1$ and $+1$ in the opposite domains. We trace this topology to complex mode couplings in the chiral background that gap finite-frequency Dirac crossings. Site-resolved density drives preserve dipole-moment conservation and reconstruct the band Chern number from band-resolved absorption spectra in the quadratic theory.

### Paper [49]
Title: Melting noncoplanar order on the square-kagome lattice: Chiral, flat-band, and staggered spin liquids from projective symmetry
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00987
Authors: Harsh Raj, Atanu Maity, Yasir Iqbal
Abstract: Noncoplanar magnetic orders carry a scalar spin chirality, and their melting by quantum fluctuations has been proposed as a route to chiral spin liquids. We ask what this route can produce on the square-kagome lattice, where couplings across the octagons stabilize noncoplanar cuboc and conical orders. We classify the fermionic spin liquids of this lattice by their projective symmetry groups, identify the symmetry class into which each noncoplanar order would melt, and compare candidate states with Gutzwiller-projected wave functions. A magnetic selection rule constrains the route: the chirality of every cuboc and conical order alternates in space, so a liquid that keeps the symmetry of its parent order has zero Chern number and no spontaneous thermal Hall response. A Chern state can descend only from uniformly chiral orders, and the regular ones we find need ferromagnetic bow-tie couplings. The energetics do not favor a chiral liquid either: among the projected wave functions we compare, a cuboc1-ordered state is lowest for strong cross-octagon couplings, and nonchiral states, a valence-bond crystal or a time-reversal-symmetric liquid, for weaker ones. The two inequivalent site orbits shape the spinons: a relative time-reversal sign between them pins flat bands to the Fermi level, and because the translations of $p4mm$ are not commutators, staggered chiral liquids exist, which are absent on sixfold-symmetric lattices.

### Paper [50]
Title: Excited-state-induced nonlinearity and polarity reversals in the thermocurrent through a quantum dot
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01006
Authors: Parvathy Gireesan, Giorgio Biasiol, Madhu Thalakulam
Abstract: Quantum dots are electrically tuneable thermal machines capable of both energy harvesting and cooling at the nanoscale in the cryogenic regime. At large thermal biases, their thermoelectric transport shows a complex behaviour, including additional extrema and polarity reversals in the thermocurrent. Though these asymmetries and nonlinearities directly affect rectification and power conversion, their origin has not been studied systematically. We address this through measurements and modelling of a single GaAs/AlGaAs quantum dot whose source is heated by white-noise excitation. Using Pauli master equation for sequential tunnelling through a two-level dot, retaining the full Fermi distributions of both leads, distinct tunnel rates for the two levels, and relaxation between them, we show that the excited state acts as a second transport channel which competes with the ground-state contribution. Strong excited-state coupling combined with slow relaxation produces polarity reversals in the thermocurrent; without both, the excited state yields only additional extrema and an amplitude asymmetry across the charge degeneracy point. Experimentally, as the source temperature increases, an additional extremum emerges in the thermocurrent which develops into a polarity reversal. Using the excited-state level spacing and ground-state tunnel rate obtained independently from pulsed-gate and bias spectroscopies, the model reproduces the positions of the observed extrema and their amplitudes to within a factor of about three, placing the device in the regime of slow relaxation and strong excited-state coupling. Our results establish thermocurrent line-shape as a probe of excited-state tunnelling and relaxation in quantum dots. Excited states, often treated as a minor correction, can dominate quantum-dot thermoelectric transport far from equilibrium.

### Paper [51]
Title: Active Jarzynski and Crooks Relations for Driven Jump-Diffusions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01009
Authors: Shuyuan Fan, Bing Miao, Jinqiao Duan, Qi Zhang
