Abstract: Non-conservative active driving breaks detailed balance in non-Gaussian jump-diffusions, obscuring the connection between external protocol work, housekeeping dissipation, and free-energy differences. Here, we establish exact finite-time fluctuation relations for driven Markov jump-diffusions---encompassing infinite-activity L\'evy processes---by comparing path probabilities under the forward and current-reversed dual dynamics relative to the instantaneous steady state (ISS). We derive a trajectory-level decomposition of total entropy production that cleanly separates transient state relaxation, housekeeping heat, and external protocol driving. When the ISS takes a Gibbsian form, gauge-tilting the dual path measure yields an Active Jarzynski equality, $\langle\exp[-(W+\Delta s_{\mathrm{act}})]\rangle_{\pi_0}=e^{-\Delta F}$, which incorporates active housekeeping entropy $\Delta s_{\mathrm{act}}$ alongside mechanical work $W$ and coexists with the conventional Jarzynski equality. Under protocol reversal, this dual framework further reveals an Active Crooks relation, where the asymmetry between forward and reverse work distributions is governed by the conditional exponential average of active dissipation. Our results provide a framework for recovering free-energy differences in driven jump-diffusions with Gibbsian instantaneous steady states and for characterizing active irreversibility through trajectory statistics.

### Paper [52]
Title: Anomalous response, re-emerging localization-transition and robust corner sates in self-similar peano fractal
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01099
Authors: Rhiddha Acharjee (Department of Physics, University of Calcutta, 92, Acharya Prafulla Chandra Road, Kolkata, West Bengal-700009, India), Sayan Bhattacharya (Department of Physics, Acharya Prafulla Chandra College, New Barrackpore, Kolkata, West Bengal-700131, India), Atanu Nandy (Department of Physics, Acharya Prafulla Chandra College, New Barrackpore, Kolkata, West Bengal-700131, India), Debnarayan Jana (Department of Physics, University of Calcutta, 92, Acharya Prafulla Chandra Road, Kolkata, West Bengal-700009, India)
Abstract: Anomalous spectral response from the non-interacting electrons on a peano fractal is recorded within tight-binding framework. It is observed that a minimal off-diagonal anisotropy can modulate the transport characteristics of the lattice. Drastic modification in the nature of the eigenstates has been validated through the workout of level spacing statistics. Further, a two-dimensional quasiperiodic Aubry-Andr\'e and Harper variation in the overlap integral can invite re-entrant localization-delocalization transition for the self-similar model. This is associated with the possible existence of single-particle mobility edge in the energy landscape. Our results are corroborated by the evaluation of inverse participation ratio, fractal dimension and quantum dynamical response. The spectrum is shown to formulate a quantum butterfly pattern with the frequency of the modulation. Chiral symmetry protected edge states are found to be insensitive with respect to moderate diagonal disorder.

### Paper [53]
Title: Comment on "Zero temperature, degenerate fermion stars" [S. Boatto, M.B. Paranjape, V. Pessanha, C.A.D. Zarro. ArXiv: 2609.20950]
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01103
Authors: Serguei N. Burmistrov
Abstract: In contrast to S. Boatto et al. \cite{Boat}, the degree of compactification or compactness is clarified for the self-gravitating spheric star of fermionic matter.

### Paper [54]
Title: Phase-Tunable Liouvillian Topology: Dual Winding Structures and Skin Effects
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01110
Authors: Daksh K. Sambhare, Koustav Roy, Saurabh Basu
Abstract: Liouvillian skin effects are usually associated with dissipation whose rates favor unidirectional flow. In this work, we show that directional edge localization can emerge even when population exchange between sites is exactly balanced, with a single tunable phase parameter $\alpha$ deciding their direction and a dual winding structure deciding their nature. Although this number-conserving bath is known for eigenstate selection, we demonstrate here that its phase controls a steady current, which Hamiltonian hopping converts to directional edge localization. Further, we study the spectral winding structure by independently twisting the left (ket) and right (bra) vector indices of the density matrix, revealing two distinct windings whose relationship is dictated by an antiunitary mirror symmetry. Nearest-neighbor jump operators break this symmetry: both windings coincide, giving rise to a common spectral winding $W_0(\alpha)=\pm1$, producing a unipolar skin effect in which the density accumulates at a specific edge. However, next nearest neighbor jump preserve it. The two windings are forced to be opposite, and the ket and bra are pushed to opposite edges, giving a bipolar skin effect of coherences present in eigenmodes, without edge localization of the density in the steady state. Remarkably, in a dimerized chain the phase-controlled density skin effect gives the steady-state zero modes a size-independent weight at the skin edge, accompanied by a kink at the topological transition. In addition, the relaxation time grows linearly with the system size, however at specific phases $\alpha$ where the jump operators are rendered Hermitian, edge-localized eigenmodes with skin character coexist with diffusive relaxation.

### Paper [55]
Title: Direct imaging of the Ni $e_g$ hole orbitals in the bilayer nickelate La$_2$SmNi$_2$O$_7$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01118
Authors: Ethan Sa\"iman, Feiyu Li, Junjie Zhang, Jean-Pascal Rueff, Victor Bal\'edent
Abstract: Models of unconventional superconductivity in cuprates and nickelates depend sensitively on the distribution of valence electrons among the $d$ orbitals. But accurately determining the orbital electron or hole occupations in these systems remains a difficult and unresolved task. Here, using $s$-core-level non-resonant inelastic x-ray scattering at the Ni $M_1$ edge, we determine the angular distribution of the Ni $3d$ holes in a single crystal of La$_2$SmNi$_2$O$_7$. Our results demonstrate that the hole occupation ratio $\hz/\hx = 0.94\pm0.06$, thus significantly above the expected value of $2/3$ for a filled $d_{z^2}$ orbitals models and close to value expected in a Hund's-coupling picture. Our results support superconductivity scenarios in which both $e_g$ orbitals contribute to the electron-pairing mechanism in La$_2$SmNi$_2$O$_7$.

### Paper [56]
Title: Nonlinear Physics and Self-Organized Criticality in Astrophysics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01119
Authors: Markus J. Aschwanden
Abstract: The definition of self-organized criticality (SOC) is ambiguous and controversial. Here we discuss the nonlinear physics of six basic SOC definitions, such as: phase transitions, fractal structures, power laws, SOC contingency, exponential growth, and diffusive decay. Statistics of SOC events or avalanche processes can be inferred from their size distributions, or from their spatio-temporal evolution.

### Paper [57]
Title: Ab Initio strain-dependent vibrational analysis of different silicene structures for 2D devices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01166
Authors: Archishman Gupta, Ankit Arora
Abstract: We report first-principles phonon dynamics studies in isotropically strained mono-layer silicene films of various topologies by density functional theory and density functional perturbation theory methods. The strain introduced in the sheets are of both tensile and compressive nature and its effect on the thermal transport properties are analysed using the Boltzmann Transport Equation (BTE) reported. We performed electronic and vibrational analysis of each silicene topology and concluded that hhk-silicene is a potential candidate for next generation electro-thermal devices due its molecular stability, bulk-Si like bandgap and tolerable thermal performance that can be engineered by strain. The novel hhk-silicene topology offers excellent thermal and electronic capabilities that are sensitive to strain along with the band gap following a near-linear monotonically decreasing trend making it suitable for strain-tronics applications.

### Paper [58]
Title: Rapid microscopic decorrelation precedes sustained collective ordering in quenched two-dimensional colloids
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01192
Authors: Ruixin Zhou, Boliang Yu
Abstract: Independent Brownian noise can rapidly separate trajectories launched from the same microscopic configuration, but configuration-space distance need not reveal their later assembly outcomes. We studied this distinction in 480 two-dimensional Brownian-dynamics trajectories generated as three independent-noise replicas of 40 parent configurations under four finite-duration quenches. Ninety-two of 160 replica sets sharing one parent and one protocol reached both ordered and non-ordered terminal outcomes. A relative-diffusion calculation relates the free-particle mean-squared replica separation to the time-integrated diffusivity. The measured mean-squared separation remained about one-sixth of the free-particle reference, while its scaling was indistinguishable for replica pairs with the same and different outcomes. In contrast, outcome-resolved global sixfold order, ordered-particle population and coherence, and reciprocal-space translational order separated progressively after quench completion. With quench durations weighted equally, global order, the state score combining global and population order, coherence, and translational order remained informative 0.50 reduced time units before terminal assessment. This result persisted after either changing the terminal window or reducing the integration timestep. Transient threshold crossings were common, and 94.5\% of non-ordered crossings subsequently lost the required ordered-particle population. An independent $N=128$ ensemble showed state-score discrimination at temperatures for which both terminal classes occurred. The assembly dynamics therefore separate generic microscopic decorrelation from the later emergence and finite-window persistence of collective order associated with terminal structure.

### Paper [59]
Title: Visualizing Anisotropic Charge Transport in CrSBr using Bias-Dependent Kelvin Probe Force Microscopy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01217
Authors: Satyam Sahu, Arijit Kayal, Bing Wu, Aljoscha S\"oll, Zden\v{e}k Sofer, Mat\v{e}j Velick\'y, Otakar Frank
Abstract: Anisotropic semiconductors offer unique opportunities for next-generation electronics due to their direction-dependent transport. However, conventional electrical measurements provide only spatially averaged characteristics. Here, we employ bias-dependent Kelvin probe force microscopy (KPFM) to directly visualize the response of CrSBr under different electrical bias configurations. Spatially resolved KPFM maps reveal highly distinct, crystallographic-direction-dependent potential profiles. To elucidate this behavior, we employ anisotropic transport simulations that capture the charge transport and electric-field distributions in CrSBr. The close agreement between experiment and simulation provides a unified picture of anisotropic charge transport and bias-induced potential redistribution. Our experiments further reveal a boundary-associated redistribution of the transport field when bias is applied along the $a$-axis, suggesting a possible role of crystal boundaries in charge transport along the low-conductivity direction. This work establishes bias-dependent KPFM as a powerful platform for uncovering localized transport phenomena in low-symmetry quantum materials.

### Paper [60]
Title: Adiabatic pulsed-field dynamics of a frustrated spin-lattice system
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01223
Authors: Hidemaro Suwa
Abstract: Magnetocaloric measurements in pulsed magnetic fields probe competing phases through the coupled evolution of magnetization and temperature. We formulate an adiabatic classical spin-lattice simulation that treats bond and site optical coordinates explicitly while representing the remaining thermal degrees of freedom by a finite internal reservoir. Magnetic work and energy exchange determine its temperature along each trajectory, providing feedback to the Langevin dynamics without external heat flow. Applied to a pyrochlore exchange-striction model motivated by CdCr$_2$O$_4$, the method produces a half-magnetization plateau, cooling near its onset, a temperature dome, and a change in cooling slope near plateau exit. Their joint emergence without an imposed temperature profile captures the experimental sequence of magnetic and thermal features. An approximate magnetization crossing near the temperature maximum is consistent with the reversible thermodynamic interpretation of the dome. The phenomenological reservoir enables this qualitative comparison within a simple model.

### Paper [61]
Title: Compliance-Shifting Elastogranular Shells
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01278
Authors: Jairaj Narendran, Paolo Limcaoco, Shashank S. Basty, Douglas P. Holmes
Abstract: Shells and grains separately exhibit complex nonlinear behavior when confined - shells buckle and snap, while grains transition from fluid-like to solid-like. When coupled, elastogranular structures have the ability to display a remarkable variety of mechanical behaviors. Gere we created an elastogranular shell where spherical grains are sandwiched between two thin, elastic shells. By evacuating the air enclosed by the two layers, we can tune the stiffness of the elastogranular shell and alter its critical buckling load. Introducing controlled vacancy defects in the arrangement of the grains allows us to more finely tune critical buckling parameters, as the shell must balance the combined effects of defect size and resultant granular packing density. Defects in the jammed granular layer have a counterintuitive effect on the shell's buckling load, as they can stiffen the shell away from the defect. With an empirically determined scaling model, we established how the shell and grains distribute load and create a compliance-shifting structure with a tunable buckling load.

### Paper [62]
Title: From First Bond to Stable Adhesion: Multivalent Bond Stabilization in Receptor-Mediated Endocytosis
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01293
Authors: Hao Wu
Abstract: After a particle forms its first receptor--ligand bond near a cell membrane, stable attachment often requires a threshold number of additional bonds. This paper develops a biophysical kinetic theory of this post-capture stage. The bond-number dynamics are modeled as a finite birth--death chain on $n=0,1,\dots,m$, where $0$ is loss of the initial bond and $m$ is the stabilization threshold. The mesoscopic rates $\lambda_n$ and $\mu_n$ encode the local energy landscape, accessible receptor--ligand pairs, steric and orientational constraints, and cooperative interactions within the growing cluster. The probability of reaching the threshold before losing the first bond is derived in closed form for general rates and, in the constant-ratio case, reduces to a geometric series controlled by the free-energy bias $\rho_n=\mu_n/\lambda_n$. The mean time to failure or stabilization follows from a tridiagonal backward equation. Conditioning on successful attachment is treated as a trajectory-selection effect: the conditioned chain is a Doob $h$-transform whose dissociation rate at $n=1$ vanishes, because successful trajectories cannot lose the first bond early. A minimal interface between the spatial first-capture probability and the bond-number chain is formulated, together with the conditions under which the stable capture probability factorizes into their product. The analysis shows that hitting probabilities constrain only the rate ratios, whereas the absolute rate scale requires time-resolved or dwell-time data. The threshold $m$ is a model-defined stabilization criterion, not a directly measured ligand count. The rates and threshold are effective parameters specified independently or inferred from kinetic data. The theory provides a quantitative framework for interpreting attachment probabilities, lifetimes, and the transition from first bond formation to stable multivalent adhesion.

### Paper [63]
Title: Before the First Bond: Depletion-Assisted Stochastic Capture at the Onset of Endocytosis
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01321
Authors: Hao Wu
Abstract: In a crowded extracellular environment, viruses and nanoparticles must reach the cell membrane within a few nanometers before the first ligand-receptor bond can form. Crowders generate an entropic depletion attraction, yet continuum models usually assume contact is already established or treat the approach deterministically. We formulate a thermodynamically consistent stochastic theory for this pre-contact stage. The sphere-plane depletion geometry uses the exact Asakur-Oosawa spherical-cap overlap, rather than a fitted cap, with either ideal van 't Hoff or pressure-only Carnahan-Starling osmotic pressure. A spatially varying near-wall mobility introduces a noise-induced drift that is essential for thermodynamic consistency and is often omitted. The first specific reaction is modeled by a Robin radiation boundary; the outer boundary is reflecting or absorbing. Three first-passage problems are solved analytically by quadrature: the mean reaction time, the reaction-before-escape probability, and the conditional success time, the last encoded by a Doob transform that exposes the selection bias of conditioning on capture. The theory separates thermodynamic inputs from conditional parameters and reveals a reaction-limited to diffusion-limited crossover controlled by the local Damk\"ohler number. For typical virus and nanoparticle parameters, the depletion well reaches $2$-$6\,k_{\rm B}T$, capture times are $0.1$-$1\,{\rm ms}$, and the conditional success time is measurably shorter than the unconditional mean. Finite-volume and Chebyshev schemes reproduce the analytic results within their discretization errors and satisfy the radiation-boundary mass balance. This provides a quantitative theoretical framework for crowding-assisted capture prior to membrane wrapping and a reference for comparing crowding, near-wall mobility, and surface reactivity in endocytosis.

### Paper [64]
Title: Artificial spin ice on the CaVO lattice: a tricritical bridge from square to kagome
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01397
Authors: F. Museur, G. M. Macauley, S. Nilsson, V. Scagnoli, P. M. Derlet, L. J. Heyderman
Abstract: Artificial spin ices (ASIs) are arrays of interacting nanomagnets in which phase transitions can be imaged and controlled. Studies of the thermodynamic have focused mainly on the square and kagome lattices, and on their variants. Here we study artificial spin ice on the CaVO lattice, formed of squares and octagons. At fixed lattice constant, the ratio $r$ of the square and octagon link lengths tunes the interactions strongly while changing the geometry only slightly. Magnetic force microscopy and Monte Carlo simulations show a continuous bridge from square-ice-like to kagome-like behavior. At small $r$ the system has a single ordering transition, whereas at large $r$ it passes from a correlated paramagnet to charge order and then long-range order. The two regimes meet in a multicritical region, accurately modeled by a Blume-Capel model of the octagon toroidal moments. From this picture, we design a comparative magnetometry protocol that unambiguously separates interaction effects from single-nanomagnet effects in thermally active samples. Finally, the charge-ordered region maps onto a tunable dimer model, with a Coulomb phase and pinch points along the line between two ordered dimer phases. The CaVO ASI thus provides access to a rich phase diagram only through geometric tuning, without requiring extreme experimental conditions.

### Paper [65]
Title: Beyond-RTA magnetoconductivity of multifold semimetals under generic orientations of electromagnetic fields
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01398
Authors: Ipsita Mandal
Abstract: We present an exact solution of the linearised semiclassical Boltzmann equation (SBE) beyond the relaxation-time approximation for multifold semimetals under arbitrarily oriented electric and magnetic fields. The framework treats the Berry curvature, orbital magnetic moment, Lorentz-force operator, and scattering terms on equal footing. Momentum-independent scattering-amplitudes couple the Fermi-surface sheets through intranode, interband, and internode processes. The scattering kernel has a finite rank and the SBE reduces to a linear system for a few moments, which is closed by the electron-number constraint. We apply this approach to four systems involving quadratic-corrected triple-point, Weyl, and Rarita-Schwinger-Weyl nodes, including tilted triple-point nodes. We obtain analytical results at zero field and to leading orders in the magnetic field, alongside numerical solutions for the full field dependence. Our results elucidate the roles of the topological properties, Lorentz-force operator, tilt, and intra- and internode scatterings in shaping the magnetoelectric conductivity. It cures severe shortcomings of previous treatments like incomplete ans\"atze and incomplete treatment of the Lorentz-force operator.

### Paper [66]
Title: Plasmon modes in three-dimensional multifold semimetals. II. Rarita-Schwinger-Weyl and Kramers-Weyl nodes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01401
Authors: Ipsita Mandal
Abstract: We investigate collective charge excitations in three-dimensional multifold semimetals in which multiple bands cross the Fermi level at a positive chemical potential, focusing on pseudospin-3/2 Rarita-Schwinger-Weyl semimetals and on Kramers-Weyl semimetals. Both systems host multiple concentric Fermi surfaces at positive chemical potential. Within the random-phase approximation, we analyse the density response, Drude weights, dielectric function, plasmon modes and their Landau damping, and Friedel oscillations, considering both small and arbitrary momentum transfers. In our companion work, arXiv:2610.18629, we investigated pseudospin-1 triple-point semimetals, with and without a quadratic correction to their dispersion, as well as Hopf and Weyl semimetals. Comparing these systems allows us to elucidate the distinct roles of band dispersions and eigenstates in shaping collective charge dynamics. Our results demonstrate that collective charge excitations can distinguish multifold nodes with multiple Fermi surfaces and reveal information encoded in their eigenstates beyond that contained in the single-particle dispersion.

### Paper [67]
Title: Learning materials spectra across modalities and scales with structure-conditioned neural fields
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01417
Authors: Tianyu Zhang, Yuan Ni, Roy Liu, Garrett Granroth, Daniel M. Pajerowski, Joshua J. Turner, Yongqiang Cheng, Zhantao Chen
Abstract: Spectroscopic observables encode rich information about material behavior across diverse, often high-dimensional domains of energy, momentum, and other experimental coordinates, providing critical insights into the physical properties underlying material functionalities and applications. However, learning these responses directly from atomic structures remains challenging due to the complexity of both structural configurations and spectroscopic observation spaces. Here, we develop a structure-conditioned neural-field framework that learns continuous spectroscopic response functions by coupling an equivariant structural encoder with a coordinate-conditioned network. The framework accurately predicts spectroscopic observables across diverse observation domains, ranging from one-dimensional densities of states to multidimensional scattering functions. We show that fine-tuning with limited spectral supervision captures strain- and composition-dependent spectral changes, with validated transfer to alloy configurations containing hundreds of atoms and computational scalability demonstrated for configurations exceeding $10^4$ atoms. Our work establishes a versatile and computationally scalable approach to learning structure-spectroscopy relationships, providing a foundation for spectroscopic analysis and future materials characterization, inverse problems, and experimental design.

### Paper [68]
Title: Polarization instability of unpolarized excited states in trapped two-component Bose--Einstein condensates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01488
Authors: Zihao Liu, Sulun Du, Mingshu Zhao
Abstract: A binary Bose--Einstein condensate in an elongated trap supports unpolarized excited states, in which both components share the same spatial profile. Within mean-field theory we show that the interspecies repulsion at which these states demix rises with the excitation level. Since each component keeps its atom number, the components separate by moving in opposite directions in the trap, and demixing sets in when the interaction energy released by the separation balances its cost in kinetic and trap energy. We show that the threshold also marks the onset of an exponential, non-oscillating growth of the separation, which divides the cloud into domains of opposite polarization, with walls at the density nodes and further walls interlaced with them. In the Thomas--Fermi regime of a quasi-one-dimensional condensate the rise of the threshold follows power laws in the ratio of the trap oscillator energy to the chemical potential. The trap raises the ground-state threshold, in units of the intraspecies repulsion, by 1.82 times the square of this ratio. Each density node of an excited state near the trap center raises the threshold further by 1.24 times the cube of this ratio. Because the steps between successive levels scale as the cube of this ratio, they fall as the inverse square of the atom number, and the dependence on the excitation level is a mesoscopic effect, appreciable only when the chemical potential is no more than a few times the oscillator energy. In a hyperfine mixture of $^{87}$Rb a Feshbach resonance tunes the interspecies repulsion across the thresholds of the lowest states.

### Paper [69]
Title: Theoretical Prediction of Superconducting 2D Hydrogenated Janus V2CSH and Nb2CSH MXenes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01502
Authors: Jubair Hossan Abir, M. R. U. S. Shapon, Md. Sharear Aman, Md. Kamrul Hassan, A. K. M. Azharul Islam, Saleh Hasan Naqib
Abstract: Two dimensional hydrogenated Janus MXenes represent a new frontier in superconducting materials, where asymmetric surface terminations enable tunable electronic and vibrational properties. In this work, we present the first comparative study of hydrogen or sulfur terminated V2CSH and Nb2CSH monolayers using density functional theory and density functional perturbation theory. Both compounds are structurally and thermally stable, exhibiting isotropic in plane elasticity and nonmagnetic multiband metallic states dominated by transition metal d orbitals. The asymmetric S or H terminations generate nonequivalent surface potentials and intrinsic out of plane dipoles. Electron phonon coupling constants of 0.39 (V2CSH) and 0.53 (Nb2CSH) yield superconducting transition temperatures of 2.36 K and 5.27 K, respectively, within the Allen Dynes framework. Low energy lattice vibrations, primarily involving metal and sulfur atoms, account for 77% of the coupling in both MXenes. These findings establish metal substitution as a design principle for tuning superconductivity in Janus MXenes and highlight Nb2CSH as a promising candidate for low temperature superconducting applications.

### Paper [70]
Title: Covariance-dependent participation scaling of Comtet disorder on open hyperbolic lattices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01510
Authors: Wei Li, Yong Cheng
Abstract: Negative curvature makes the electrostatic Green function decay exponentially, producing a finite disorder-variance budget even as radial volume grows exponentially. We investigate whether this near-field concentration also determines wave-function participation on open hyperbolic lattices. A Gaussian control preserves the full one-site distribution of Comtet disorder at every vertex while removing its off-diagonal covariance. Complete-window participation ratios on $\{7,3\}$ graphs with $N=847,2240,5887$ yield an effective-exponent difference of $-0.028$ at $W/t=0.5$ and $+0.116$ at $W/t=3$; both signs persist upon extending to $N=15435$. Size-resolved squared-kernel radii lie near $2.45\ell$ on the larger graphs of this sequence, below the cutoff-matched continuum value $3.12\ell$. Distance-shell weights resolve the finite discrete measure associated with this shift, while higher participation moments and spacing ratios characterize the accompanying wave-function and spectral structure. A marginal-matched exponential-kernel family gives positive exponent contrasts at all five sampled kernel lengths at $W/t=0.5,3$, separating the Comtet response from a description based on the decay length alone. These results distinguish a concentrated variance budget from the covariance-dependent participation response of finite open hyperbolic systems.

### Paper [71]
Title: Superradiance fingerprints of moir\'e exciton-biexciton condensates: path-entangled biphoton vs. supercurrent-enabled one-photon channel
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01515
Authors: Haochen Wang, Shengxian Li, Hongyu Lu, Mingxing Chen, Wang Yao
Abstract: Hybrid moir\'e excitons form a dense array of electrically tunable single-photon emitters, entailing cooperative optical responses with intriguing consequences. With the strong dipole-dipole interaction, they also realize Bose-Hubbard quantum simulators of emergent correlated matter such as Bose-Einstein condensates (BEC). Here we reveal the characteristic cooperative radiation from condensates of hybrid moir\'e excitons in twisted MoTe$_2$ bilayers. In each individual moir\'e trap, the layer hybridization selection rule dictates that the ground-state exciton is a quantum emitter of orbital-angular-momentum photons, with an exceptionally long radiative lifetime. Ground-state emission becomes pronounced only upon forming condensates, where inter-sublattice dipole attraction and intra-sublattice repulsion together lead to the coexistence of exciton and biexciton BEC with comparable weights. The biexciton (molecular-type) BEC decays through superradiance of path-entangled biphotons, whereas the exciton (atomic-type) BEC is dark at rest and becomes superradiant when it carries a supercurrent. Our findings not only uncover a novel source of entangled light but also establish unique fingerprints for exploring the cold chemistry of atomic-molecular condensates with moir\'e excitons.

### Paper [72]
Title: Fredholm approximations to the Bethe-Salpeter equation with spherical-Bessel kernels for Wannier-Mott excitons
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01570
Authors: Christian Tantardini, Benedikt Maurer, Claudia Draxl
Abstract: We develop a deterministic radial approximation to the static Bethe-Salpeter equation (BSE), designed for rapid preliminary screening of Wannier-Mott exciton binding across direct-gap bulk materials. The approximation replaces the electron-hole transition problem by small radial eigenvalue problems that require no stochastic sampling. Starting from the four-point BSE and its matrix representation in a Bloch electron-hole basis, we retain one valence and one conduction band in the effective-mass limit within the Tamm-Dancoff approximation. We introduce a central projection of the screened direct interaction and omit bare electron-hole exchange. The resulting model applies to spatially extended excitons whose envelopes vary slowly on the scale of the unit cell, provided that the effective-mass description and central screened interaction adequately represent the exciton of interest. A partial-wave decomposition separates the angular-momentum channels and expresses their interaction kernels as spherical-Bessel transforms of the real-space screened Coulomb interaction. Nystr\"{o}m discretization produces real symmetric radial matrices suitable for standard eigensolvers. Their dimension is set by the radial grid independently of the Brillouin-zone sampling used to calculate screening. We implement the formulation in the all-electron, full-potential code $\texttt{exciting}$. For bulk GaAs, the compact radial problem reproduces the corresponding conventional matrix-BSE binding energy within $(1~\mathrm{meV})$ at matched sampling. Further refinement achieves finite-mesh stability within the same tolerance on denser screening meshes without increasing the radial matrix size.

### Paper [73]
Title: Exact solution of the dynamic pseudogap model: Fock-space formalism for Gaussian fluctuations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01574
Authors: Michael V. Sadovskii
Abstract: We present an exact solution of the dynamic finite-$Q$ pseudogap model, in which electrons scatter between two nested Fermi-surface patches off a classical Gaussian order-parameter field with characteristic frequency $\omega_0$ and damping $\gamma$. The disorder-averaged Green's function is shown to be a single matrix element of the resolvent of a sparse non-Hermitian matrix acting on a Fock space of two (commensurate fluctuations) or four (incommensurate fluctuations) bosonic modes with complex energies. The non-commutativity of the pseudospin vertices at different times, which prevented an exact treatment so far, is retained in full. We derive the representation, its symmetries, and the exact limits it contains: the static continued fractions, the closed-form commensurate solution at the Fermi surface, and the second-order self-energy. A numerically exact implementation gives spectral functions and densities of states for all values of the fluctuation frequency, damping and electron energy. We find that the sideband comb of the spectral function stays close to multiples of $\omega_0$ and that quasiparticle damping is governed by an energy-dependent decoherence rate. At resonance of a quasiparticle energy with $\omega_0/2$, we obtain the static fuctuating-gap in a rotating frame, which leads to resonant splitting of the quasiparticle peak.

### Paper [74]
Title: A Clifford-index approach connecting boundary Dirac fermions to sigma-model topological terms in the periodic table of topological insulators and superconductors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01619
Authors: Y. X. Zhao
Abstract: The periodic table of topological insulators and superconductors emerges from two distinct approaches: boundary Anderson localization and momentum-space K-theory. Connecting these approaches physically requires deriving the boundary sigma-model topological terms from the underlying fermion theories. We develop an index-theoretic framework for minimal massless boundary Dirac models in the eight real Altland--Zirnbauer classes. Its central ingredients are an elliptic cylinder operator interpolating between two sigma-field textures, a quotient index defined by the cylinder's graded Clifford symmetry algebra, and an index theorem identifying its protected zero-mode index with the relative homotopy class of the endpoint textures. To establish this correspondence, we deform the cylinder texture while preserving the kernel's index class and localize the obstruction to a Clifford defect. An exact oscillator-kernel calculation fixes the generator normalization. A field-independent congruence and a common regularization provide a real antisymmetric Pfaffian representative whose sign transport is related to the cylinder mod-two index by Witten's spectral-flow argument. This yields the nontrivial $\mathbb{Z}_2$ theta weight for one minimal physical module. Combining complex-parent anomalies with physical Clifford multiplicities and real-target periods also gives the real-class Wess--Zumino terms with unit spherical level. The framework thus provides an operator-level connection between boundary Dirac theories and the sigma-model topological terms associated with the periodic table.

### Paper [75]
Title: Atomistic Insights into MOCVD Growth of GaAs using a Closed Loop Autonomous System Combining Directed Reinforcement Learning and Kinetic Monte Carlo Simulations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01646
Authors: Hrishi V. Harsora, Ashutosh Krishna Amaram, Raghavan Ranganathan, Tarun K. Agarwal
Abstract: Conventional process optimization of the Metal-Organic Chemical Vapor Deposition (MOCVD) growth parameters to achieve high-quality thin films relies on iterative, resource-intensive experimental runs. While predictive modeling can circumvent this cost and effort, simulating thin-film growth is constrained by the vastly different length and time scales of the deposition process. While continuum (e.g., Computational Fluid Dynamics (CFD)) and first-principles methods (e.g., Molecular Dynamics (MD)) struggle to balance experimental length/time scales and atomistic accuracy, Kinetic Monte Carlo (KMC) bridges this divide by resolving atomistic events whilst remaining tractable at experimental length and time scales. However, the accuracy of KMC relies on kinetic rate parameters that require costly density functional theory (DFT) calculations. In order to circumvent this, we present a framework that calibrates the rate parameters directly against experimental growth data. A forward KMC growth model is coupled in a closed loop with a Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) optimizer that navigates the high-dimensional parameter space, with the search steered by the degree of rate control of each reaction rate parameter. The approach invokes no material-specific assumptions and hence, any deposition process whose forward kinetics can be reduced to a set of rate-controlled events is amenable to the same treatment. We demonstrate it for the MOCVD of GaAs, where the calibrated model captures the characteristic temperature dependence of the measured growth rate. The framework is also able to recover the three well known kinetic regimes characterized by change in the rate determining step from precursor chemisorption to desorption. As a result, the framework converts routinely measured growth data into a mechanistically interpretable model of the deposition process.

### Paper [76]
Title: Structural and Optical Properties of PVP-Modified ZnS Nanoparticles for Photocatalytic Dye Degradation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01660
Authors: Vigneshwari S R, Dushyant Singh, Subha Maity, Tharundev V V, Mukesh K. Kumawat, Mukesh Dhanka, Krista R. Khiangte
Abstract: Pristine and PVP-modified ZnS nanoparticles were investigated to examine how PVP addition affects structural, surface, optical, and methylene blue (MB) decolorization properties. X-ray diffraction confirmed cubic zinc-blende ZnS in all samples, with apparent Scherrer coherent-domain sizes of 3.10-3.31 nm. Electron microscopy revealed agglomeration, while HRTEM and electron diffraction confirmed locally crystalline regions in S1 and S4. The estimated direct Tauc gap decreased from 3.69 eV for S1 to 3.58 eV for S4. Between 100 and 300 K, the principal TO-like Raman band softened on heating, while visible photoluminescence changed predominantly within the overlapping green-yellow region. After 210 min under 254 nm irradiation, MB-band losses near 664 nm reached 19.33%, 58.18%, 70.00%, and 94.42% for S1-S4, compared with 6.58% for the catalyst-free control. PVP modification therefore alters optical and dye-decolorization responses while preserving the principal ZnS phase; however, dye-band attenuation alone does not establish complete mineralization.

### Paper [77]
Title: Origin of bias-voltage-dependent transient negative capacitance in PZT ferroelectric capacitor with labyrinthine-like domains
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01697
Authors: Ganga S. Kumar, Kusumpal Yadav, Miral Verma, Parushottam Majhi, Pragati Sharma, Kaustuv Chatterjee, Sandip Bysakh, Sudipta Goswami, Prabir Pal, Chandan K. Ghosh, S. B. Roy, Vasant Sathe, Devajyoti Mukherjee, Dipten Bhattacharya
Abstract: We investigated how the bias-voltage-dependent variation in the ferroelectric domain switching pathways affects the transient negative capacitance in multidomain PZT ferroelectric capacitor with labyrinthine-like domains having, primarily, 90$^\circ$ walls. Using the vector piezoresponse force microscopy, x-ray photoelectron spectroscopy, and energy dispersive x-ray spectroscopy, we show that, together with strain, local variation in composition results in the formation of labyrinthine domains in epitaxial films of thickness $\sim$1 $\mu$m. Clear signature of emergence of bias-voltage-dependent pathways could be observed in the Raman modes exhibiting splitting due to an intermediate state - different from the initial and final states - at the ``intermediate state of switching". The polarization relaxation patterns also show different states emerging depending on the bias voltage characteristics. The phase-field simulation of relaxation of ``intermediate state of switching" corroborates the experimental results and also exhibits dependence of the domain-wall area on the bias voltage and damping parameter. Combining the results of experiments and theoretical calculation, we show, therefore, that the variation of bias voltage leads to a change in the domain switching pathways and each pathway is associated with specific domain-wall area at the ``intermediate state of switching". Since, the net domain-wall area at the ``intermediate state of switching" governs the magnitude of transient negative capacitance, this work thus traces the origin of tunability of transient negative capacitance in PZT ferroelectric capacitor.

### Paper [78]
Title: Quantum-enhanced multiphase estimation with spin-1 Bose-Einstein condensates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01746
Authors: Jiaheng Jin, Xinwei Li, Dong E. Liu, Li You
Abstract: We investigate quantum-enhanced two-phase estimation in a spin-$1$ Bose-Einstein condensate, where spin-exchange collisions generate a spin-nematic squeezed state in the $m_F=\pm1$ modes. The resulting pair-correlated state has a rank-one quantum Fisher information matrix (QFIM) for the two mode phases relative to the $m_F=0$ mode and therefore cannot resolve them independently. Nevertheless, we find that a parameter-independent passive three-mode transformation lifts this rank deficiency and enables joint estimation of both phases. In the undepleted-pump regime, we derive expressions for the QFIM, optimize the equal-weight multiparameter quantum Cram\'er-Rao bound, and obtain a full-rank probe with squeezing-enhanced sensitivity below the matched fixed-total-number standard quantum limit (SQL). These analytical predictions are confirmed by exact-diagonalization calculations. We further construct a parameter-independent readout that maps the phase combinations onto displacement channels accessible through atomic homodyne or parity measurements. Our results provide a route from spin-mixing-generated quantum correlations to two-phase estimation in spinor condensates.

### Paper [79]
Title: Asymmetric Little--Parks Oscillations as a Probe of Coupled Pairing Channels in Surface-Superconducting \texorpdfstring{PtBi$_2$}{PtBi2}
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01764
Authors: Yuriy Yerin, Stefan-Ludwig Drechsler, Carsten Timm, Jeroen van den Brink
Abstract: Motivated by the recent discovery of unconventional surface superconductivity in PtBi$_{2}$, we theoretically study its signatures in the Little-Parks oscillations of a narrow ring made from the same material. Using a Ginzburg-Landau model with three coupled pairing channels allowed by the trigonal crystal symmetry, we show that a fixed in-plane magnetic field can make the mean-field transition temperature asymmetric under reversal of the normal flux through the ring, while preserving periodicity in the superconducting flux quantum. The leading weak-field contribution to this asymmetry is cubic in the in-plane field strength. The flux-odd component respects the threefold crystal symmetry and changes sign when the in-plane field is rotated by $\pi/3$ at fixed magnitude. Within the homogeneous ring model, this asymmetry is absent for a single pairing channel and arises from the coupling between the three channels. Our calculations further show that increasing the in-plane magnetic field can enhance the superconducting nucleation temperature through mixing of pairing channels, illustrating magnetic control of multicomponent superconductivity in mesoscopic rings. Little-Parks interferometry with independent control of flux and field direction thus offers a mesoscopic probe of the pairing state, complementary to spectroscopic studies of the gap structure.

### Paper [80]
Title: Three New Phases in the Two-Dimensional Potts Model at Complex Temperature
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01771
Authors: Fang-Cheng Wang, Qi-Jun Ye, Xin-Zheng Li
Abstract: The complex-temperature phase structure of the two-dimensional Potts model has remained largely unresolved despite decades of study. Here we determine the complex-temperature phase diagram of the 3-state Potts model, and uncover three gapless phases beyond the familiar ferromagnetic and paramagnetic phases. This is done by combining tensor-network free energies with the Poisson equation to compute the Fisher zero density in the thermodynamic limit. The resulting zero distribution unifies and revises long-standing conjectures on the zero loci, and outlines each phase. One is a non-conformal gapless phase, with an exponentially growing number of low-lying transfer-matrix levels distributed across all momentum sectors. The other two are Luttinger-liquid phases with complex-valued Luttinger parameters related by the Kramers-Wannier duality. At the antiferromagnetic critical point, the Luttinger parameter equals 3, while its limit from the adjacent Luttinger-liquid phase is 6. These findings reveal rich phase structures and unconventional critical behavior in a classical textbook model.

### Paper [81]
Title: Stable AgBiS2 near-infrared absorbers for thin film tandem photovoltaics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01797
Authors: Shiling Dong, Yongjie Wang, Hae-Jun Seok, Mengyuan Huang, Edward Newton, Der Ying Chen, Yixin Wang, Xiaoyu Guo, Keisuke Takemoto, Dusan Ivanovic, Avari Roy, Yongge Yang, Grey Christoforo, Rebekah Fraser, Seongrok Seo, John O'Sullivan, Ha Kyung Park, Jack M. Woolley, Ji-Seon Kim, Ruy S. Bonilla, Henry J. Snaith, Qing Shen, Aditya Sadhanala, Lydia H. Wong, Robert L. Z. Hoye
Abstract: AgBiS2 has rapidly risen in efficiency as single-junction photovoltaics (PVs), but its potential for tandem PVs is unexplored. Herein, we perform optical modeling based on the experimental absorption spectrum of AgBiS2, revealing that, as the NIR sub-cell, efficiencies exceeding 47.9% are achievable in triple-junction tandems, superseding tandems using Pb/Sn perovskite NIR cells (35.9%). However, fulfilling this potential requires overcoming the limited transport properties of AgBiS2 nanocrystal films, which constrains film thicknesses to only 30-50 nm, severely restricting NIR response. A solution-phase ligand exchange process is herein developed, allowing one-step deposition of passivated AgBiS2 nanocrystals that maintain efficient charge-carrier extraction despite an order of magnitude increase in film thickness. These films enable 24.0%-efficient four-terminal tandems with visible-light-absorbing lead-halide perovskite sub-cells, and monolithic tandems successfully demonstrating voltage addition. AgBiS2 PVs have high stability, retaining >90% of the original efficiency after 3 months of storage in dry air without encapsulation. This work establishes the significant potential of AgBiS2 as a stable and scalable NIR absorber for tandem PVs.

### Paper [82]
Title: FLAKE: Friction and Lattice Analysis of Kinetics and Energetics of rigid 2D clusters on crystalline substrates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01805
Authors: Andrea Silva
Abstract: When a small solid island slides across a crystalline surface, the outcome (smooth flow or stick-slip, free rotation or angular locking) is controlled almost entirely by the geometric relationship between the island and the surface: their relative orientation, size and lattice symmetry. These phenomena are central to nanoscale friction, to the interpretation of colloidal monolayer experiments and to structural superlubricity. FLAKE (Friction and Lattice Analysis of Kinetics and Energetics) is a Python package for simulating the statics and Langevin dynamics of a rigid 2D cluster sliding over a periodic or quasiperiodic substrate potential. It enables fast, systematic exploration of how geometry and substrate symmetry control the energy landscape and friction, studies that are prohibitively expensive with fully atomistic simulations. FLAKE has been the primary simulation tool for three published studies on structural superlubricity and colloidal friction.

### Paper [83]
Title: Temperature-Dependent Raman Signatures of Local Symmetry Breaking in Vacancy Double Perovskite Cs$_2$SnI$_6$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01830
Authors: Tomke E. Glier, Nicholas J. Weadock, Julian A. Vigil, Cole Shank, Xixi Qin, Gabriel J. Graf, Hemamala I. Karunadasa, Volker Blum, Michael F. Toney, Dmitry Reznik
Abstract: Understanding and controlling the optical, thermal and mechanical properties of K$_2$PtCl$_6$-type double perovskites is essential for the design of lead-free, high-performance thermoelectric and optoelectronic materials. In this study, we investigate Cs$_2$SnI$_6$ using temperature-dependent near-resonance Raman scattering, complemented by density functional theory-based phonon calculations. The appearance of Raman-forbidden phonon modes in the Raman response at cryogenic temperatures provides strong evidence for local symmetry breaking that does not develop into a global structural phase transition. Through detailed analysis of phonon intensities, energies, and spectral lineshapes, we characterize the temperature evolution of the symmetry-broken structural response of Cs$_2$SnI$_6$. Below 200 K, multi-phonon scattering is strongly enhanced, and symmetry-forbidden Raman modes emerge. The temperature dependence of phonon intensities follows a phenomenological Arrhenius behavior, revealing an activation energy of 15-20 meV. These results establish temperature-dependent Raman spectroscopy as a sensitive probe of local structural distortions and anharmonic structural dynamics in Cs$_2$SnI$_6$, providing a robust experimental basis for discussing the microscopic origins of symmetry breaking and electron-phonon coupling in halide double perovskites.

### Paper [84]
Title: Multipolar Anisotropy of Magnonic Orbital Pumping in $d$-Wave Altermagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01848
Authors: Myeonil Choi, Guidobeth S\'aez, Daegeun Jo, Yuriy Mokrousov, Hyun-Woo Lee, Kyoung-Whan Kim, Dongwook Go
Abstract: We show that antiferromagnetic magnons in $d$-wave altermagnets lead to orbital pumping with distinctive $d$-wave angular dependence on the direction of the N\'eel vector. We find that the anisotropy is generally much larger than that of spin pumping. We elucidate that this difference originates from the direct coupling of the orbital response to the electrical quadrupole moments induced by staggered local crystal field in an altermagnet. By symmetry analysis, we show that the anisotropy does not cancel for the two opposite-handedness antiferromagnetic magnons, suggesting that our prediction can be observed even with thermally excited magnons. We further analyze the handedness dependence for the situation with coherent magnons. These results establish multipolar anisotropy of orbital pumping as a direct dynamical signature of $d$-wave altermagnetic order and highlight the potential of altermagnets as a platform for magnon-driven orbitronics as well as for the interplay between magnons and electrical multipoles.

### Paper [85]
Title: Interaction-Enabled Bulk-Boundary Correspondence without a Global Band Gap
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01873
Authors: Cong Ding, Xuzhen Cao, Zhifu Shi, Yuqing Li, Ying Hu
Abstract: A challenge of growing interest is how to make topology observable when conventional gap-based frameworks no longer suffice. In bulk-edge correspondence, this challenge arises when the global band gap closes and localized boundary modes disappear, even though the bulk topology remains intact. Here we show that interactions unlock a novel emergent boundary response from this spectrally hidden topology without reopening the global band gap. These interaction-induced excitations originate from a bulk-band minimum, yet reconstruct the characteristic boundary channels of a gapped topological parent sharing the same Bloch eigenspaces. We establish this interaction-enabled bulk-boundary correspondence for first- and higher-order topology in one- and two-dimensional bosonic lattices, respectively, and identify a general compatibility principle governing its emergence. Our results extend bulk-boundary correspondence beyond its conventional spectral manifestation, with experimental prospects in cold-atom and photonic platforms.

### Paper [86]
Title: Fractional quantum Hall effect at {\nu}=1/2 in wide GaAs quantum wells: Subband structure, exact diagonalization, and the magnitude of the gap
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01885
Authors: Sankar Das Sarma
Abstract: Recent experiments on a wide GaAs quantum well report a fractional quantum Hall state at filling factor {\nu}=1/2 with an unusually large activation gap, a sharp maximum of the gap as a function of density, and a possible transition from the non-Abelian Moore-Read Pfaffian to the Abelian Halperin 331 state. We analyze this system theoretically, with self-consistent subband calculations, exact diagonalization, and a reanalysis of the published transport data. We find that the well has two relevant subbands, that with the second subband frozen out the well most likely has no incompressible state at {\nu}=1/2, that a strict bilayer at the maxima of the charge density does not produce 331, and that an active second subband is essential: the ground state is a paired state of electrons in both subbands, on a continuous path between the Pfaffian and 331, with a neutral gap much larger than that of a one-subband Pfaffian. The position of the state on this path is controlled by electrostatic energies that the accessible system sizes contain only in part, and the standard overlaps do not identify the state at these sizes. Our results are more consistent with a Pfaffian-like state than with 331, but they are not definitive. The transport data do not support the reported magnitude of the gap; the gap we extract is smaller and comparable to the calculated two-subband neutral gap. A benchmark calculation for the Laughlin state at {\nu}=1/3 in the same well provides an experimental test of the electrostatic treatment.

### Paper [87]
Title: Disorder, Interactions, and the Fate of the Two-Dimensional Electron System: Wigner Crystal, Metal-Insulator Transition, and Amorphous Solid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01899
Authors: Sankar Das Sarma
Abstract: We present a unified theoretical study of the interplay between Coulomb disorder and Coulomb interactions in two-dimensional electron systems, covering the Wigner crystallization transition, the metal-insulator transition, and the crossover from a Wigner crystal to an amorphous localized solid in both zero magnetic field and the lowest Landau level. Using Hartree-Fock theory, variational Monte Carlo, and percolation and strong-localization arguments, we show that quenched charged disorder destroys true long-range Wigner crystalline order in the thermodynamic limit at any nonzero charged-impurity density, within the Imry-Ma-Larkin framework; the finite-system density image is that of an amorphous solid with crystalline coherence over at most a few lattice spacings. For the two-dimensional metal-insulator transition we show, in a controlled nonlinear-Thomas-Fermi model of long-range Coulomb disorder at setback d, that the half-depletion density of the semiclassical landscape and the Ioffe-Regel-Mott density of Born-RPA transport track each other to within a factor of order unity over the physically relevant range of impurity density and setback, with a slow n_i^(-1/10) d^(-1/5) spread; the agreement reflects the common energy scale E_dis ~ E_F at the transition rather than a theorem, and modest independent short-range transport channels can shift the ratio by factors of order two. The article is a theoretical study under well-defined approximations; it takes the recent scanning tunneling microscopy imaging experiments on two-dimensional electron systems and the longstanding disorder-versus-interaction debate on the two-dimensional metal-insulator transition as motivation, and leaves to the reader the comparison between its calculated signatures and the published experimental analyses.

### Paper [88]
Title: Symmetry-Based Transport Criterion and Robust Design for RuO$_2$-type Altermagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01944
Authors: Zhaoqing Li, Yi Liu, Yizheng Wu, Zhe Yuan
Abstract: Altermagnetism combines vanishing net magnetization with momentum-dependent spin splitting, enabling spin-transport functionalities without ferromagnetism. Although rutile RuO$_2$ has been proposed as a d-wave altermagnet, its magnetic ground state remains highly controversial. Moreover, existing transport verifications suffer from severe ambiguity due to the entanglement of spin-splitting effects and time-reversal-even spin-Hall contributions. Here, we introduce a two-pronged strategy to break the current verification impasse. First, we establish a definitive, symmetry-based transport criterion: by identifying specific crystallographic geometries where the spin-Hall background is strictly forbidden, we provide a zero-background window for unambiguous altermagnetic detection. Concurrently, to bypass the uncertain bulk energetics, we propose the RuO$_2$/MnO$_2$ superlattice as a prototypical platform to robustly enforce the d-wave altermagnetic order via interfacial magnetic proximity. Within this platform, symmetry-resolved spin-transport analysis reveals unconventional responses, including collinear spin currents enabling field-free magnetization switching and sizable spin-polarized charge currents. By combining a universal transport criterion with a robustly engineered material platform, our work transcends the debates over bulk properties, providing an actionable, definitive experimental roadmap for verifying d-wave altermagnetism.

### Paper [89]
Title: Weak-Signal Amplification through Stochastic Resetting
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01991
Authors: Wei Wang, Kaixuan Li, Jinjie Zhu, Andrey G. Cherstvy, Ralf Metzler, Yong Xu
Abstract: Stochastic resetting (SR) offers a distinct route to amplifying weak periodic signals in nonlinear systems. Here, we demonstrate SR-induced resonance (SRIR) in a system with double-well potential driven by a weak periodic force, where the amplitude of the mean periodic response exhibits a pronounced maximum at an optimal SR rate. The underlying mechanism exploits dynamical instability: SR repeatedly brings the system close to an unstable state, where the phase of the periodic force at each reset biases the subsequent motion toward one of the stable states. The local instability then amplifies this weak bias into a large-scale response. The SRIR arises from a competition between rare resets that limit phase-dependent state selection and frequent resets that prevent the system from fully relaxing to the selected state. We develop a general renewal formulation for the periodic response and a coarse-grained approximation that predicts the optimal resetting rate, revealing the low-frequency scaling $r_{\rm opt}(\omega)\propto\omega^{2/3}$, in agreement with numerical simulations. SRIR is fundamentally distinct from conventional noise-induced resonance (NIR), which relies on noise-activated interwell transitions whose rates are periodically modulated by the external driving force. In the weak-forcing and slow-driving regime, SRIR can achieve much stronger signal amplification than NIR. SRIR persists for a finite spread of resetting positions, and our framework extends beyond bistability. We further discuss how SRIR and NIR can be selectively controlled. Our work proposes a novel approach to the classical paradigm of stochastic resonance.

### Paper [90]
Title: Topological Defect Statistics in a Weakly First-Order Phase Transition
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02022
Authors: Antonin Rottman, Leonard Logari\'c, Fumika Suzuki, Adolfo del Campo
Abstract: The celebrated Kibble-Zurek mechanism (KZM) characterizes the nonequilibrium dynamics of second-order phase transitions and predicts universal scaling of the average number of defects with the quench time. However, the KZM is not directly applicable to first-order phase transitions, where defect formation is instead governed by nucleation processes. Here, using a Ginzburg-Landau model, we investigate the defect-number statistics in a weakly first-order phase transition. We show that the resulting statistics contain signatures of both Kibble-Zurek dynamics and nucleation, revealing the interplay between these two defect-formation mechanisms. In particular, we find that the defect-number distribution evolves from approximately Gaussian to nearly Poissonian across the crossover from the KZM regime to nucleation-dominated defect formation. While the first two cumulants are well described by a simple additive estimate of the KZM and nucleation contributions, the third cumulant exhibits a plateau and an enhancement beyond this estimate, providing a sensitive probe of the interplay between the two defect-formation mechanisms.

### Paper [91]
Title: Trapping photo-carriers in undoped gate-defined quantum dots using an on-chip microwave resonator for charge readout
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02050
Authors: Pierre Leflo\"ic, Zhiren Wang, Yasmine Faraj, Harshana Runjeet, Michel Pioro-Ladri\`ere, Alicia Kam, Louis Gaudreau, Mathieu Juan
Abstract: Scaling up gate-defined quantum dot systems is hindered by the rapid growth in the number of control gates. To tackle this challenge, we propose a different approach where the quantum dots are created from optically generated charges trapped beneath accumulation gates. By shining an above-the-gap laser light onto an undoped GaAs substrate, we demonstrate that it is possible to create and separate electron-hole pairs to form quantum dots. For the charge readout, we use a superconducting coplanar waveguide resonator capacitively coupled to the quantum dots. With these elements, we demonstrate a working many-charge double quantum dot device comprised of only two plunger gates, one tunnel coupling gate and one sensing gate. With our approach, we show that populating quantum dots does not require engineered reservoirs, source/drain bias, ohmic contacts, or in-situ doping. As a result, the number of gates can be kept minimal and the fabrication process can be simplified. Moreover, this approach can be applied to a wide range of direct-bandgap semiconductor quantum dot systems. This hybrid device demonstration is a first step towards a different scalability approach for quantum dot arrays.

### Paper [92]
Title: Valence-Selective Electrophoretic Transport in Neutral Carbon Nanotubes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02077
Authors: Giuseppe Mattia Amato, Felipe Jim\'enez-\'Angeles, Monica Olvera de la Cruz
Abstract: Understanding how confinement alters the transport of charged molecules is central to the advancement of separation, energy, and sensing technologies. Polyamines are small, linear molecules with ionizable groups, making them an ideal model for investigating charge transport in confinement beyond monatomic ions. Using all-atom molecular dynamics (MD) simulations, we investigate how molecular length, net charge, and charge placement affect the electrophoretic transport of polyamines through single-walled carbon nanotubes (CNTs). We find that nanometer-scale confinement reverses the mobility ordering observed in free solution -- in a CNT of radius 0.68 nm, spermine (q = +4) travels more slowly than ethylamine (q = +1), contrasting with the expected scaling of mobility with valence in bulk. Further, we find that mobility decreases faster than the inverse of valence, though molecular size and length vary alongside this trend. Comparisons of doubly protonated spermidine microspecies reveal the additional influence of charge placement at fixed net valence. Upon moving one proton of dicationic spermidine from the central to a terminal nitrogen, the mobility is increased by 42%. We analyze hydration structure and solute-solvent association, identifying local-solvation differences between protonation topologies that accompany these distinct transport responses under confinement. These results suggest that neutral CNTs could support valence-selective nanofluidic transport, with additional sensitivity to molecular structure and protonation topology.

### Paper [93]
Title: Unoccupied spin texture of ordered Bi films on vicinal Ge substrates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02085
Authors: F. Goto, G. Bussetti, L. Nicola\"i, A. Pulkkinen, C. Zucchetti, F. Bottegoni, M. Finazzi, L. Du\`o, F. Ciccacci, J. Min\'ar, A. Calloni
Abstract: Vicinal surfaces offer a compelling testing ground in surface science: breaking the high symmetry of a singular surface introduces periodic step arrays that can modify surface states on the terraces and influence epitaxial ordering; the interplay between these effects governs morphology and electronic structure, especially in heavy, spin--orbit-coupled materials. We combine low-energy electron diffraction (LEED) and spin-polarized inverse photoemission (SP-IPES) with ab-initio calculations for the Bi$(1\,1\,1)$ reference, for the first time on a true high-index vicinal bismuth surface. On the ordered Bi$(9\,9\,11)$ vicinal film selected by Ge$(2\,2\,3)$, the measured unoccupied-state spin-sign sequence matches the calculated initial-state spectral function of Bi$(1\,1\,1)$ terraces. Conversely, the pseudo-cubic Bi domains retained on Ge$(4\,4\,3)$ and Ge$(3\,3\,1)$ exhibit a characteristic spin-polarized surface-state response referenced to the macroscopic vicinal surface. These results show that the substrate cut selects not only the morphology of the Bi film, but also the crystallographic reference frame of its unoccupied spin texture.

### Paper [94]
Title: When Does Triadic Balance Reduce to Pairwise Order?
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02117
Authors: Sepideh Arab Mohammadi, Amir Kargaran, Ali Hosseiny, Reza Jafari, Abbas Ali Saberi
Abstract: Structural-balance interactions are naturally defined on triads, but when pair affinities are themselves generated by continuous node attributes, it is not clear whether the resulting interaction remains genuinely higher order. Here we address this question for agents represented by unit vectors in an attribute space, with pair affinity given by their scalar product and a Heider product assigned to each triangle. For planar attributes, we show that the triadic interaction reduces exactly, at finite system size, to a pairwise Curie--Weiss XY model of the doubled angles. The ordered phase is therefore nematic and undergoes a continuous mean-field $O(2)$ transition at $k_{\mathrm B}T_c=J/8$. The same reduction reveals a local $\mathbb Z_2$ degeneracy: each nematically ordered state corresponds to $2^N$ polar assignments, whose binomial multiplicities make two nearly equal opposite groups typical at low temperature. Monte Carlo simulations support the exact critical temperature, the mean-field Binder-cumulant value, and the predicted complete-graph finite-size scaling. Finally, a tensor representation of the interaction shows that the planar reduction is dimension specific: in two dimensions the cubic invariant is constrained by lower-order invariants, whereas for $d\geq3$ it is generically independent. These results identify when attribute-induced triadic balance is effectively pairwise and when genuinely higher-order structure can remain.

### Paper [95]
Title: Accelerating phase-field simulations of grain growth via tensor decomposition
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02118
Authors: Chaoqian Yuan, Dingxiang Zhu, Ye Lu
Abstract: Phase-field models provide a powerful framework for simulating grain growth phenomena in polycrystalline materials, but their application to large-scale systems is limited by the high computational and memory costs associated with resolving multiple phase variables over fine spatial and temporal scales. This paper presents a tensor decomposition based model reduction framework for accelerating phase-field simulations of grain growth. The proposed method exploits the underlying low-rank structure of the evolving phase-field variables and represents the high-dimensional solution in a spatially decomposed tensor format. By performing the numerical computations directly within this reduced representation, the framework decreases storage requirements and reduces the cost of repeatedly solving the phase-field equations. The number of modes for the decomposed approximation is controlled during the online computation with a subspace mode-updating algorithm, to balance computational efficiency and approximation accuracy. The method is assessed using representative grain growth problems, and its predictions are compared with those obtained from conventional full-order finite element simulations. The results demonstrate that the formulation can accurately reproduce the evolution of grain morphology and the principal characteristics of grain growth kinetics while substantially improving computational efficiency. The proposed framework therefore provides a promising approach for extending phase-field simulations to larger spatial domains, finer discretizations, and systems containing a large number of phase variables.

### Paper [96]
Title: Theory of the charge response of the boron-vacancy spin in monolayer hexagonal boron nitride
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02137
Authors: Dongyeon (Tyler),  Kim, Theodore Scharlotta, Daniel Hashemi
Abstract: The boron vacancy $V_{\mathrm{B}}^{-}$ in hexagonal boron nitride is a leading two-dimensional spin sensor, but the microscopic connection between nearby charge positions and its spin Hamiltonian remains poorly understood. Calculations for carbon and oxygen donors 3.85--8.08 $\mathring{\mathrm{A}}$ away show that direction matters, not only distance. In the frozen lattice, reversing a carbon donor's azimuth at 4.35 $\mathring{\mathrm{A}}$ (C1 to C1m) more than halves $|E|$ and turns its axis across the mirror line. The ratio is 0.467 with the Perdew--Burke--Ernzerhof (PBE) functional, against 1 for distance alone. A field-plus-gradient model fitted to six donor configurations, including C1 but excluding C1m, predicted a ratio of 0.443 before C1m was calculated. Heyd--Scuseria--Ernzerhof hybrid statics give 0.432 and PBE relaxation 0.600. Exploratory analysis suggests why direction matters: a charge's potential differs at the three vacancy nitrogens and moves the spin of the two $e'$ holes among them, more when the charge faces one nitrogen than the gap between two. $E$ follows this spin imbalance through one constant, $c\approx8$ GHz per $\mu_{\mathrm{B}}$, across donors and applied fields, whereas in PBE $D$ is nearly insensitive to it. A three-site model with one fitted screening constant reproduces the frozen-lattice $E$ to 83 MHz rms. Results are for one charge state in a free-standing monolayer; absolute $|E|$ varies by tens of percent across approximations.

### Paper [97]
Title: Stability, Collective Modes, and Quasiparticle-Phonon Coupling of Metallic Wigner Crystals in Rhombohedral Graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02151
Authors: Sunghoon Kim, Yang-Zhi Chou, Jay D. Sau, Sankar Das Sarma
Abstract: Recent transport experiments in rhombohedral multilayer graphene have reported evidence for metallic Wigner crystals (mWCs), characterized by the coexistence of crystalline order and a small density of hole-like carriers. This coexistence raises fundamental questions about the stability of mWCs and the fate of their collective excitations in the presence of gapless fermions. Using self-consistent Hartree-Fock and time-dependent Hartree-Fock calculations, we demonstrate that itinerant carriers profoundly influence both. We find that mWCs with electron pockets are generally unstable to particle-hole fluctuations, while hole-doped mWCs at lower densities remain locally stable. In the stable hole-doped mWC, the longitudinal phonon strongly hybridizes with particle-hole excitations, while the transverse phonon remains relatively well defined. Quasiparticle coupling is strongly mode-dependent and is typically stronger for longitudinal-like excitations than for transverse modes. Our results demonstrate that self-doped carriers not only enable metallicity but also fundamentally reshape the stability, collective dynamics, and quasiparticle interactions of electronic crystals.

### Paper [98]
Title: Phase behavior of a two-dimensional suspension of active pentagons
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02176
Authors: R. Fern\'andez-Quevedo Garc\'ia, B. Wu-Zhang, C. M. Barriuso G., W. Lin, M. Ruiz-Garc\'ia, H. Xiao, C. Valeriani
Abstract: Applying insights acquired when studying idealized particle systems to real-world amorphous materials remains a significant challenge, primarily due to the complex interplay between particles' shape and interaction mechanisms. It is known that regular pentagons do not tessellate the two dimensional plane, making them the simplest regular polygons unable to form periodic tiling. This geometric constraint, combined with the complexity of interactions among non-spherical particles, may give rise to rich emergent behaviors, especially when dealing with self-propelled pentagons. Thus, geometry, interactions, and self-propulsion collectively lead to novel dynamical emerging phases. In this context, we investigate how activity and excluded-volume interactions affect the collective behavior of a two-dimensional suspension of active pentagon-shaped particles. Our results reveal that active pentagons not only undergo an arrested motility-induced phase separation (MIPS) but, under given conditions, they form traveling/rotating clusters exhibiting translational/chiral motion. Interestingly, both phases present an enhanced stability due to particles' activity, shape and excluded-volume interactions, forming an ice-ray lattice whose internal structure represents the densest packing fraction a two-dimensional pentagon suspension may achieve.

### Paper [99]
Title: Modeling Crystallization in Concentrated Suspensions of Compressible Microgels
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02184
Authors: Oreoluwa E. Alade, Alan R. Denton
Abstract: Soft colloidal particles, such as microgels, made of cross-linked polymer gels that can swell by absorbing a good solvent, have internal degrees of freedom that allow them to respond to external stimuli by changing size. Sensitive responses to variations in temperature and concentration have inspired many practical applications, e.g., to drug delivery, biosensing, filtration, and photonic crystals. At sufficiently high concentrations, mutual crowding can drive microgels to interpenetrate or deform by faceting. To explore the influence of particle softness and compressibility on thermodynamic phase stability, we develop a coarse-grained model in which spherical microgels interact via the Hertz elastic pair potential and can respond to crowding by deswelling and faceting or interpenetrating, as governed by the Flory-Rehner theory of polymer networks. In our model, faceting reduces the volume available for swelling, while interpenetration affects polymer solvent mixing entropy. By performing Monte Carlo simulations that incorporate novel trial changes in particle size and shape, we compute equilibrium swelling ratios and Lindemann ratios to determine the fluid-solid phase stability boundary over a range of particle softness, tuned by varying cross-link fraction. Predictions from our facet model are in qualitative agreement with experiments and molecular-scale simulations. In contrast to the phase behavior of incompressible Hertzian spheres, we find that suspensions of compressible microgels crystallize at significantly lower volume fractions when accounting for the free energy costs of faceting and interpenetration. Our results extend previous studies of microgel phase behavior and can help to interpret experiments.

### Paper [100]
Title: Thermodynamic Underpinnings Affecting Compressive and Tensile Plasticity in Refractory Compositionally Complex Alloys
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02202
Authors: Eric A. Lass, Carla Joyce C. Nocheseda, Christopher Tandoc, Yong-Jie Hu, Peter K. Liaw
Abstract: Refractory compositionally complex alloys (R-CCAs) show great promise as next generation structural materials for extreme conditions, but alloys with excellent high temperature mechanical properties most often exhibit virtually zero room temperature tensile ductility, hindering potential commercial application. Several semi-empirical metrics have been employed for predicting plasticity in R-CCAs, including valence electron concentration, Pugh ratio, Rice criteria, and the D-parameter. While many currently available ductility metrics correlate very well when compressive plasticity is considered, only the density functional theory (DFT) based D-parameter shows a strong correlation in tension. A new ductility metric is developed using CALculation of PHAse Diagrams (CALPHAD) thermodynamics, Gamma, with includes the free energy difference between the body-centered cubic (BCC) and hexagonal close-packed (HCP) phases, the interfacial free energy of a (110) BCC surface, and an electronegativity-corrected lattice distortion. The Gamma parameter exhibits better correlation with tensile ductility than D-parameter and performs equally well in compression. While this improvement is modest, a thermodynamically grounded ductility metric is clearly required to capture intrinsic ductility behavior. Extrinsic contributions to brittle behavior in R-CCAs, namely interstitial oxygen content, are also briefly discussed. Combining the Gamma ductility parameter with CALPHAD-calculated oxygen solubility is proposed as a first order attempt to capture both intrinsic and extrinsic contributions to ductile behavior in R-CCAs.

### Paper [101]
Title: Statistical mechanics of spontaneous behavior
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02220
Authors: Daniel Y. Takahashi, Ahmed El Hady
Abstract: Why do a few behaviors fill most of an animal's time? The usual answer is that these behaviors are started more often, because learning and reinforcement favor them. Here we show that how long a behavior lasts can, by itself, explain much of what is observed. We consider a system that runs until it meets a constraint, such as a wall, and then resets. The fraction of time it spends in each behavior then follows the Boltzmann law, and the partition function is the mean time between resets. The four laws of thermodynamics follow as theorems from four postulates, and critical points that act as bottlenecks become the most occupied states.
