In 47 multi-day recordings of fruit flies, the dominant behavior accounts for only $13\%$ of bout starts yet fills $49\%$ of the time, and its typical duration identifies the individual fly. These results lay the groundwork for a statistical mechanics of behavior.

### Paper [102]
Title: Ferroelectric Switching in x-cut MgO-doped Thin-Film Lithium Niobate: Implications for Periodic Poling and Domain-Wall Electronics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02233
Authors: Niels Staal, Michael R\"using, Tobias Babai-Hemati, Boris Koppitz, Nivedita Vishnukumar, Laura Bollmers, Noah Spiegelberg, Philipp Mues, Sebastian Lengeling, Tobias Schwabe, J. Christoph Scheytt, Christof Eigner, Lukas M. Eng, Laura Padberg, Christine Silberhorn
Abstract: Thin-film lithium niobate (TFLN) revives a mature ferroelectric as a wafer-scale platform for integrated photonics and, increasingly, for domain-wall electronics such as nonvolatile memories and memristors. Both applications rely on controlled ferroelectric switching, which is poorly understood in TFLN: periodic poling consistently requires electric fields above 30~kV/mm, far above the coercive fields of about 21~kV/mm and 4.5~kV/mm of bulk congruent and 5~mol\% MgO-doped lithium niobate, respectively. Here, we present systematic ferroelectric hysteresis measurements on x-cut, 5~mol\% MgO-doped TFLN with coplanar electrodes as used for periodic poling, combining the positive-up-negative-down (PUND) method with second-harmonic microscopy (SHM). We obtain a spontaneous polarization of $(0.72\pm0.02)$~C/m$^2$, consistent with bulk values, while the coercive field varies between 24 and 40~kV/mm with the voltage ramp rate and depends on the electrode geometry. We identify three contributions to the high coercive fields: (i) fast voltage ramps, (ii) finger electrodes, and (iii) the coplanar surface-electrode geometry. Together with bulk data, the coercive field follows a single power law of the ramp rate over five orders of magnitude, showing that the high fields are dominated by switching dynamics rather than by intrinsic thin-film properties. Moreover, the apparent internal field depends on the electrode geometry, and reverse switching leaves buried, electrically isolated domains. These results provide guidelines for scalable, fab-compatible periodic poling---one of the principal bottlenecks of TFLN device integration---and for the write and erase operation of TFLN domain-wall devices.

### Paper [103]
Title: Band Alignment of Grafted versus Directly Bonded Si/Ge Heterostructures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02259
Authors: Pargam Vashishtha, Yang Liu, Tsung-Han Tsai, Yi Lu, Samuel A. Haessly, Chenyu Wang, Nanditha M. Dissanayake,  Edward, K. Huang, Zhenqiang Ma
Abstract: The electronic properties of semiconductor heterostructures are influenced by the local bonding and electrostatic environment at the heterointerface. Here, we investigate how the interface-formation mechanism modifies the band alignment of Si/Ge heterostructures by directly comparing Al2O3-mediated semiconductor grafting with direct bonding. Interface-sensitive X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy combined with Kraut analysis reveals type-I band alignment for both structures, but with different band offsets: the valence-band offset increases from ~0.08 eV for directly bonded Si/Ge to ~0.21 eV for grafted Si/Al2O3/Ge, accompanied by a corresponding reduction in the conduction-band offset. Raman spectroscopy further distinguishes the two interfacial configurations. The different core-level shifts and band offsets indicate that the electronic structure is sensitive to the interfacial bonding and electrostatic environment introduced by the two integration schemes. These results demonstrate that, even for an identical Si/Ge material pair, the interface-formation pathway provides an additional degree of freedom for controlling heterojunction band alignment, offering a route toward interface-engineered electronic and optoelectronic devices.

### Paper [104]
Title: Topological Acoustic Metamaterials with Synthetic Magnetism: Observation of Hofstadter Spectrum and Topological Edge States
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02262
Authors: Elena Rybalova, Jackson Saunders, Curtis Rasmussen, Nicholas H. Patino, Emil Prodan, Massimo Ruzzene, Camelia Prodan
Abstract: Architected materials are increasingly used to implement topological dynamics, yet realizing the gauge fields and symmetries that protect the topology with purely passive elements remains the central practical challenge. Here we turn a previously proposed theoretical strategy into an integrated platform that accepts a theoretical model as input and returns a passive acoustic metamaterial with identical dynamics. We demonstrate it experimentally on the Hofstadter model at $\theta = 2\pi/3$, fabricating a spin-Chern insulator whose two sectors carry Chern numbers $C_s = \pm 1$, and observe the predicted helical edge states.

### Paper [105]
Title: Lattice-Induced Splitting, Connectivity, and Topology of Anyon Bands
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02268
Authors: Siddhartha Sarkar, Xiaohan Wan
Abstract: Fractional Chern insulators realize fractional quantum Hall states without external magnetic fields, while their underlying lattice periodicity allows otherwise dispersionless anyons to acquire finite bandwidth. Here we show that flux--lattice commensurability can also give rise to multiple anyon bands with nontrivial topology and symmetry-protected connectivity. In multiband vortexable systems, commensurability between the lattice periodicity and the effective magnetic unit cell of fractionally charged quasiholes leads to the folding of a quasihole Landau level into multiple bands. Interactions lift their degeneracy, producing dispersive bands with Chern numbers distinct from that of the parent quasihole Landau level. We further demonstrate that these Chern numbers can be redistributed through Dirac crossings under continuous deformations that preserve exact electronic flatness, while crystalline symmetries can enforce protected band touchings. Our results establish that fractionalized excitations can exhibit the rich band topology familiar from electronic Bloch bands, opening possibilities for nontrivial anyon dynamics beyond conventional Landau-level physics.

### Paper [106]
Title: Chirality-Induced Angular-Momentum Pumping by Moving Charges
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02276
Authors: Jun-ichiro Kishine, Ron Naaman
Abstract: Chirality-induced spin selectivity is established even in light elements at room temperature; dissipation and electron correlations are implicated, here combined in one mechanism. Injected electron wave packets coherently radiate chiral phonons; electronic absorption competing with phonon decay sets the fraction electrons receive and its temperature dependence. Spin-orbit coupling itself is not renormalized; it becomes effectively large because both the orbital and spin moments it couples are enhanced by Coulomb correlations.

### Paper [107]
Title: Defect Chemistry of Hafnia-Based Ferroelectrics under Oxide-Electrode Constraints
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02317
Authors: Sehyeok Jeon, Aloysius Soon, Yongjin Shin
Abstract: HfO$_2$ heterostructures with La$_{0.67}$Sr$_{0.33}$MnO$_3$ (LSMO) epitaxial electrodes have attracted interest as ferroelectric systems with electrode-dependent defect chemistry. However, the formation and stability of defects under LSMO-constrained chemical conditions remain poorly understood. In this work, we use density functional theory calculations to systematically investigate oxygen vacancies and La-, Sr-, and Mn-related interstitial and substitutional defects in HfO$_2$ under chemical environments constrained by the LSMO electrode. We find that LSMO stability substantially narrows the accessible O chemical potential range relative to bulk HfO$_2$, altering the predicted defect formation energies. While La and Sr substitutional defects are governed primarily by preferred ionic valence states, oxygen vacancies and Mn substitutional defects exhibit charge states sensitive to the local atomic environment. In addition, defect-complex calculations in orthorhombic HfO$_2$ reveal negative binding energies, indicating favorable defect association. The La- and Mn-related defect complexes exhibit thermodynamic competition, with their relative stability depending on the Fermi level and oxygen chemical potential. Lastly, extending the analysis to Hf$_{0.5}$Zr$_{0.5}$O$_2$ and ZrO$_2$ reveals common oxygen-vacancy trends, while the stability of LSMO-derived defects depends more strongly on the host material and crystal phase. These results provide a thermodynamic basis for understanding how electrode-constrained chemical environments and host structures influence defect formation, charge compensation, and defect-complex stability in HfO$_2$-based materials.

### Paper [108]
Title: Amplification of valley polarization in an hBN-separated MoSe2-WSe2 heterostructure
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02351
Authors: Fateme Mahdikhany, Jacob Cutshall, Daniel Shanks, Garett Reichenbach, Davoud Adinehloo, Michael R. Koehler, David G. Mandrus, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Vasili Perebeinos, Brian J. LeRoy, John R. Schaibley
Abstract: Transition metal dichalcogenide heterostructures host long-lived interlayer excitons in the +K and -K valleys that can encode long-lived valley polarization. We investigated the low-temperature valley polarization dynamics in an MoSe2-bilayer hBN-WSe2 heterostructure. The bilayer hBN spacer results in an interlayer exciton lifetime exceeding 2 {\mu}s and an anomalous valley polarization amplification effect. Our theoretical model shows that this amplification results from the long exciton lifetime and exciton-exciton interactions. This work sets the stage for long-lived interlayer excitons to be used in nonlinear valleytronic transistors and amplifiers.

### Paper [109]
Title: Imaging the Current Distribution in Superconducting Transition-Edge Sensors with Scanning SQUID Microscopy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02352
Authors: Samantha Walker, Austin Kaczmarek, Douglas A. Bennett, Jason E. Austermann, W. Bertrand Doriese, Shannon M. Duff, Johannes Hubmayr, Kelsey M. Morgan, Daniel R. Schmidt, Daniel S. Swetz, Joel N. Ullom, Joel Weber, Michael D. Niemack, Katja C. Nowack
Abstract: Transition-edge sensors (TESs) exploit the resistive transition of a superconductor to detect minute changes in temperature. While significant progress has been made in understanding the temperature- and current-dependent resistive transition in these devices, important aspects of TES behavior under bias remain unresolved. In particular, existing TES models largely neglect spatial variations in the superconducting state and current distribution arising from device geometry and proximity effects associated with the superconducting leads and normal metal features. Using scanning superconducting quantum interference device (SQUID) microscopy (SSM), we directly image local superconducting properties and transport current in two molybdenum-gold (MoAu) X-ray TES designs. We measure the local diamagnetic response to identify superconducting regions in devices voltage-biased within the transition and the Oersted field generated by the transport current to reconstruct the corresponding current distribution. We observe bias-dependent reconfigurations of the current distribution that track current-induced changes in superconducting regions, highlighting the importance of mesoscale spatial variations for TES device physics, operation, and design.

### Paper [110]
Title: Fermi surface and field-induced Lifshitz transition in the antiferromagnet NbMnP exhibiting an anomalous Hall effect
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02360
Authors: Taichi Terashima, Yuya Hattori, David Graf, Yuki Arai, Hitoshi Sugawara, Hisashi Kotegawa, Ibuki Terada, Michi-To Suzuki
Abstract: We performed Shubnikov--de Haas (SdH) oscillation measurements on the $\mathbf{Q}=0$ antiferromagnet NbMnP, which exhibits a large anomalous Hall effect. The SdH frequencies observed in the low-field region up to 17.5~T are well described by first-principles band-structure calculations based on the $B_{3g}+B_{2u}$ noncollinear magnetic structure determined by neutron diffraction. The effective masses are enhanced by approximately a factor of two relative to the calculated band masses. High-field measurements up to 41.5~T reveal a Lifshitz transition accompanied by a pronounced evolution of the SdH frequency spectrum. These results confirm that first-principles calculations provide a reliable tool for the development of functional antiferromagnetic materials, while also demonstrating that field-induced changes in the Fermi surface itself need to be taken into account in understanding their high-field magnetotransport properties.

### Paper [111]
Title: Full edge control of bulk energy gap in armchair graphene nanoribbons
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02367
Authors: Lin Wang, Thomas Luu, Ulf-G. Mei{\ss}ner
Abstract: In solid-state physics, the ability to open and tune an electronic energy gap provides a direct route to controlling charge transport and accessing distinct electronic phases. In solids with physical boundaries, a bulk energy gap formed exclusively by extended bulk states is conventionally expected to be governed primarily by the interior bulk region, with only a minor contribution from the boundaries. Here we show that, in stark contrast to this conventional expectation, the bulk gap of armchair graphene nanoribbons in the $N_w=3p+2$ family with $p\in\mathbb{Z}\ge 0$ can be fully controlled through the hoppings solely at the boundaries. This full boundary control originates from an exact cancellation between the gap contributions of long-range hoppings along and across the ribbon in the bulk region. It persists in the presence of Hubbard interactions and holds irrespective of ribbon width in this family. Our findings establish boundary dynamics as an unexpectedly powerful means of manipulating bulk electronic properties, enabling width-preserving engineering of one-dimensional (1D) band structures and topological phases, with potential applications in qubits, artificial quantum spin chains, topological quantum dots and other quantum devices.

### Paper [112]
Title: The effect of local stacking fault fluctuations on dislocation glide and the interplay with solid solution strengthening in Al-Mg alloys
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02386
Authors: Hyunsoo Lee, Xin Liu, Liam Myhill, Yang Li, David Rodney, Pierre-Antoine Geslin, Nikhil Chandra Admal, Yinan Cui, Giacomo Po, Enrique Martinez
Abstract: Concentrated alloys are characterized by a random arrangement of solute atoms of different sizes, which generates spatially fluctuating displacement and stress fields with non-trivial correlations. This chemical disorder also induces local fluctuations in stacking-fault (SF) energies, giving rise to fluctuating SF forces acting on the partial dislocations bounding the SF. In this work, we investigate the characteristics of the SF noise, including spatial correlations and the impact of such SF energy fluctuations on dislocation glide. Specifically, the gliding behavior of screw, edge, and mixed dislocations in a model concentrated Al-Mg random alloy with varying Mg content is analyzed. The interplay between SF energy fluctuations and solid-solution noise is also discussed. We show that SF forces are spatially correlated, in close analogy with long-range elastic stresses, and exhibit a robust negative correlation along the slip direction. We identify the physical origin of these correlations and demonstrate that they significantly contribute to the critical resolved shear stress for dislocations to glide. In contrast, correlations in other directions are weaker but can still be non-negligible, depending on the underlying interatomic interactions.

### Paper [113]
Title: Machine-Learned Interatomic Potential for the Mo-S-Al-O System
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02403
Authors: Emir Bilgili, Nicholas Taormina, Simon R. Phillpot, Youping Chen
Abstract: We develop a machine-learned neuroevolution potential (NEP) for the Mo-S-Al-O system, targeting MoS$_2$ heteroepitaxy on c-plane sapphire, $\alpha$-Al$_2$O$_3$(0001). The potential is trained on 63,611 PBE+D3 structures distilled from about 1.1 million curated density-functional-theory (DFT) configurations, using an energy-difference loss function that directly targets interfacial and orientation-dependent energies. On held-out data, the energy and force errors are 29 meV/atom and 0.23 eV/$\mathring{A}$. Validated against DFT on a suite of physical properties, the NEP reproduces the lattice constants within 0.3 %, the elastic constants within 17 %, the phonon dispersions with mean deviations below 2.5%, and the surface energies within 1.3 %. At the MoS$_2$/sapphire interface, it reproduces the adhesion energy minimum within 0.8 %, the interfacial energy within 2.2 % and the most stable orientation of the monolayer. It preserves the DFT-predicted energetic ordering of edges, point defects (Spearman $\rho$ = 0.995) and mirror-twin boundaries ($\rho$ = 1.0), and reproduces tilt grain-boundary energies within 8 % ($\rho$ = 0.98) and stacking-fault energy barriers within 14 %. The resulting potential enables future studies of the atomic-scale mechanisms of defect formation in two-dimensional heteroepitaxy.

### Paper [114]
Title: Gate-tunable superconductor-semiconductor coupling and its impact on zero-energy modes in PbTe-Pb nanowires
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02411
Authors: Ruidong Li, Shuai Yang, Wenyu Song, Zeyu Yan, Yuhao Wang, Zehao Yu, Li Chen, Zixun Xia, Zonglin Li, Shan Zhang, Jiaye Xu, Lining Yang, Weizhao Wang, Xiao Feng, Tiantian Wang, Yunyi Zang, Lin Li, Runan Shang, Dong E. Liu, Qi-Kun Xue, Ke He, Hao Zhang
Abstract: Superconductor-semiconductor coupling in hybrid nanowires plays a pivotal role in Majorana devices. Here, we demonstrate this tunable coupling in PbTe-Pb nanowires and its impact on the induced gap and zero-bias peaks (ZBPs). In the strong coupling regime, the device reveals a clean superconducting gap, where robust and non-robust ZBPs are observed in distinct magnetic field ranges. Conversely, increasing the gate voltage drives the system toward the weak coupling regime, where the gap becomes narrow and soft. Consequently, robust ZBPs disappear while non-robust ones persist. Our results establish a direct link between tunable coupling and robust ZBPs, providing guidelines on engineering Majorana zero modes and topological qubits.

### Paper [115]
Title: Experimental realization of loss-induced quadrupole topology from a gapped trivial insulator in RLC circuits
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02435
Authors: Wenxue He, Chen Wang, Fan Yang, Hechen Ren
Abstract: Non-Hermitian topological transitions offer a route to controlling topology through dissipation, but their experimental signatures depend on how loss modifies the system's response. Here, we experimentally realize loss-induced quadrupole topology in a square-octagon RLC circuit whose ideal Hermitian limit is a gapped trivial insulator. Patterned grounding resistors introduce on-site loss while the conservative couplings remain fixed. Band spectra and biorthogonal nested Wilson loops identify a loss-driven topological transition accompanied by closure and reopening of the real line gap. Impedance spectroscopy and spatial maps reveal corner-localized responses consistent with the predicted quadrupole phase. Comparing two finite-loss configurations, we observe enhanced corner contrast at stronger patterned loss within the same predicted phase. These results connect dissipation-driven topology to observable boundary responses in RLC circuits.

### Paper [116]
Title: Moir\'e reconstruction at a twisted SrTiO3 interface
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02464
Authors: Pengcheng Fei, Meiyu Wang, Suzhen Liu, Zhongwei Zhang, Yang Wu, Bo Hao, Hanhao Zhang, Dingcheng Ma, Shijie Yang, Weiye Nie, Jiacheng Wang, Jibo Xu, Geliang Shen, Junjie Wang, Chi Ding, Huifeng Chen, Sheng Xu, Xinrui Yang, Lu Han, Haocheng Quan, Ling-Ling Ma, Zaiyao Fei, Liqi Zhou, Songhua Cai, Zhengbin Gu, Yu Deng, Jian Sun, Di Wu, Yuefeng Nie
Abstract: Twisted oxide double layers have emerged as a promising platform for exploring moir\'e physics, including polar topologies and emergent electronic phases. However, the intrinsic complexity of oxide surfaces, such as contamination, roughness, and the presence of two distinct terminations (AO and BO2), can complicate the identification of buried interfacial structures. Here, using atomically flat SrTiO3 membranes with a predominantly SrO-TiO2 interfacial configuration, we directly visualize a moir\'e-periodic structural reconstruction at the buried interface that is distinct from the geometric moir\'e of rigid stacking. The reconstructed interface hosts head-to-head-like polar distortions, moir\'e-modulated Ti electronic structure, and enhanced microwave admittance in otherwise paraelectric and insulating SrTiO3. Our work demonstrates that, with a well-defined termination, moir\'e reconstruction could serve as a route to emergent electrical behavior in twisted perovskite oxides.

### Paper [117]
Title: Observation of Superconductivity in a Cu-Rich High-Entropy Alloy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02469
Authors: Arumugam Sonachalam
Abstract: A Cu-rich multielement alloy containing Zn, Sn, Ag, and Au was synthesized and studied to explore its structural and physical properties. The alloy was prepared by vacuum-sealed melting of high-purity elements followed by rapid quenching in cold water. Powder X-ray diffraction confirms that the alloy crystallizes in a face-centered cubic (fcc) Cu-type structure. Field-emission scanning electron microscopy and energy-dispersive X-ray spectroscopy (EDS) mapping show uniform distribution of all constituent elements. Magnetic measurements indicate the appearance of a superconducting transition at low temperature, while electrical resistivity remains metallic without reaching zero resistance. This difference between magnetic and transport behaviour suggests that the superconducting state may be limited to local or partial regions within the sample. These findings indicate that the Cu-rich multielement alloy exhibits unusual low-temperature properties and may represent a new class of compositionally complex Cu-based materials with superconducting characteristics.

### Paper [118]
Title: Electrically tunable moir\'e hybridization in scalable twist-with-a-spacer structures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02471
Authors: Ian Babich, Daria Belotcerkovtceva, Benjamin Fuller, Ilya Begichev, Nansi Zhou, Alex Boschi, Vaidotas Miseikis, Gianluigi Baiardi, Andrei Kudriashov, Konstantin S. Novoselov, Stiven Forti, Antonio Rossi, Fabio Beltram, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Sergio Pezzini, Camilla Coletti, Vladimir Falko, Alexey I. Berdyugin
Abstract: Moir\'e superlattices emerge from the interplay between interlayer hybridization and long-wavelength periodicity, both set by the twist angle between layers in direct contact. Here we show that moir\'e minibands and pronounced Brown-Zak oscillations persist even when twisted graphene layers are separated by an atomically thin spacer, such as a misaligned graphene monolayer. This twist-with-a-spacer architecture reduces interlayer coupling, such that moir\'e hybridization can be tuned with a modest displacement field without changing moir\'e periodicity, allowing us to disentangle the roles of those parameters in miniband formation. Importantly, the spacer suppresses atomic relaxation, enabling highly uniform, rigid superlattices at twist angles down to ~0.1 degree. This robustness allows twist-with-a-spacer structures to be grown directly by scalable chemical vapour deposition (CVD): devices fabricated from CVD-grown structures exhibit the same moir\'e physics phenomena as those assembled manually. Our work provides insights into the physics of moir\'e bandstructure formation and opens a route towards scalable moir\'e systems.

### Paper [119]
Title: Galilean electrodynamics fluctuation in a superfluid: from general coordinate invariance to Tkachenko mode
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02482
Authors: Neng-Chun Chiu, Tzu-Ken Shen, Siau Ihan
Abstract: We explore coupling the Galilean electrodynamics (GED) to an s-wave superfluid. Governed by the general coordinate invariance, the proposed effective Lagrangian hosts an unconventional superfluid whose mass is shifted by the GED gauge multiplet. We compute its electromagnetic and gravitational responses in the linear regime. In the long-wavelength limit, we discover a vanishing pressure response, and remarkably the collective mode assumes a quadratic dispersion relation reminiscent of the Tkachenko mode in an oscillating vortex lattice in (2+1) dimensions. We further demonstrate that the soft sector of the vortex lattice model is a GED in disguise, and illustrate the relation between the gauge multiplet and the Lifshitz scalar in the lowest-Landau-level regime of the vortex lattice.

### Paper [120]
Title: For any static interaction the self-energy and vertex corrections to the electron-gas polarizability combine exactly into one integrand free of the Coulomb singularity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02514
Authors: Carl A. Kukkonen
Abstract: The first-order corrections to the polarizability of the uniform electron gas are a self-energy insertion and a vertex correction. For the Coulomb interaction the coefficient of q^2 in each diverges logarithmically at small wave vector, and the two divergences have opposite signs and cancel in their sum. This Note shows that for any interaction that depends only on the momentum it transfers, the two integrands combine exactly into one before any integration is done. At zero temperature the combined integrand has no Coulomb singularity and no chemical-potential counterterm. For screened interactions, which must be integrated numerically, it should be computed in place of the two diagrams. The Note gives the derivation and its physical reason, the one term added at finite temperature, and checks for bare, Yukawa and screened interactions. With a frequency-dependent interaction the combination is no longer exact.

### Paper [121]
Title: Topological origin of the Gutenberg-Richter law: Dynamic decoupling of fault cascades and energy dissipation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02541
Authors: Bin Chen, Mingze Zhao, Puyu Cao
Abstract: The Gutenberg-Richter (G-R) law is seismology's most robust empirical regularity, yet a first-principles derivation of its slope-the b-value-has remained elusive. Here, we present an analytical theory based on a finite hierarchical fault network composed of velocity-weakening asperities separated by velocity-strengthening creeping zones. By coupling network topology with fracture mechanics, we derive a generalized closed-form expression of b-value,$\(b=\frac{3}{3+2\gamma}\frac{\log_{10}k}{\log_{10}q}\)$, where k is the threshold branching ratio and q is the area amplification factor. Crucially, we reveal a dynamic decoupling between k and q governed by energy dissipation. The empirical universality of b=1 naturally emerges as the fault network self-organizes into a critical attractor (k=q) that maximizes two-dimensional spatial filling. Furthermore, grounded in Rate-and-State Friction rheology, we demonstrate that foreshock acceleration (inverse-Omori) and aftershock decay (Omori-Utsu) are dual manifestations of the similar hierarchical cascade. In the mean-field limit, these temporal exponents are decoupled from static fracture energy, revealing a profound orthogonality between time-dependent friction and static energy release. By unifying the G-R law, Bath's law, and the foreshock-aftershock asymmetry, this framework might provide a physics-based pathway for operational earthquake forecasting.

### Paper [122]
Title: Magnetically and electrically tunable nonlinear Hall effect in Tt-symmetric antiferromagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02567
Authors: Yueyue Ning, Yongliang Song, Yanrui Ding, Zhichao Zhou, Xiao Li
Abstract: Tt-symmetric antiferromagnets can exhibit multiferroicity with coexisting ferroelectricity and magnetic order, and thus host multiple order-parameter configurations. However, how to electrically read out these configurations is an issue meriting further investigation. Here, by combining tight-binding and first-principles calculations, we propose electrically and magnetically tunable nonlinear Hall transport in ferroelectric Tt-symmetric antiferromagnets. Model calculations show that ferroelectric switching can reverse the sign of the Berry curvature dipole (BCD), whereas the Neel vector rotation modulates its magnitude, allowing both ferroelectric and magnetic order to be reflected in the nonlinear Hall signal. First-principles calculations of the VOCl2 monolayer confirm these behaviors and predict a sizable BCD that gives rise to a potentially detectable Hall voltage. Our findings open up opportunities to integrate electrical and magnetic modulations of the nonlinear Hall effect within a single material, with potential applications in multi-state information encoding and readout in antiferromagnetic spintronics.

### Paper [123]
Title: Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584
Authors: Adam M. Krajewski, Ricardo Amaral, Jonathan W. Siegel, Luke A. Myers, Rushi Gong, Shun-Li Shang, Zi-Kui Liu
Abstract: Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.

### Paper [124]
Title: Nonadiabatic molecular dynamics simulation with external field
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02606
Authors: Chengji Li, Fan Zheng
Abstract: Nonadiabatic molecular dynamics simulations with classical path approximation (CPA-NAMD) cannot simulate the carrier dynamics under an external field. We develop CPA-NAMD-extF, a first-principles density-matrix framework for calculating field-driven electronic responses along precomputed finite-temperature ionic trajectories. The method combines explicit external-field coupling with self-consistent updates of electronic density while retaining the computational efficiency of the classical-path approximation. Benchmarks against regular TDDFT methods show close agreement for time-dependent dipoles, finite-field currents, and linear optical responses. Applications to the linear absorption spectrum of crystalline and amorphous SiO$_2$ demonstrate its capability to large and structurally complex systems. This method is also applied to the 2D hybrid perovskite $(\mathrm{BA})_2\mathrm{CsPb}_2\mathrm{Br}_7$. Finite-temperature calculations show that the second-order opto-current response is strongly affected by structural fluctuations. Real-space DC current density further reveals pronounced Br-centered vortex currents, and its microscopic origin is investigated.

### Paper [125]
Title: Collective dipole effects amplify field-driven ion transport in amorphous Li$_3$PO$_4$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02617
Authors: Haruka Doyu, Anh Khoa Augustin Lu, Satoshi Watanabe
Abstract: Amorphous solid electrolytes often outperform their crystalline counterparts, but how disorder shapes ionic transport under strong electric fields is poorly understood. We use molecular dynamics with a machine-learned interatomic potential and a machine-learned Born-effective-charge model to measure Li mobility in amorphous Li$_3$PO$_4$ and in a crystal with Li vacancies, from the linear to the strongly nonlinear regime. The amorphous phase shows a much stronger field enhancement, and we trace this to a collective dipole effect. The migrating ion carries the same charge in both phases. Alone it would not make the amorphous phase respond more strongly. What makes the difference is the environment. In the amorphous network the surrounding atoms move with the hop. Their motion makes the work the field performs vary widely between hops. Since hopping rates grow exponentially with the work, the most strongly assisted hops can dominate the drift. Disorder thus enhances field-driven transport through the environment rather than the carrier itself, a mechanism that may operate in disordered ion conductors in general.

### Paper [126]
Title: Phonon Angular Momentum Multipoles and Nonlinear Thermal Responses
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02619
Authors: Xun-Jiang Luo, Mingliang Tian
Abstract: Chiral phonons carry angular momentum and couple lattice dynamics to magnetic and transport phenomena. In magnets, however, a systematic classification of momentum-space phonon angular momentum (PAM) textures and experimental signatures of the resulting thermal responses remain elusive. Here we classify the PAM textures for all 122 magnetic point groups (MPGs) using two equivalent approaches: directly solving the linear symmetry constraints on the multipole expansion coefficients and constructing the allowed textures from group-representation basis functions. Both methods yield the same fivefold classification according to the leading nonzero order: $s$-wave (monopole), $p$-wave (dipole), $d$-wave (quadrupole), $f$-wave (octupole), and $g$-wave (hexadecapole). We further show that a temperature gradient generates a nonequilibrium PAM density whose $n$th-order term in $\nabla T$ contains an energy-weighted PAM $2^n$-pole tensor and a kinematic remainder, thereby linking the response order and partial-wave channel through exact selection rules. Because the Berry curvature obeys the same symmetry constraints, this taxonomy is in one-to-one correspondence with Berry-curvature multipoles and their nonlinear Hall responses. We propose a cantilever torque measurement whose allowed angular harmonics are fixed by the MPG, with period $2\pi/n$ for a pure $n$th harmonic, providing a direct probe of the partial-wave content of PAM in both nonmagnetic and magnetic materials.

### Paper [127]
Title: Strain-tunable electronic transport in disordered borophene nanoribbons with line defects
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02646
Authors: Pei-Jia Hu, Jia-Le Yu, Tie-Feng Fang, Ai-Min Guo, Qing-Feng Sun
Abstract: Resonant tunneling intrinsically exists in disordered borophene nanoribbons (BNRs) with randomly distributed line defects (LDs), providing a versatile platform for transport modulation. Here, we theoretically investigate the effects of in-plane mechanical strain on the electronic transport properties of disordered BNRs coupled to metallic electrodes. Our results demonstrate that the resonant peaks in disordered BNRs of different widths remain robust against variations in electrode parameters and can be efficiently modulated via mechanical strain. Specifically, uniaxial strain significantly modulates the energies, widths, and amplitudes of these resonant peaks, and can even suppress the peaks selectively and induce new ones. In particular, the response of the resonant energies to the applied strain exhibits remarkable anisotropy, where strains along the longitudinal and transverse directions induce opposite energy shifts. Moreover, identical resonant peaks recurring in different nanoribbon widths respond uniformly to applied strain. Biaxial strain enables highly efficient conductance switching across multiple distinct levels, with the modulation frequency increasing for wider nanoribbons. Our findings establish a promising defect-strain-engineering strategy for manipulating electronic transport in borophene and other gapless materials.

### Paper [128]
Title: Competing interaction driven broad magnetic entropy distribution in triangular net compounds \text{Ba$_3$RE(BO$_3$)$_3$}, RE= Dy and Ho
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02703
Authors: Krishna Nand Prajapati, Prabuddha Kant Mishra, Shashi B Singh
Abstract: Triangular magnetic lattices have attracted significant attention as a model platform for exploring frustrated magnetism and quantum spin liquids, particularly in rare-earth-based systems. These materials also hold considerable promise for cryogenic magnetic refrigeration. In this work, we investigate the magnetic and magnetocaloric properties of the rare-earth (RE) based compounds $\text{Ba}_3\text{RE}(\text{BO}_3)_3$ ($\text{RE} = \text{Dy}$ and $\text{Ho}$). Polycrystalline samples synthesized via a solid-state reaction method crystallize in a hexagonal structure with the space group $P6_3mc$, featuring triangular nets separated by approximately $8.8~\text{\AA}$ along the $c$-axis. Detailed magnetic investigation reveals a deviation from pure paramagnetic behavior at low temperatures ($T < 20\text{ K}$). This is further corroborated by isothermal magnetization measurements, which exhibit non-overlapping $M$ vs. $H/T$ plots and reduced magnetization moments, suggesting significant crystal electric field (CEF) splitting of localized $f$-states. For an applied magnetic field change of $70\text{ kOe}$, the maximum entropy change (Relative Cooling Power) reaches $4.2\text{ J kg}^{-1}\text{ K}^{-1}$ ($204\text{ J kg}^{-1}$) and $7.3\text{ J kg}^{-1}\text{ K}^{-1}$ ($230\text{ J kg}^{-1}$) for the Dy- and Ho-based compounds, respectively. Overall, the positive slopes of Arrott plots, the universal scaling behaviour of $\Delta S_{\text{m}}/\Delta S_{\text{m}}^{\text{max}}$ versus reduced temperature ($t$), and the field exponent $c$ of the RCP versus $H$ curves indicate a second-order nature of entropy distribution, making them promising candidates for magnetocalorics in the millikelvin range.

### Paper [129]
Title: Universal model of the growth of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors on polar surfaces: pyramids and glue atoms. VI. Surface diffusion on a nonmetal-terminated surface
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02723
Authors: Pawel Strak, Stanis{\l}aw Krukowski
Abstract: Ab initio calculations were used to simulate surface diffusion of adsorbates on the nonmetal-terminated surface of tetrahedral semiconductors: GaN, GaP, GaAs and GaSb. These surfaces are wurtzite gallium nitride, i.e. GaN(000-1) surface, zinc blende gallium phosphide, i.e. GaP(-1-1-1) gallium arsenide, GaAs (-1-1-1) and gallium antimonide GaSb (-1-1-1) surfaces. The mixed coverage consists of Ga adatoms in the first layer with a nonmetal atom attached above them forming a triangular-based pyramid. The pyramidal structures are stable during surface jumps involving rotations around one of the basal Ga adatoms. Thus, the diffusion proceeds via rotation (pivot) of the pair of the other basal Ga adatoms around an immobile Ga adatom moving them into new, originally empty sites. Using an energy-path determination procedure, the diffusion barriers were obtained as the difference between the initial site energy and the activated complex state energy. The detachment energy of the NM adatom was obtained assuming a final state in atomic and molecular form. The energy barrier for detachment to the atomic state is high for all atoms, on the order of 5 eV. For the molecular state these barriers are different: smaller for nitrogen, on the order of 1 eV, which is related to the high dissociation energy of the N2 molecule. For P and As the barrier is above 2 eV. For Sb it was not determined. Accordingly, the surface diffusion length is macroscopically large, indicating that all NM adatoms arrive at the steps.

### Paper [130]
Title: Universal model of the growth of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors on polar surfaces: pyramids and glue atoms. VII. Step kinetics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02735
Authors: Pawel Strak, Stanis{\l}aw Krukowski
Abstract: Ab initio calculations were used to simulate the kinetics of attachment of new segments of the crystalline lattice (pyramids) at steps on the nonmetal-terminated surface of tetrahedral semiconductors: GaN, GaP, GaAs and GaSb. These surfaces are wurtzite gallium nitride, i.e. GaN(000-1) surface, zinc blende gallium phosphide, i.e. GaP(-1-1-1) gallium arsenide, GaAs(-1-1-1) and gallium antimonide GaSb(-1-1-1) surfaces. Diffusion species are gallium adatoms at high coverage or gallium adatoms connected with nonmetal atom creating stable pyramids. These pyramids arrive at the steps, and cannot be attached be directly because the Ga adatom at the pyramid base cannot be attached directly. Therefore they are incorporated into the step by additional nonmetal atoms glue atoms arriving from the vapor or detached from other segments of the surface terraces.

### Paper [131]
Title: Quantized pumping of boundary Hilbert space in chiral Floquet phases
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02785
Authors: Ryota Nakai, Taozhi Guo, Shinsei Ryu
Abstract: Chiral Floquet phases support quantized chiral flow along their boundaries even when the bulk is fully localized. In this Letter, we establish a complementary phenomenon, which we call Hilbert-space pumping: under a boundary condition twisted by time translation, a part of the boundary Hilbert space, of quantized dimension, is transferred between the opposite boundaries. The twist is the temporal analogue of Laughlin's flux insertion in the quantum Hall effect. For noninteracting fermions, the number of transferred boundary modes is determined by the winding number, as we show analytically for the boundary dynamics and numerically for the anomalous Floquet Anderson insulator. For interacting systems whose boundary dynamics is a quantum cellular automaton, the transferred dimension is set by the Gross-Nesme-Vogts-Werner (GNVW) index: the twisted boundary Floquet operator is the Floquet operator on a tilted time slice, whose Hilbert-space dimension is multiplied by the index for each period of twist. We derive this relation for matrix product unitaries and test it numerically in random unitary circuits. Applied to the matrix product operators of non-invertible symmetries, the same twisted rank returns the quantum dimension of the symmetry operator in addition to its flow index.

### Paper [132]
Title: High-Entropy A-Site Disorder Enhances Oxygen Evolution Reaction Activity and Stability in Nickelate Thin Films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02796
Authors: Min-Ju Choi, Le Wang, Krishna Prasad Koirala, Hyoju Park, Mark E. Bowden, Elizabeth Helen Krenkel, Aiping Chen, Jueli Shi, Conan Weiland, Iradwikanari Waluyo, Adrian Hunt, Peter V. Sushko, Yingge Du
Abstract: Rare-earth nickelates are among the most active oxide electrocatalysts for the oxygen evolution reaction (OER), owing to their tunable Ni-O covalency, near-unity eg occupancy, and sensitivity to A-site chemistry, oxygen stoichiometry, and epitaxial strain. Here, we use epitaxial A-site high-entropy (La0.2Pr0.2Nd0.2Sm0.2Eu0.2)NiO3 thin films to reveal how epitaxial strain influences defect formation, Ni valence, electronic structure, and OER activity in a chemically disordered rare-earth (RE) sublattice. Tensile strain promotes Ruddlesden-Popper fault formation, interrupts the corner-sharing NiO6 network, and lowers the average Ni oxidation state. Although the occupied density of states near the Fermi level remains largely unchanged, O K-edge X-ray absorption spectroscopy reveals increased low-energy unoccupied states under tensile strain. First-principles calculations suggest that tensile strain shifts RE 4f-derived states toward the Fermi level, enhancing RE 4f-O 2p-Ni 3d hybridization. These coupled structural and electronic changes lead to earlier OER onset, stronger pre-catalytic Ni redox features, and higher activity than compressively strained films and representative single-A-site nickelates. The high-entropy nickelate also shows enhanced resistance to OER-induced structural degradation compared with LaNiO3. These results suggest that A-site configurational disorder and epitaxial strain can jointly tune defect formation and rare-earth-derived electronic states, providing a route for improving perovskite oxide electrocatalysts.

### Paper [133]
Title: Coulomb Blockade in a One-Dimensional Array of Strong Josephson Junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02798
Authors: Bayan Karimi, Ze-Yan Chen, Jukka P. Pekola, T. Yamamoto, M. Houzet, L. I. Glazman
Abstract: We measure current across a voltage-biased, long array of Josephson junctions. Current-voltage characteristics do not reveal any critical current and instead display the presence of threshold voltage and finite activation energy, consistent with the Coulomb blockade. However, the respective energy scale is well below the value prescribed by a single-island geometric capacitances. We explain it by strong quantum fluctuations of charge associated with a large value of Josephson energy, $E_J\gg E_C$, where $E_C$ is the charging energy associated with the inter-island capacitance. Comparison between the data and theory points to an exponential (in parameter $E_J/E_C$) suppression of the threshold. We achieve a quantitative agreement with theory by accounting for charge-$e$ solitons associated with the quasiparticles entering the array from its leads. This scenario however underestimates the current below threshold in comparison with the experiment.

### Paper [134]
Title: Cluster Condensation and Symmetry-Protected Thermal Rigidity of Bose gases on the Haldane Sphere
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02799
Authors: Meng-Jie Wang, Jun-Hui Zheng
Abstract: We show that a spinless Bose gas on a Haldane sphere with a small monopole charge \(q\) realizes, in the lowest Landau level (LLL), an exactly solvable SU(2)-protected cluster condensate: a Hafnian condensate of two-boson singlets for \(q=1\), analogous to resonating-valence-bond singlet coverings, and a four-boson singlet condensate for \(q=3/2\). Unlike topologically protected quantum Hall states, the exactness of these states relies only on SU(2) symmetry and repulsiveness, with no pseudopotential fine-tuning. Their SU(2)-protected degeneracy renders the single-particle correlations exactly temperature-independent even as the clusters dissociate, a thermal rigidity absent in conventional Bose--Einstein condensates (BECs). Increasing the interaction drives a transition to a vortex BEC with spontaneously broken rotational symmetry.

### Paper [135]
Title: Observing oscillations using the non-magnetic and magnetic spin Hall effect in a non-collinear antiferromagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02804
Authors: Monalisa Cavalcante, Jos\'e Laurentino, Carlos Eduardo, Kevin Mesel, Jos\'e Holanda
Abstract: In this study, it was observed that, in non-collinear IrMn3, both the non-magnetic spin Hall effect (NMSHE) and the magnetic spin Hall effect (MSHE) exhibit oscillations as the triangularly ordered magnetic moments of IrMn3 evolve with temperature. These oscillations are strongly influenced by the magnetic properties at the interface between IrMn3 and the adjacent material. Permalloy (Py), a ferromagnet, and platinum (Pt), a paramagnet, were employed as adjacent layers. Such experimental analyses demonstrate the pioneering nature of this phenomenon, which is validated through magnetoresistance measurements performed under different experimental conditions.

### Paper [136]
Title: The maximum packing fraction of disordered bidisperse discs: theory and predictions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02805
Authors: Raphael Blumenfeld
Abstract: Predicting the highest packing fraction that disordered packing of bidisperse discs can achieve, $\phi_{max}$, is an important unresolved problem. Such a predictive theory is hindered by the density dependence on the packing protocol and by ensuring disorder. In bidisperse packings, this is more complicated because $\phi_{max}$ is not a number but a function of the disc species size ratio, $D$, and one of the disc species concentration, $p$. These difficulties have been overcome in monodisperse disc packings by using the cell order distribution (COD)~\cite{Bl21}. Focusing on size ratios that prohibit rattlers, a COD-based theory is constructed here. It introduces bidisperse statistical closure equations, which combine an explicit disorder criterion with consistent cell and edge statistics, and yields $\phi_{max}(p,D)$ and structural predictions. The a-priori disorder-guaranteeing criterion proposed here ensures fast exponential a decay of the occurrence probability of ordered clusters with size. Using a constrained maximum-entropy analysis to ensure maximum disorder, the occurrence probabilities of all the possible cell and edge configurations are derived in terms of $p$ and $D$ and they are found to be binomial. All the results are assembled to yield an exact prediction of $\phi_{max}(p,D)$.

### Paper [137]
Title: Magnetic order in Co[Cr(CN)$_6$]$_{2/3}\cdot z$H$_2$O and its interplay with correlated vacancy order
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02820
Authors: Rebecca E. Witte, Elodie A. Harbourne, John Cattermull, Tristan N. Dolling, Simon J. Cassidy, Fabio Orlandi, Pascal Manuel, Andrew L. Goodwin
Abstract: Prussian blue analogues (PBAs) are versatile hybrid framework materials that often exhibit local and long-range departures from their cubic parent structure, influencing their functionality. Long-range magnetic order is common in PBAs, owing to magnetic interactions between transition metal ions via bridging cyanide ligands in the structure. Here we report the magnetic structure of cobalt (II) hexacyanochromate, Co[Cr(CN)$_6$]$_{2/3}\cdot z$H$_2$O, determined using neutron powder diffraction. No evidence for magnetostriction at the magnetic ordering temperature is observed in contrast to the reported behaviour of a related PBA. Following experimental characterisation of the magnetic order of this PBA, we employ a simple Monte Carlo model to gain a better understanding of the relationship between hexacyanometallate vacancy distributions and magnetic ordering behaviour in these materials. This model shows that magnetic ordering is robust to the extent and nature of vacancy correlations, with only minor effects noticeable in the critical region, suggesting that PBAs with the same composition but different vacancy correlations might be expected to show similar magnetic behaviour.

### Paper [138]
Title: Superinsulation, electric confinement in condensed matter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02841
Authors: M. Cristina Diamantini, Carlo A. Trugenberger
Abstract: Superinsulation is the confining phase of condensed matter systems. It is a genuine quantum state of matter, dual to superconductivity, just as confinement in particle physics is dual to the Higgs phase. In contrast to hadron physics, the gauge field that becomes confining is not colour SU(3) but the conventional U(1) electromagnetic field. Superinsulation arises in superconductors with an emergent granular condensate when core-less XY vortices between condensate grains become tensionless, unobservable Dirac strings and magnetic monopoles are liberated and condense. This magnetic condensate confines electric fields into flux tubes, strings that generate a linear potential between Cooper pairs. These dual Abrikosov flux tubes give rise to infinite electrical resistance, persisting up to a finite deconfinement temperature. We shall review the theoretical framework of superinsulation together with its experimental validation, including the direct observation of the linear confining potential and we shall stress how phase-only approaches and disorder-based ideas for the phases around the superconductor-to-insulator transition are hard to reconcile with recent experimental observations.

### Paper [139]
Title: Zero-field plasmon splitter: Controlled interaction between quantum anomalous Hall edge states
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02879
Authors: Dominik Hoborka, Torsten R\"oper, Fabian Hassler, Alexey A. Taskin, Yoichi Ando, Erwann Bocquillon
Abstract: The quantum anomalous Hall (QAH) effect provides chiral edge states without the need for an external magnetic field, but unlike in conventional quantum Hall systems, their trajectories cannot be readily controlled electrostatically. Here, we engineer Coulomb interactions between two spatially separated, counter-propagating QAH edge channels using a floating metallic island. Microwave measurements reveal frequency-dependent redistribution of injected edge plasmons between two output channels, demonstrating interaction-induced hybridization of the collective modes. A long-range interaction model quantitatively reproduces the response and reveals a very low plasmon velocity in the coupling region, strongly reduced by electrostatic screening. Our results establish engineered Coulomb coupling as a route to control collective excitations in QAH edge states and provide a building block for chiral plasmonic circuits and electron quantum optics in magnetic topological insulators.

### Paper [140]
Title: Intrinsic Zak Phase of Solitons: A Geometric Correspondence with the Center-of-Mass Position
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02918
Authors: Xiao-Xiao Hu, Chen Cheng, Hong-Gang Luo
Abstract: The center-of-mass position of a nonlinear soliton in a lattice is commonly attributed to the interplay between nonlinearity and lattice confinement. Here, we reveal its geometric origin by establishing an exact relation between the soliton position and an intrinsic Zak phase. Unlike the Zak phase of a linear Bloch band, this intrinsic Zak phase arises from the nonlinear self-consistent band of the lattice reconstructed by the soliton density. We construct a composite periodic function to define this phase and demonstrate that its topological winding during nonlinear Thouless pumping dictates the quantized soliton displacement. Our results establish band geometry as a fundamental descriptor of nonlinear soliton position in a lattice and provide a geometric framework for their topological transport.

### Paper [141]
Title: Molecular Curvature Enables Tubular {\pi}-Stacked Pathways in Three-Dimensional Conductive Metal-Organic Frameworks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02945
Authors: Weishan Li, Jia-Xiang Zhang, Tongyang Zhao, Hao Chen, Tianyang Chen, Ze-Fan Yao, Jin-Hu Dou
Abstract: Conductive metal-organic frameworks (MOFs) are commonly constructed from planar aromatic ligands, whose geometries favor conventional {\pi}-stacking arrangements. However, intrinsically twisted ligands offer an alternative geometric approach to directing aromatic packing and constructing unconventional framework architectures. Here, we employ intrinsically twisted, butterfly-shaped dibenzo[g,p]chrysene-2,3,6,7,10,11,14,15-octaol (DBC) ligands to construct a series of lanthanide MOFs. Single-crystal X-ray diffraction reveals an interpenetrated three-dimensional framework in which {\pi}-{\pi} interactions between the aromatic fragments generate axially extended tubular {\pi}-stacked pathways, alongside one-dimensional channels rich in hydrogen-bonding sites. Lanthanide substitution preserves the overall tubular framework topology while modulating aromatic stacking distances and local pore environments. PrDBC single crystals exhibit mixed electron-proton transport, with electron and proton conductivities reaching 1.62 x 10-3 and 4.37 x 10-4 S cm-1, respectively, at 338 K and 30% relative humidity. This work highlights molecular curvature as a structural design strategy for constructing three-dimensional conductive MOFs that integrate tubular aromatic stacks with tunable pore environments for mixed electron-proton transport.

### Paper [142]
Title: Frequency-domain spin-wave logic gate based on three-magnon confluence
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02946
Authors: Vladimir D. Bessonov, Stephanie R. Lake, Bikash Das Mohapatra, Jannis Bensmann, Georg Schmidt, Rudolf Bratschitsch, Sergej O. Demokritov, Vladislav E. Demidov
Abstract: Spin-wave-based information processing provides a promising platform for low-power nanoscale computing by exploiting coherent magnon dynamics and intrinsic nonlinear interactions. A key advantage of wave-based processing for efficient miniaturization is the ability to perform operations in the frequency domain without requiring separate propagation paths for the input and output signals. Here, we demonstrate a frequency-domain spin-wave logic gate based on resonant, non-degenerate three-magnon confluence in an yttrium iron garnet nanowaveguide. Under resonant conditions, two input spin waves at different frequencies efficiently generate an output spin wave at the sum frequency, which occurs only when both input signals are present, thereby directly implementing an AND logic operation. We further show that the output intensity scales linearly with each input intensity and is therefore proportional to their product, enabling analog multiplication of two signals. These results demonstrate nonlinear magnon confluence as a versatile mechanism for frequency-domain spin-wave logic and analog signal processing.

### Paper [143]
Title: Generative diffusion in long-range correlated landscapes: Speciation cascade and the foam structure of the score
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02955
Authors: Jerome Garnier-Brun, Giulio Biroli, Marc M\'ezard, Jean-Philippe Bouchaud
Abstract: While state of the art for image and video generation, the fundamental reasons for the effectiveness of generative diffusion remain unclear. In particular, the observed advantage of following an entire reverse process with respect to single-shot or autoregressive methods, and for what type of data it may hold, is not well understood. In this work, we aim to better understand the role of the reverse process by considering a complex yet analytically tractable data model, the Gibbs measure of a high-dimensional Gaussian random potential with long-range correlations. Focusing on the exact score, we show that the speciation threshold, in which a trajectory commits to one class of the data, becomes a speciation window, in which the reverse process progressively resolves the Parisi tree of pure states of the full replica-symmetry breaking measure, committing to ever finer branches down to a single pure state. Importantly, these commitments take place at noise levels where the noisy sample distinguishes the pure states only through an energy of order one, beyond the reach of standard replica saddle points. Constructing a Larkin argument suited to such $O(1)$ energy gaps, we describe the score as a foam, with slab-like cells separated by shock-like boundaries. In the variance-exploding process we consider, this window is followed by a shrinkage of the samples towards the data support, and finally by reconstruction within the selected pure state. The denoising error of the exact score follows these stages, dropping during the speciation window with a profile set by the Parisi function alone, and then within the selected state. We believe that the generative foam we describe, and in particular the boundaries between cells, could play an important role in determining where the errors of trained diffusion models redistribute the mass of the generated samples when the data are long-range correlated.

### Paper [144]
Title: Anion Engineering Enables Concurrent Optimization of Phonon and Carrier Transport in AMg$_2$X$_2$ Zintl Thermoelectrics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02961
Authors: Ning Wang, Jincheng Yue, Hui Zhang, Xingchen Shen, Wenling Ren, Shuyao Lin, Yanhui Liu, Yue-Wen Fang, Tian Cui
Abstract: The simultaneous suppression of lattice heat transport and enhancement of carrier transport is central to the development of high-performance thermoelectric materials. Here, we investigate the microscopic mechanisms governing phonon and carrier transport in CaAl$_2$Si$_2$-type AMg$_2$X$_2$ (A = Ca, Sr, Ba; X = P, As, Sb, Bi) Zintl compounds using first-principles calculations, machine-learning interatomic potentials, and Boltzmann transport theory. As X changes from P to Bi, the widening phonon band gap restricts three-phonon scattering channels and increases the relative importance of four-phonon processes. In CaMg$_2$Bi$_2$ and BaMg$_2$Bi$_2$, four-phonon scattering dominates the relaxation of low-frequency heat-carrying modes at 900 K. The Bi-based series also exhibits an anomalous increase in lattice thermal conductivity from Ca to Ba despite the increasing cation mass. Mode-resolved analysis links the lower thermal conductivity of CaMg$_2$Bi$_2$ to reduced low-frequency group velocities and stronger anharmonic scattering, with Ca-related mid-frequency vibrations providing additional four-phonon scattering channels. Including both particle-like and coherent contributions, its lattice thermal conductivity reaches 0.23 W$\cdot$m$^{-1}$K$^{-1}$ at 900 K. Meanwhile, the increasing covalent character of the vertical Mg-X bonds is accompanied by enhanced band-edge dispersion and reduced carrier effective masses. These electronic changes, together with conduction-band convergence, favor n-type transport and yield a predicted peak $ZT$ of 3.68 at 900 K in CaMg$_2$Bi$_2$. These results elucidate the interplay of phonon spectral structure and higher-order anharmonicity in thermal transport, alongside the distinct electronic response to anion substitution.

### Paper [145]
Title: Hidden variable $\mathbb{Z}_2$ topological order in a bilayer kagom\'e antiferromagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03073
Authors: Hao Tian, Pengwei Zhao, Gang v. Chen
Abstract: Topological orders in realistic systems are quite rare, raising one fundamental question of whether they might be harbored with hidden degrees of freedom rather than directly exposed in conventional spins or electrons. We here propose a set of hidden variables and their effective interactions in a bilayer kagom\'e antiferromagnet. By properly organizing four local states of the spin dimer into two pseudospin-$1/2$ variables ($\boldsymbol{\tau}$ and $\boldsymbol{\mu}$), we identify a regime where an effective Balents-Fisher-Girvin model emerges, realizing a $\mathbb{Z}_2$ topological order in the hidden $\boldsymbol{\tau}$ sector while the auxiliary $\boldsymbol{\mu}$ sector remains a trivial paramagnet. This hidden nature drastically alters the experimental manifestations. Physical spins map to composite objects, typically convolving fractionalized anyon continua with non-topological magnon excitations. Nevertheless, specific symmetry-selected channels, such as the longitudinal intralayer response, exclusively reveal the bare two-vison continuum without collective-mode dressing. Our findings demonstrate that the topological order could be buried in certain hidden and emergent degrees of freedom, pointing to a promising pathway for seeking elusive topological states disguised within hidden internal degrees of freedom.

### Paper [146]
Title: The normal approximation to macroscopic dynamical fluctuations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03102
Authors: Christian Maes, Kate\v{r}ina Mlad\'a, Karel Neto\v{c}n\'{y}
Abstract: Under local detailed balance, the Lagrangian of macroscopic dynamical fluctuations has a canonical structure: its time-antisymmetric part is the entropy flux, and its time-symmetric part is a Legendre pair of kinetic and dissipation functions. We show what survives of that structure for normal (Gaussian) fluctuations around a nonequilibrium steady state. The quadratic Lagrangian keeps the canonical form, with the Hessian of the dissipation function as noise matrix. However, the force on the fluctuations differs from the linearized thermodynamic force by a term proportional to the driving and to the state dependence of the dynamical activity, so that local detailed balance is violated at the Gaussian level. Nevertheless, the Gaussian dynamics is gradient flow for the quadratic part of the nonequilibrium entropy, around an emergent nondissipative current. Finally, we show which parts of the Lagrangian can be reconstructed from measured Gaussian fluctuations.

### Paper [147]
Title: Ultrafast Polar Topology Reversal via THz-Driven Inertial Switching
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03108
Authors: Denan Li, Shi Liu
Abstract: Topological polar structures in ferroelectric heterostructures offer functional states beyond ordinary domains. As polarization reversal underpins ferroelectric devices, switching between degenerate topological variants, which we refer to as topology reversal, would greatly expand the ways to control polar textures. However, polar topology reversal is challenging because it requires coordinated flipping of oppositely oriented local polarizations, a process not naturally driven by a uniform electric field. Using a multiscale modeling approach, we show that polar topology can be stabilized and reversed in a simple Pt/PbTiO$_3$/Pt nanocapacitor. Electrode-controlled boundary conditions drive tetragonal PbTiO$_3$ into competing orthorhombic- and rhombohedral-like polarization states, which form building blocks for diverse topological structures, including vortex arrays, skyrmion lattices, dipole spirals, and dipole waves. We further show that the same out-of-plane THz pulse can reversibly switch a double-$\mathbf q$ dipole wave between degenerate topological variants within a few picoseconds, while preserving its spatial modulation. This process is enabled by damping-assisted inertial switching, in which the THz pulse injects energy to drive large-angle polarization rotation across shallow barriers, while dissipation removes excess energy and traps the system in the reversed state. These results establish ferroelectric nanocapacitors as a simple platform for programmable polar topology and low-power, THz-speed control through dissipative dynamics.

### Paper [148]
Title: Synchronization and Spatiotemporal Phases in Driven Precessing Domain Walls
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03110
Authors: Luz P\'erez Herrera, Enzo Fabrizio Pusiol, Alejandro B. Kolton
Abstract: The dynamics of extended magnetic domain walls driven by constant and alternating magnetic fields are studied using a collective coordinate $u$-$\phi$ model. While purely constant-field driven systems exhibit deterministic spatiotemporal chaos and roughening, the alternating component can lock the precessional frequency via Shapiro-like steps. Using linear stability analysis and Floquet theory, the phase boundaries of stable, synchronized flat wall states are mapped. By studying the nonlinear dynamics of the extended system, a rich phase diagram is identified, composed of five dynamical regimes characterized by the combination of domain wall spatial order (flat, rough, or periodic) and domain wall precession temporal order (pinned, synchronized, or unsynchronized). Notably, the macroscopic temporal order of the synchronized phases can lead to a flat domain wall or to a periodic structure dominated by a narrow band of linearly unstable modes, both with coherent precession of the domain wall magnetization, while unsynchronized phases can be flat, with coherent precession, or rough with spatiotemporal chaos and incoherent precession.

### Paper [149]
Title: Many-Body Correlations in Phase-Space Microscopes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03112
Authors: Y. Yang, C. Weitenberg, N. R. Cooper
Abstract: The recently proposed "phase-space microscope" (PSM) extends quantum gas microscopes by allowing joint, single-shot measurements of position and momentum. We show that the $n$-body correlation functions constructed from such phase-space snapshots determine, in principle, all $n$-body observables of a many-body quantum state. This establishes phase-space imaging as a complete probe of strongly correlated matter, beyond what snapshots of position and momentum can provide---a limitation that goes back to a famous question posed by Pauli. Even without the full reconstruction of the density matrices, the PSM provides valuable insight into the quantum states. We demonstrate this power through several applications: witnessing entanglement hidden from both position and momentum, including between spatially separated collective degrees of freedom; resolving pairing structure across the BEC-BCS crossover, including the phase of the pair wavefunction and the reconstruction of its two-body Wigner function; probing inhomogeneous systems and preformed pairs without long-range order, relevant to the pseudogap phase; extracting the interaction energy in a magnetic field; and measurements of the local Chern marker. Furthermore, we discuss phase imprinting in the matter-wave Fourier plane as an alternative method for probing time-reversal-symmetry-broken phases such as chiral $p$-wave superconductors. Together, these results establish the PSM as a versatile new tool for exploring fundamental features of quantum many-body phases in strongly correlated quantum matter.

### Paper [150]
Title: Exact fermionic matrix elements from the Bethe-ansatz solution of the Richardson-Gaudin model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03141
Authors: Peter D. Johannsen, Valerii K. Kozin, Jelena Klinovaja, Daniel Loss
Abstract: We introduce an effective Hamiltonian description of mesoscopic superconducting systems based on the Bethe-ansatz solution of the Richardson--Gaudin model. Our central result is a compact determinant formula for the matrix elements of fermionic creation and annihilation operators, expressed entirely in terms of eigenvalue-based variables. This formula allows single-electron tunneling terms to be represented directly in the eigenbasis of the Richardson--Gaudin model, extending the eigenvalue-based framework beyond overlaps and previously known form factors. As a benchmark, we apply the method to a superconducting Anderson impurity model comprising a quantum dot coupled to an ultrasmall superconducting grain. The resulting low-energy description, formulated naturally in the canonical ensemble, captures even-odd effects, subgap excitations, and quantum-dot physics. The same framework gives access to observables not previously computed for such grain-dot systems in the canonical ensemble, such as the fixed-particle-number optical conductivity and flux-tunable spectra.

### Paper [151]
Title: Characterisation of surface layers formed on plasma-facing components in controlled fusion devices: Role of heavy ion elastic recoil detection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03160
Authors: Petter Str\"om, Per Petersson, Marek Rubel, Armin Weckmann, Sebastijan Brezinsek, Arkadi Kreter, S\"oren M\"oller, Krzysztof Rozniatowski
Abstract: Wall components retrieved from the TEXTOR tokamak after tracer experiments with nitrogen-15 and molybdenum hexafluoride (MoF6) injection were studied to determine deposition patterns and, by this, to conclude on material migration. Toroidal limiter tiles made of carbon fibre composites and fine grain graphite were examined using time-of-flight heavy ion elastic recoil detection analysis. Molybdenum deposition patterns indicated migration based on erosion and prompt re-deposition. Nitrogen-15 was trapped together with the deposited molybdenum. Some information on the depth distribution of species in the top 400 nm layer of the limiters was obtained; however surface roughness of the samples strongly limited resolution. In the case of molybdenum, the largest concentration was found in the 100 nm outermost layer, whereas fluorine and nitrogen-15 displayed more irregular profiles. Other species, besides deuterium fuel and carbon-12, were also identified: boron-10 and boron-11 originating from boronisations, carbon-13 from earlier tracer experiments, nitrogen-14 from plasma edge cooling and metals eroded from the Inconel wall.

### Paper [152]
Title: Enforced Gaplessness by Combining Non-Anomalous Symmetries
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03164
Authors: Takamasa Ando, Kantaro Ohmori
Abstract: Symmetry-enforced gaplessness is a phenomenon in which a quantum system is forced to be gapless due to its global symmetry. Previous works have mainly focused on anomalous symmetries. Another approach, using symmetry spans, which specify two embeddings of a common symmetry into two larger symmetries, was proposed by the authors. In this work, we show that a symmetry span can enforce gaplessness even when both larger symmetries are invertible and non-anomalous: each alone admits a trivially gapped phase, but because the embeddings differ by a twist associated with a symmetry-protected topological phase, the symmetry they generate admits no gapped phase with finitely many ground states. We present such a span of a finite group and $\mathrm{U}(1)^2$ in 1+1 dimensions, with continuum realizations and a lattice Hamiltonian, with a finite-dimensional local Hilbert space and finitely many parameters, that is gapless in any region of its parameter space. We also discuss a 2+1-dimensional example and spans of only finite symmetries.

### Paper [153]
Title: A Topology-Based Approach to Representative Volume Element Assessment in Solid Oxide Cell Anodes Using Large Volume Tomography and Persistent Homology
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03176
Authors: Aleksandra Kukie{\l}ka, Maksym Szemer, Szymon Buchaniec, Katarzyna Berent, Grzegorz Brus
Abstract: Determining the representative volume element is essential for establishing statistically meaningful microstructure-property relationships. Conventional approaches rely on the convergence of selected geometric descriptors, which may yield different estimates of the representative volume. Here, we propose a topology-based framework for assessing the representative scale of a nickel-yttria-stabilized zirconia solid oxide cell anode reconstructed using plasma focused ion beam-scanning electron microscopy (121.8 um x 83.97 um x 45.99 um). Persistent homology was used to characterize the pore network, and normalized persistence images of subvolumes were compared with those of the largest available reference volume using the L1 distance. This provides a continuous measure of topological similarity across observation scales, rather than defining representativeness through a single convergence threshold. The topological distance decreases with increasing volume and is described by empirical exponential models over the investigated range (R2 in [0.972, 0.983]). Comparison with porosity and specific surface area highlights differences in the scale-dependent behavior of geometric and topological descriptors. The framework was also tested on three independent synthetic realizations, demonstrating its potential for quantitative assessment of topological similarity in porous microstructures.

### Paper [154]
Title: Pressure-correlation phase diagram unraveling quantum phase transitions in altermagnetic $\epsilon$-FeOOH and realization of piezo-anomalous Hall effect
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03180
Authors: Arijit Mandal, Nayana Devaraj, Birabar Ranjit Kumar Nanda
Abstract: We establish a pressure-correlation phase diagram that yields multiple pressure-tunable quantum phase transitions in iron oxyhydroxide ($\epsilon$-FeOOH) across three critical pressures, $P_{c1}>P_{c2}>P_{c3}$. The transitions arise from the competition among electron-correlation, crystal-field, and Hund's coupling. First-principles calculations reveal that the earlier reported high-spin (HS) to low-spin (LS) crossover at correlation-sensitive $P_{c1}$ is accompanied by a transition from charge-transfer to Mott insulating (CTI-MI) phase, and a change in the spin-quantization axis (SQA) from $\hat{y}$ to $\hat{x}$. The HS-LS crossover and CTI-MI transition is driven by the simultaneous increase in crystal-field splitting and decrease in Hund's coupling. The critical pressure $P_{c2}$ governing the structural transition from centrosymmetric $Pnnm$ phase to noncentrosymmetric $P2_1nm$ phase is found to be independent of electron-correlation. When correlation is strong, a third critical pressure ($P_{c3}$) emerges in the lower-pressure regime of the phase diagram. Validating the recent experimental observation, $P_{c3}$ is responsible for changing the SQA from $\hat{z}$ to $\hat{y}$. By exhibiting altermagnetism in both $P2_1nm$ and $Pnnm$ phases and possessing finite anomalous Hall conductivity (AHC) only for SQA along $\hat{x}$ and $\hat{y}$, $\epsilon$-FeOOH enables a unique piezo-anomalous Hall effect where one can switch on the AHC at a lower pressure and change its direction at a higher pressure.

### Paper [155]
Title: Density and Current Fluctuations for Run-and-Tumble Particles With and Without Interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03187
Authors: L\'eo Touzo, Gregory Schehr, Pierre Le Doussal
Abstract: We study density and current fluctuations in a one-dimensional gas of run-and-tumble particles (RTPs), whose intrinsic velocities switch between two opposite values at a constant rate. We consider particles with and without pair interactions, including regularized Riesz gases, and also obtain corresponding results for Brownian particles. By linearizing the Dean-Kawasaki (DK) equations around a homogeneous stationary state, we compute density correlations and the variance of the integrated current through the origin. For repulsive Riesz interactions decaying as $1/|x|^s$ with $s>0$, this variance grows at long times as $t^{1/2}$ for $s>1$, but as $t^{s/(1+s)}$ for $0<s><1$. Starting from the same DK description, but retaining the Poisson statistics of direction reversals, we derive a macroscopic fluctuation theory (MFT) for RTPs with smooth pair interaction potential, which allows to compute the large time large deviation of the time integrated current. We show that, in the absence of interactions, the MFT saddle point equations can be solved explicitly using a generalized Cole-Hopf transform. This allows to recover and generalize previous results for the current distribution obtained by microscopic calculations, for RTPs with annealed or quenched step initial conditions. Beyond these distributions, MFT allows to compute the optimal density histories producing a prescribed current through the origin.
