Title: Ideal interaction dynamics of defects and background fields in condensed matter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32973
Authors: Fran\c{c}ois Gay-Balmaz, Tudor S. Ratiu, Cesare Tronci
Abstract: While defect dynamics in condensed matter systems is widely studied and several methods are available to track the defect position over time, the formulation of dynamical models for the coupled evolution of both defects and background field quantities seems to be more challenging. Yet, the overall behavior of a system containing moving defects is strongly influenced by this defect-field interaction. Here, we propose a modeling framework for the conservative coupled evolution of the defect density and the background fields upon focusing on two relevant cases of study: the Gross-Pitaevskii theory of quantum fluids and the Ericksen-Leslie model of nematic liquid crystals. To achieve the coupled evolution, we combine the Euler-Poincar\'e variational setting of continuum dynamics with the gauge theory of topological defects. While the latter allows us to overcome certain ambiguities in definition of the defect density, the Euler-Poincar\'e method provides a unified modeling approach in the absence of defect reconnection. Suitable variational regularizations are also proposed to deal with the motion of singular defect lines.

### Paper [365]
Title: Defining 2-group symmetries on lattices and extracting their anomalies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37745
Authors: Yitao Feng, Yu-An Chen
Abstract: Ordinary $0$-form symmetries $G$ can mix with $1$-form symmetries $\Pi_2$ to form a $2$-group $\mathcal{G}$. We give a concrete lattice definition for a class of finite $2$-group symmetries in terms of local relations among symmetry operators. These relations identify the Postnikov class $[\rho]\in H^3(BG,\Pi_2)$ that characterizes the mixing. We develop a method to extract anomalies of these symmetries in one spatial dimension by restricting symmetry operations to an interval and comparing their composition near its endpoints. We also construct explicit qubit symmetry actions in $d=1,2,3$ spatial dimensions for a $2$-group with $G=\Pi_2=\mathbb{Z}_2$. These actions share the same nontrivial Postnikov class but include both anomaly-free and anomalous realizations. Their cohomological anomalies are classified by $H^{d+2}(B\mathcal G,U(1))$, which is $\mathbb{Z}_2$ for $d=1,2$ and $\mathbb{Z}_2\times\mathbb{Z}_2$ for $d=3$, and our constructions realize every class in these dimensions. Our results connect higher-group symmetry and its anomalies to concrete lattice operations, providing a microscopic framework for studying anomalous symmetries.

### Paper [366]
Title: Exact holographic superfluid at the Bogomolny point: transport, large charge, and R\'enyi entropies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40371
Authors: Siqing Yu
Abstract: The Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$, in the probe limit, is a standard holographic model of a strongly coupled superfluid, usually solved numerically. When the scalar mass saturates the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound and the quartic coupling takes the Bogomolny value, its condensed phase can be found in closed form on every static, uncharged AdS$_5$ background with maximally symmetric spatial sections. We prove that it is linearly stable, as is any nodeless probe-limit condensate in the standard quantization with a nonnegative quartic coupling and no superflow. On each slicing it answers a different question about the dual conformal field theory. On the planar black brane it is the superfluid at all temperatures below $T_c$: the superfluid fraction follows the Gorter-Casimir two-fluid law exactly, and the speed and the attenuation of sound are found in closed form. In global AdS$_5$ it describes a state of charge $Q$ on $\mathbb{R}\times S^3$, arguably the lowest-energy one, and gives its scaling dimension in closed form at all $Q$, classically in the bulk. Its excitations include towers that the superfluid effective theory does not describe; they come from an infrared fixed point of the probe sector. The hyperbolic slicing gives in closed form a homogeneous hairy phase of the black holes that compute the charged R\'enyi entropies of a ball. Its condensate does not vanish at the entangling surface. When sourced by operators inserted there, the phase sets in at R\'enyi index exactly one at zero chemical potential, as found for a neutral scalar.
